| T27 | `fillBillingLinks` unscoped full-history read | MED | ✅ Done | `MonthlyBiller.fillBillingLinks` reads all billing history into memory. The outer query `SELECT bm FROM BillingMonth bm ...` has no current-month filter, so it loads every historical `BillingMonth` and, for each, `getResultList()`s that month's entire `BillingGrid` table plus a per-row `COUNT(BillingLink)` (N+1). It does not rewrite historical billing — the `if (linkCount == 0)` guard means it only inserts missing links (and for history, none are missing, so nothing is written) — but it needlessly reads and materializes all historical grid rows every run, which (with EclipseLink's default-on L2 cache) accumulates memory and grows every month the app runs. Affects both the legacy and launcher paths (shared method). Fix: scope the outer query to the current month (`BillingHelper.getMonthFor()`) and eliminate the N+1 `COUNT`. Low-risk, helps both paths, defuses a growing memory/time liability. (Surfaced during the Monthly Billing Launcher OOM incident; the launcher-side L2 eviction is the immediate mitigation, this is the root-cause cleanup.) De-N+1 follow-up shipped in commit `7c1e64e` (per-month link pre-load replacing the per-row COUNT). |
| T28 | Add "Summit TPA GUID" field to the Initialize Application wizard | LOW | 💡 Backlog | Mirror how `SUMMIT_PATH` is handled: read `request.getParameter("summitTpaGuid")` (or similar) in the init flow and persist via the guarded `createConstant(em, "SUMMIT_TPA_GUID", ...)` pattern in `DatabaseInitializer.addPspConstants()`, so each new PSP sets its own per-install GUID instead of inheriting SSA's literal. Deferred until a second PSP is onboarded — `addPspConstants()` intentionally does not seed `SUMMIT_TPA_GUID` today, to avoid baking SSA's GUID into every new PSP init. Companion one-time backfill for already-initialized environments: `docs/deployment_backlog.md` D-77. |
| T29 | SECURITY: remove/gate `/CreateBpoTestUser` | HIGH | ✅ Done 2026-09-08 (S36) — removed, not gated; runtime-unverified, not yet deployed | Unauthenticated account-creation endpoint: a plain unauthenticated `GET` created two `User` rows — `bpoadmin@test.com` (UserRole 102, BPO Admin) and `bpouser@test.com` (UserRole 103, BPO User) — and printed the shared hard-coded credential in cleartext in the HTTP response. **Path corrected 2026-09-08:** the real path is `src/main/java/net/superiorstate/ams/controller/authentication/CreateBpoTestUser.java`; this row previously carried the abbreviated `CreateBpoTestUser.java:149-155` and FINDING 1 elided it as `controller/.../`, so neither located the file on its own. See `docs/analysis/security_findings_2026-07-28.md` FINDING 1. **Closed by REMOVAL, not by gating:** a repo-wide search across code, JSPs, docs, scripts and configuration found **zero callers and zero documented operational dependencies** — every reference was an inventory entry or the finding itself — so the decision rule's REMOVE limb fired without discretion. The `@WebServlet` annotation inside the deleted class was the **sole** declaration of the `/CreateBpoTestUser` mapping (`web.xml` declares no servlet mappings at all), so deleting the file removed endpoint and mapping together and no second file needed touching. ⚠️ **Code-verified only:** source gone, sole mapping declaration gone with it, `BUILD SUCCESS`, and the class confirmed absent from both `target/classes` and the freshly built WAR. **Runtime-verified: nothing** — no request was issued to the endpoint before or after the change, so the expected 404 is an inference from the mapping's deletion, not an observation. ⚠️ **Not deployed:** `superiorstate.biz` still runs the previously deployed WAR, so the endpoint remains open in production until a build carrying this ships. Follow-ups filed: **T220** (the now-inert `LoginFilter` allowlist entry) and **T221** (the accounts this endpoint may already have created — removing the creator does not revoke them). |

| T39 | `HealthSherpaService` needs multi-applicant support | MED | 💡 Backlog | An **extension**, not a rewrite — `quoteSingleApplicant` and its existing retry/parsing behavior stay as-is; this adds a second method alongside it. Three features need it: **A2** (provider check) — the quote request accepts an optional request-level `providers` array of NPIs, and the response returns per-plan `covered` status; this is the endpoint that makes A2 viable at all. **A3** (design census) — real household composition (multiple ages/relationships in one call) instead of one synthetic applicant. **The rate-cache warm job** (`RateCacheWarmService`) — could collapse its canary call into the primary call rather than making a separate one, if the response exposed a per-applicant premium breakdown the current single-applicant method doesn't need. Response shape: `gross_premium_per_applicant.non_dependents`, keyed by `relationship` (`primary` / `spouse`) — caps two adult ages per call. Request shape: `applicants[]` array, each entry `age`, `relationship`, `smoker`. Not scoped or sized. Logged 2026-07-30. |
| T40 | `MonthlyBiller.getOrCreateBillingMonth` calls `java.sql.Date.toInstant()`, which throws unconditionally | MED | ✅ Done | `java.sql.Date.toInstant()` throws `UnsupportedOperationException` unconditionally — documented JDK behavior, since `java.sql.Date` carries no time component. `getOrCreateBillingMonth(Date fullDate)` (`data/service/MonthlyBiller.java:704-705`) calls it twice, both inside the `NoResultException` branch that creates a new `BillingMonth` when none exists for the target date — i.e. the no-existing-billing-month path, not the get path. Call chain (corrected — three live entry points reach this line, not the one originally logged): `/CreateBilling25` → `MonthlyBiller.run()` → `fillBillingGrid(int)` → `getOrCreateBillingMonth(Date)` → `.toInstant()`; also `BillingPipelineRunner.java:245` (launcher worker, step 11/14) and `CreateBillingDiag.java:154`. Observed once in a production stack trace, 2025-10-15 14:32:09, across eleven months of continuous `catalina.out` history (2025-09-10–2026-07-31) — rare because the branch is rarely reached, but deterministic once reached: every call into it fails. **Fixed** with `fullDate.toLocalDate()`, this codebase's dominant idiom for this exact conversion (77 occurrences across 20+ files, already used on this field at `BillingMonth.java:60`) and non-throwing for a date-only value; the `ZoneId`/`Instant` round-trip was never meaningful since the source value has no time component. The `catch (NoResultException e)` branch itself was kept as-is — it is intentional self-repair (reconstructing a missing `BillingMonth`), not a bug to remove. The race that lets this branch be reached at all — concurrent deletion of the `BillingMonth` row mid-run — is a separate, unfixed defect, logged as T49. Logged 2026-07-31; fixed 2026-07-31. |
| T41 | `fields` request parameter to trim quote response payload | LOW | 💡 Backlog | `POST /api/v1/quotes` accepts a `fields` parameter restricting which plan attributes are returned; `name`, `year`, `hios_id` and `gross_premium` are always included regardless. The current response carries a large per-plan object — nested `issuer`, `networks[]`, `cost_sharing`, `cost_sharing_tiers`, `benefits`, `benefits_pretty`, `urls`, `providers[]`, `drugs[]` — of which `RateCacheWarmService` reads only `hios_id`, `metal_level`, `gross_premium` and `issuer.name`. Across a multi-county, multi-plan-year warm run now paginating at `per_page: 100`, the saving is material. Not adopted because getting the field list wrong breaks parsing silently — the same failure class as the pagination defect. Needs a verified field list before adoption. Logged 2026-07-31. |
| T42 | `include_non_enrollable_offex` semantics unverified | LOW | 💡 Backlog | HealthSherpa documents `include_non_enrollable_offex: true` as causing non-enrollable off-exchange plans to be included in results. Observed behaviour does not match the obvious reading: all 23 UnitedHealthcare plans — the carrier recorded as quotable-but-not-API-enrollable in this county — were returned without passing the flag. So either "non-enrollable" means neither `api_enrollment` nor `deeplink_enrollment` (UHC presumably having the latter), or the default already includes them. Matters because it determines which plans legitimately belong in a market range shown to an employer. Resolve by comparing plan sets with and without the flag, and cross-checking `api_enrollment` / `deeplink_enrollment` per plan. Logged 2026-07-31. |

D-79 rewritten in the same change to require the constant on every environment, production included, with no inherited value. | T44 | LCSP and benchmark silver computed from off-exchange plans only | MED | ✅ Done | **Closed 2026-08-01 (S7-B), by trace — it was already fixed and the row never said so.** A genuine on-exchange source exists and has since **`ba023bd` (2026-07-31, "ICHRA item 8 (T44): on-exchange LCSP and benchmark silver")**. V078 added nullable `onex_lcsp_premium` / `onex_benchmark_silver_premium`; `RateCacheWarmService:316-317` issues a **second** base-age quote with `off_ex=false` (`HealthSherpaService.buildRequestJson:208` puts it on the wire), and `:325-328` derives LCSP = lowest silver and benchmark = second-lowest silver from that response. `IllustrationServlet:530` reads `getOnexLcspPremium()` — the **onex** column, never `lcsp_premium` — so the affordability table has been fed by the on-exchange rail since that commit. ⚠️ **The original note below is retained and is still accurate about `lcsp_premium`/`benchmark_silver_premium`**, which keep their off-exchange meaning and still feed the RANGE table; what changed is that affordability no longer uses them. **Runtime-confirmed on production** by Kevin's 2026-08-01 walk (Hopkins 48223, PY2026): the column rendered real figures, which it can only do if the post-V078 re-warm actually ran — V078 does not backfill, and an unwarmed county renders "On-exchange rates not cached for this county". ⚠️ **`docs/ichra_strategy.md` is now stale on this in two places — §3 row 3 (line 84, "Cached LCSP is off-exchange only (T44)") and §9 step 4 (line 267, "T44 — on-exchange LCSP … 2 calls")** — both still describe this as unbuilt. **Not edited here; flagged for Kevin.** Original note follows. — `RateCacheWarmService` quotes with `off_ex: true`, so `lcsp_premium` and `benchmark_silver_premium` in `rating_area_rate_cache` derive from off-exchange silver plans only. For ICHRA affordability the applicable LCSP is the lowest-cost silver plan offered on the Exchange for the rating area; the APTC benchmark (second-lowest silver) is on-exchange by definition. The two sets differ in the reference county — 45 plans on-exchange against 65 off-exchange. The dangerous direction is an off-exchange-only silver plan undercutting the true on-exchange LCSP, producing an LCSP that is too low and making an ICHRA offer appear affordable when it is not. Two halves: the empirical one is two staging calls differing only in `off_ex` (blocked 2026-07-31 — no key configured anywhere, see D-78/D-79); the compliance one is whether an indicative illustration figure is held to the same standard as an affordability determination, and belongs with O18 and O25. Logged 2026-07-31. |
| T45 | `ProposalBuilder` has no role gate | HIGH | 📋 Planned | `ProposalBuilder.doGet` reads `isPspAdmin`, `isAgent`, and `isAgencyAdmin` purely as data-loading branch selectors, never as an authorization gate. Its final `else` assigns an empty prospect list and the method forwards unconditionally — there is no `sendError`, no `sendRedirect`, and no early return anywhere in `doGet`. Any authenticated session (Client, Applicant, BPO user) reaches the page and gets an empty render. This is the same hole Phase 2 closed in `AgentHome`, whose `isAuthorized` Javadoc documents the fix; it was never applied here. Remedy: the `AgentHome.isAuthorized` five-flag + `sendError(403)` idiom. Security finding, not cosmetic debt. Logged 2026-07-30. |
| T46 | `isPspSales` is absent from the navbar Sales dropdown condition | MED | 💡 Backlog | `navbar25.jsp` line 186 gates the Sales dropdown on `isAgent \|\| isAgencyAdmin \|\| isPspAdmin`. `isPspSales` is missing, despite being in `AgentHome.isAuthorized`'s five-flag set and in `AgencyScopeResolver`'s pspWide bucket. | T47 | Verify representative-ZIP county assignment against HealthSherpa for low-containment counties | MED | 💡 Backlog | `county_reference` (V076) picks each county's representative ZIP as the ZCTA with the greatest share of its land area inside that county. 225 of 254 Texas counties scored a containment of exactly 1.0. The remaining 29 sit between 0.6301 (Reagan County, ZIP 76932) and 1.0 — the ZCTA crosses a county line, though it remains majority-inside its assigned county. Containment measures land area, not postal assignment. What matters operationally is whether HealthSherpa's own ZIP-to-county mapping agrees with ours for those 29. This could not be tested when V076 was written because no installation has ever authenticated to HealthSherpa (D-78/D-79 have never been applied anywhere). Benign failure: HealthSherpa rejects the ZIP/FIPS pair, the warm job logs and skips, the county has no cached rates, and the illustration page honestly reports none. Bad failure: HealthSherpa silently returns the ZIP's actual county's rates under our requested FIPS, and wrong rates are cached as authoritative. Remedy once a credential exists: quote each sub-1.0 county and confirm the returned market matches the intended county. Blocked on D-78/D-79. |
| T48 | `source_env` is advisory provenance, not a partition or a safety mechanism | LOW | 💡 Backlog — **amended 2026-08-03 (S12-A/S12-B), not closed** | `rating_area_rate_cache.source_env` records `STAGING` or `PRODUCTION` per row, and V074's header describes it as recording which environment a row was fetched against. That is true per row but weaker than it reads: `RateCacheDAO.replaceCountyRates` deletes by `(planYear, countyFips)` only, and the unique key `uq_rarc_year_county_age_tobacco` excludes `source_env` — so staging and production rows for the same county silently overwrite one another. ~~Nothing in `src/` ever compares, filters, or branches on the value.~~ ~~`IllustrationServlet` neither filters nor displays it, so an agent-facing illustration carries no indication of which environment produced its numbers.~~ **⚠️ Both struck clauses are false as of S12-A (2026-08-03) and were false the same day T48 was filed.** Using `git log -S` (read-only history): T48 was filed in `42caf4e`. Two **later commits the same day** — `b0e524b` ("ICHRA item 6", adding `ProposalBuilder.attachRangeSnapshot`/`attachAgeBandSnapshot`'s write-time gate and `ViewProposal.java:138`'s read-time gate) and `0b4711b` ("gated front door... AGE_BAND illustration mode", adding `IllustrationServlet.setProvenanceAttributes` and `illustration25.jsp`'s red banner/meta-line/hand-off gate) — both postdate it. **T48 was accurate the instant it was written and was invalidated by same-day work that never circled back to correct it** — a different failure than ordinary drift, worth naming as its own pattern. **Ten sites now branch on `source_env`**: `RateCacheWarmService` (writes it), `RateCacheAdmin.java:93` (displays current warm-config env), `RateCacheDAO.check` (S11-G, the shared availability gate), `ViewProposal.java:792` (`putIchraMarketTokens`, T130), `ViewProposal.java:138` (gates whether `ichraSnapshot` renders on a customer-facing proposal at all), `ProposalBuilder.java:598`/`:658` (refuse to **write** a snapshot from non-production rows), and `IllustrationServlet.java:816-833`/`GroupConversionServlet.java:523-539` (`setProvenanceAttributes`, driving the red banner + meta-line + disabled hand-off button on both agent tools). Full trace: S12-A (`docs/session_s12a` — read-only, no file; see S12-B's close-out for the citation) and `docs/session_s12b_closeout.md`. **⭐ One clause of T48 survives and is the reason this item stays open, not closed:** the Illustration's own **premium computation is still ungated** — `handleRangeMode`/`handleAgeBandMode` compute and render figures from whatever rows exist regardless of `source_env`; only the *label* (banner, meta-line) and the *downstream hand-off* (button disabled, snapshot write refused) are gated, confirmed independently sufficient to prevent a customer-facing leak (S12-A), but the figures shown to the agent themselves carry no gate of their own — by design, since gating them would make an agent-facing tool unable to show test data at all. Consequence, restated accurately: `source_env` is not a partition (still true — same-county STAGING/PRODUCTION rows still silently overwrite each other, per the unique-key gap above, untouched by S12-A/B) and remains advisory at the **cache-row level** — but it is no longer accurate to call it advisory **system-wide**: three independent write/read gates and two display surfaces now treat it as load-bearing. **S12-B additionally closed the one dropdown-labeling gap that was genuinely fixable** (`IllustrationServlet.pricedCountyFips`) and named the one that is not (`GroupConversionServlet.loadAvailableCounties` — a true filter, not a label; making it provenance-aware today would empty the `/GroupConversion` dropdown, since every warmed county is currently staging-sourced). Remedy, if wanted: still include `source_env` in the delete scope or the unique key (unchanged from the original text — not addressed by S12-A/B), and separately, decide whether `/GroupConversion`'s dropdown should eventually distinguish staging-warmed counties once more than one environment is realistically in play. Not urgent while only one environment is ever configured at a time. |
| T49 | No mutual exclusion between the legacy billing servlets and the launcher | MED | 💡 Backlog | `/CreateBilling25` and `/ClearBilling25` are unguarded servlets with no locking between them. The Monthly Billing Launcher has an `isRunActive` 409 guard at `LaunchMonthlyBilling.java:62-64`, but it covers launcher-to-launcher concurrency only — a hand-driven `/ClearBilling25` during an in-flight launcher run is not blocked, and neither is a `/ClearBilling25` during a `/CreateBilling25` run. `ClearBilling25.java:75` deletes the `BillingMonth` row. A create run reads that row at step 2 and again at step 11; a clear landing between them drops step 11 into the `getOrCreateBillingMonth` exception branch. That is what produced the 2025-10-15 production abort logged as T40. Before T27, `/CreateBilling25` took roughly 101 minutes, so a run that looked hung invited exactly this reflex. Fixing T40 lets the run survive that race by reconstructing the row. It does not fix the race — a concurrent clear can still delete rows a create run is mid-way through building, producing a half-built month that looks complete. Possible remedies: extend the launcher's `isRunActive` guard to cover the legacy servlets; a DB-level advisory lock; or retire the legacy servlets once the launcher is production-deployed. Priority rises when the launcher reaches production, since `CLEAR_BILLING` followed by `CREATE_BILLING` then runs on every launch. Logged 2026-07-31. |
| T50 | Tarrant County 48439's representative ZIP is unusable (DFW Airport) | MED | 💡 Backlog | Distinct from T47 — T47 is about ZCTA *containment* crossing county lines; this is a containment-**1.0** ZIP that is simply unusable. V076's representative-ZIP selection picked **75261 — DFW Airport** for Tarrant, a ZCTA with almost no residential population. Observed symptom: Tarrant skips during cache warm while Bexar 48029, Dallas 48113, Harris 48201 and Hopkins 48223 all warmed successfully (44 age rows each, 2026-07-31). Root cause: a gap in V076's representative-ZIP selection rule — it optimises for containment and has no population floor. Severity: benign today (only four counties warmed), rises with the county list (D-83). Cross-reference T47 as related-but-distinct. Logged 2026-07-31. |
| T51 | `ProposalSettings.updateScope` lets an ICHRA section be reverted to `scope='ALL'` | HIGH | 📋 Planned | Creation is blocked; reversion is not. `updateScope` was widened to admit the `ICHRA_ILLUSTRATION` section type and reuses `CUSTOM`'s shared "show on all proposals" radio, so an admin can flip a correctly LOS-scoped ICHRA section back to `scope='ALL'` through the shared UI after creation. Consequence: a `scope='ALL'` section then renders on **every** proposal for that PSP, for every line of service, on the public customer-facing proposal link — a direct rule-2 breach (S5, `docs/swbd_ichra_build_plan.md` §4). Both `ProposalSettings`'s scope handler and its JSP card are hard-gated to `CUSTOM`, which is why widening was needed and why the reversion path opened. Severity: the downside is a public leak; the fix is small. Logged 2026-07-31. |
| T52 | Matched chatbot skills silently ignore their configured `model` and `max_tokens` | MED | ✅ Done | `chatbot_skill.model` and `chatbot_skill.max_tokens` (V046) are not consumed on the text-only skill path. `ChatAssistant.executeSkill` routes a text-only skill through `ClaudeApiService.ask(EntityManager, String, String)`, and that 3-arg overload hardcodes `DEFAULT_MODEL` (`claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`) and `DEFAULT_MAX_TOKENS` (1024) into the request body — it never reads the skill's values. The file-upload branch is unaffected: it calls `askWithContent(systemPrompt, contentBlocks, skill.getModel(), skill.getMaxTokens())`, which does honour them. So a text-only skill configured for Sonnet with 4096 tokens actually runs on Haiku with 1024, and the admin UI shows a setting that has no effect. `EMAIL_DRAFT_ASSISTANT` is NOT affected — it is fetched by name via `ChatbotSkillDAO.getBySkillName` from `EmailAssistantDraft` and does not go through `executeSkill`. Affects any keyword-matched text-only skill, including `ICHRA_DESIGN_ADVISOR` (V080), whose system prompt was written to ask for short answers because of this. Fix: add a model/max-tokens-aware overload for the text-only path (`ClaudeApiService.ask(String, List, String, int)` already exists and could be reused with a single-message list) and pass `skill.getModel()`/`skill.getMaxTokens()` from `executeSkill`. ⚠️ Note this changes the effective model for every existing keyword-matched skill, so it needs a check of what is currently seeded per environment before shipping. Surfaced 2026-07-31 while building V080; deliberately not fixed there to keep that migration data-only. Logged 2026-07-31. **⚠️ Exposure changed 2026-07-31 (V082 + T58): this is no longer PSP-admin-only in effect.** `ICHRA_DESIGN_ADVISOR` is now `is_admin_only = 0` and the chat assistant admits ICHRA-entitled agency users, so **agents** — not just SSA staff — now reach an advisor running on a model and token budget nobody chose, on a citation-bearing, compliance-adjacent surface. Priority is unchanged only because the fix still needs its own per-environment survey of seeded keyword-matched skills before it can ship; it is no longer an internal-only defect. **Fixed 2026-07-31 (`4ea160d555c7aac84e0610b7aac1d49ebc3d57ab`), in `ChatAssistant.executeSkill`'s text-only branch only.** No change to `ClaudeApiService` was needed — `ask(String, List, String, int)` already existed and builds a byte-identical request body to the 3-arg `ask(EntityManager, String, String)` for a single-message list (same system/messages shape, headers, timeout, `extractResponseText`, error strings); only `model` and `max_tokens` differ. **Defensive by design:** the configured values are used only when the model is non-null/non-blank **and** max_tokens > 0; otherwise the historical `ask(null, systemPrompt, question)` call runs verbatim, so a skill that configured nothing is byte-for-byte unchanged. Values pass through unvalidated on purpose — no allow-list, no correction, no warning — per the fix spec. **The deferred blast-radius survey was done from the migration files, not a database.** Only two migrations insert into `chatbot_skill`: V065 seeds `EMAIL_DRAFT_ASSISTANT` (`claude-sonnet-4-20250514`, 4096) and V080 seeds `ICHRA_DESIGN_ADVISOR` (`claude-sonnet-4-20250514`, 2048). **Only `ICHRA_DESIGN_ADVISOR`'s effective behaviour changes** (Haiku/1024 → Sonnet/2048). `EMAIL_DRAFT_ASSISTANT` is unaffected for two independent reasons: V065 never populates `trigger_keywords` at all, so `getTriggerKeywordSet()` returns empty, `scoreKeywordMatch` returns 0, and it can never clear `findMatchingSkill`'s `>= 2` threshold — and its real caller, `EmailDraftService` (`:200,206`), already passes model/maxTokens explicitly and never routes through `executeSkill`. ⚠️ **What this survey cannot cover:** rows created or edited through the Skill Manager admin UI after seeding are invisible to migration files, so any skill configured at runtime will also start honouring its setting from this commit forward. That is precisely why the fix is written defensively rather than unconditionally — an admin who set a value gets it, an admin who left the seeded defaults alone sees no change, and no skill's behaviour moves by accident. |
| T53 | Chatbot skills have an unguarded path around their own guardrails | MED | 💡 Backlog | Architectural property of the V046/V063 mechanism, not of any one skill. Two facts combine badly. **(1)** `ChatAssistant.executeSkill` injects **no** knowledge-base content — for a text-only skill it calls `ClaudeApiService.ask(null, skill.getSystemPrompt(), question)`, so the `system_prompt` is the model's entire context; chunks are retrieved only on the *other* path, `executeKBSearch`. **(2)** Skill selection needs **≥2 distinct `trigger_keywords`** matched by substring (`ChatbotSkillDAO.findMatchingSkill` / `scoreKeywordMatch`); a message scoring 0–1 never matches, falls through to KB search, and is answered from chunks **with no skill boundaries applied at all**. Consequence: any skill whose guardrails live in its `system_prompt` while its content lives in the KB has an unguarded path straight around its own guardrails — and the fallback can surface a chunk whose safe reading depended on a boundary that was never loaded. Concretely, for `ICHRA_DESIGN_ADVISOR` a phrasing like "how long before the plan year do I have to tell employees?" named neither arrangement, scored 0, and could surface the LA-07 QSEHRA notice-runway chunk as though it answered an ICHRA question — which LA-08 explicitly forbids. **Current exposure: none.** That skill is `is_admin_only = 1` and its `ichra_design` KB sits outside non-admin eligibility (`KnowledgeSearchService.getEligibleKBs` gives non-admins only `summit_official`/`summit_supplemental`), so neither path is reachable by an agent today. **Exposure begins the moment any such skill is opened to non-admins** — which is the stated purpose of the ICHRA advisor (build-plan item 12). **Mitigated for that one skill in V080** by duplicating the refusal boundaries into chunks and scope-fencing the LA-07 chunk body; **not mitigated mechanism-wide** — the next skill anyone writes inherits the same trap. Possible remedies: have `executeSkill` also retrieve and inject chunks; or apply a matched skill's `system_prompt` as a preamble on the fallback path too; or a KB-level "always-load boundary chunk" convention (`reload_strategy = ALWAYS_LOAD` already exists and `getAlwaysLoadChunks` is already implemented, but `executeKBSearch` does not call it). Surfaced 2026-07-31 while reviewing V080. Logged 2026-07-31. **⚠️ Premise correction, 2026-07-31 (V082):** the "Current exposure: none" verdict above rested on **two** guards, and V082 removed one — the skill is now `is_admin_only = 0`. The verdict itself still holds, because the second guard is independently sufficient: `getEligibleKBs(false)` returns only `summit_official`/`summit_supplemental`, so a non-admin's fallback KB search retrieves **no** `ichra_design` chunks and the trap cannot fire. But it is now **single-guarded**. Concretely: flipping the `ichra_design` KB to non-admin visibility — the obvious-looking move if someone later decides citations or coverage need improving — is now the one change that would open this exposure for real, where before V082 it would still have been blocked by the skill flag. **Verified 2026-07-31 that no such flip is needed:** citations on the matched-skill path come from 14 literal `Source:` lines inline in the `system_prompt`, not from retrieval (`ChatAssistant.java:194` sends `system_prompt` + question only), so the KB is not on the answer path for a matched skill. See `docs/analysis/ichra_demo_path_role_walk.md` §4. Corollary worth knowing: V080's §3b boundary chunks, written to guard the fallback path, are unreachable for non-admins and now protect PSP admins only. |
| T54 | Multi-word and hyphenated `knowledge_chunk` keywords are silently dead — existing KBs unsurveyed | MED | 📋 Planned | `KnowledgeSearchService.scoreChunk` scores a chunk keyword with `queryWords.contains(kw)` — **exact token equality** against the tokenized query, not substring. `tokenize` lowercases, strips everything outside `[a-z0-9\s]`, and splits on whitespace, so a stored keyword containing a **space or a hyphen can never match any token** and contributes nothing, silently. It costs the chunk its `WEIGHT_KEYWORD` (5 per hit) — by far the heaviest signal, against 3 for a title word and 1 for a content word — so an affected chunk is retrievable only by weaker title/content matches and can be crowded out of the `MAX_RESULTS = 8` window by less relevant chunks. There is no warning, no validation, and nothing in the Knowledge Manager UI that would reveal it. ⚠️ Note the trap: the sibling mechanism is the *opposite*. `ChatbotSkillDAO.scoreKeywordMatch` uses `lower.contains(kw)` — substring on the raw message — where multi-word keywords work fine. The two matchers read almost identically and behave differently, which is how this got past authoring. **Five dead keywords were found and fixed in the V080 LA-07/QSEHRA-runway chunk alone** (`45 days`, `90 days`, `short first year`, `eligibility date`, `la-07`), and the rest of V080 was authored single-word once the behaviour was understood. **The existing V063 KBs have NOT been surveyed** — `summit_official` and `summit_supplemental` are reachable by **non-admin users today** (`getEligibleKBs(false)` returns exactly those two), so if their chunks carry multi-word keywords a live, user-facing feature is running degraded right now and nobody would see it. `style_voice`, `federal_rules` and `ssa_business` should be checked too, though V063 seeded them schema-only. **Next step is a read-only survey, not a build:** `SELECT kb_id, chunk_id, title, keywords FROM knowledge_chunk WHERE keywords REGEXP '[ -]'` — count affected rows per KB, then decide whether to split the offending keywords into single words or teach `scoreChunk` to also test phrase containment against the raw query (the latter changes retrieval for every existing KB and needs its own before/after check). Do not fix blind; measure first. Surfaced 2026-07-31 while building and reviewing V080. Logged 2026-07-31. |

| T58 | No external agency role can satisfy the chatbot entry-point gate at all | MED | ✅ Done | Established from the role model in code, not from any row. `AuthDAO.assignUserRoles` (`AuthDAO.java:92-125`) maps role 2 → `isAgent` and role 8 → `isAgencyAdmin`; **neither sets any of `isPspAdmin` / `isPspUser` / `isBpoAdmin` / `isBpoUser`**. This is a **prior blocker to T57**: T57 is about the skill being admin-only, but the entry point excludes agency roles before skill selection is ever reached, so both must change for an agency user to use the advisor. Fixing requires an edit to `navbar25.jsp` (or a new agency-facing entry point) and is deliberately **not** attempted as part of any ICHRA-owned change. Whether an agency user *should* see this content at all is a disclosure decision, not a gate fix — see `docs/analysis/ichra_demo_path_role_walk.md` Q1. Strictly a widening: every session that saw the widget before still does; ICHRA-entitled agency agents (role 2) and agency admins (role 8) gain it. `AgencyScopeResolver.resolve` admits both roles (lines 113-123), so Forrest reaches it on role 2 today and on role 8 later. | T59 | ICHRA hub's "Age-Band Net Cost" and "Affordability Threshold" cards point at the same URL | LOW | ✅ Closed — **and it was never a defect** | ⭐ **Re-closed 2026-08-01 (prompt K) on better reasoning than the fix that shipped.** T59 said affordability "has no URL of its own" and treated that as a gap. **It was not.** Affordability is a **section that appears when a basis is chosen**, not a step with an address — the defect existed only inside the three-step framing of the hub, and **the framing was the error, not the URL**. Kevin's observation dissolved it: *"steps 1, 2 and 3 are really 3 versions of the same thing — the only difference is level of detail available."* All three cards were always `/Illustration` with a different `mode`; the code had been saying so the whole time. The `&affordabilityBasis=FPL` deep-link shipped in `e849dac` is still correct and still useful as an entry point — it just is not the resolution of a defect. See `ichra_flow_and_handoffs.md` §4's decision block. Prior close-out follows. **Closed 2026-08-01 (`e849dac`), session 6.** The framing below was half wrong on the key point: `affordabilityBasis` has been a first-class request parameter since item 9 (`IllustrationServlet.java:238`) and `illustration25.jsp:160-162` already pre-selects the dropdown from it — a distinct URL always existed, nothing linked to it. Fixed by pointing the second card at `Illustration?mode=AGE_BAND&affordabilityBasis=FPL`; FPL rather than INCOME because the entered-income basis requires an income on *every* census row and rejects the submit otherwise, so a card landing on INCOME would error on first click. Also re-graded from LOW's "no user-visible breakage": it left build-plan §1 **step 5** — the flip point the demo turns on — with no entry point of any kind. Original note follows. Both cards in `ichraHome25.jsp` link to `Illustration?mode=AGE_BAND` — identical `href`, two distinct titles and descriptions. `IllustrationServlet` treats affordability as a sub-mode computed within `AGE_BAND` handling rather than as a separately addressable mode, so there is no distinct URL to point the second card at today. Either the affordability card should carry a parameter that pre-selects the affordability basis, or the two should be merged into one card — but which is right is a product question about whether affordability is its own surface or a facet of the age-band view. Role-independent; noticed while walking the demo path, not caused by it. No user-visible breakage: both cards land on a working page. Surfaced 2026-07-31 during the demo-path role walk. Logged 2026-07-31. |
| T60 | `ichraNavVisible` session cache has no invalidation | MED | 💡 Backlog | `IchraAccessResolver.isAvailableForNav` caches its answer under session attribute `ichraNavVisible` for the session's lifetime once computed. Flipping `agency.ichra_enabled` for an agency mid-session leaves any already-logged-in member of that agency stale — the nav entry and chatbot widget stay hidden until they log out and back in — and there is no admin path to clear the cached value early. Workaround: log out, log in. Logged 2026-08-01. **Effect observed, not just reasoned about, 2026-08-01:** the T61 confirm-before test required a freshly-logged-in agent to see the result — the entitled agent's session had never cached `ichraNavVisible`. Consistent with T60's known effect; a same-session before/after re-check was not run, so this is corroboration, not a new test of T60 itself. Still open. |
| T61 | `agency.ichra_enabled` has no admin UI write path | MED | ✅ Done | V077 added the column with `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` and no backfill by design, but no JSP or servlet ever sets it — `Agency.setIchraEnabled()` has zero callers anywhere in the codebase. Entitlement is direct SQL only today (`UPDATE agency SET ichra_enabled = 1 WHERE ...`). V067's `markup_enabled` precedent, which item 2 of `docs/swbd_ichra_build_plan.md` said to "copy exactly," did ship a checkbox (`agencyManager25.jsp` + `AgencyAction.editAgency`) — the DDL was copied but the UI was not. Estimated ~6 lines mirroring `markupEnabled`, no migration needed. Logged 2026-08-01. **⚠️ Priority raise recommended 2026-08-01 (session 5): this may be a correctness issue, not just a convenience gap.** If the EclipseLink shared-cache hypothesis recorded in `docs/session_closeout_2026-08-01_session5.md` holds, a raw-SQL flag flip does not take effect until the application restarts, because `em.find(Agency.class, id)` keeps serving a cached row. An admin checkbox writing through JPA would evict the cache correctly and make entitlement take effect immediately. Untested — nobody has flipped the flag on a running instance and re-checked without a restart. Confirm that first; if it holds, this moves from MED/📋 Planned to HIGH. **Fixed 2026-08-01** — mirrored V067's `markup_enabled` UI exactly, which is what build-plan item 2's "copy V067 exactly" always implied: a plain checkbox (`name="ichraEnabled"`, "Enable ICHRA", with helper copy) directly beneath the markup checkbox in `agencyManager25.jsp`'s edit-agency modal, and `agency.setIchraEnabled("on".equals(request.getParameter("ichraEnabled")))` immediately after the `setMarkupEnabled` line in `AgencyAction`'s `editAgency` case. No migration — V077's column already existed and `Agency` already mapped it with both accessors. No entity change, no cache-eviction code. **Edit path only, mirroring markup exactly:** `createAgency` does not set `markupEnabled` either, so a newly created agency is entitled by saving it and then editing it — deliberately not widened beyond the precedent. **The EclipseLink shared-cache question above remains untested, and is now testable through the UI:** toggling the checkbox writes through JPA (`em.find` → setter → transaction commit), which updates the shared cache, where the raw-SQL `UPDATE` bypassed it entirely. If an agency entitled via this checkbox shows ICHRA without an application restart while one entitled by SQL does not, that confirms the hypothesis. **T60 stays open regardless** — `ichraNavVisible` is cached per session, so an already-logged-in agent must still re-login to see the change. **Verified 2026-08-01 on `v0.83.01`:** a second agency was entitled through this checkbox on the running production instance — no restart, no redeploy — and a freshly-logged-in agent of that agency saw ICHRA immediately. The UI path works without a restart; T61 is closed in practice, not merely in code. This is consistent with the shared-cache hypothesis, not a disproof of it — see the session-5 close-out addendum and **T64**. |
| T62 | Design Advisor citations render as an imperative instruction, not an attribution | LOW | 💡 Backlog | Observed in the first live role-2 runtime check (2026-08-01): a substantively correct answer closed with **"Cite domain_and_compliance_rules.md section 5"** — imperative phrasing that reads as the `system_prompt`'s instruction leaking into the output rather than a clean attribution ("Source: …"). **Root cause is verified, not inferred: the string is reproduced verbatim from V080's own few-shot example answers.** `V080__ichra_design_advisor_skill.sql` contains four `Cite <doc>.md section <n>` occurrences, and they sit inside the `A:` exemplar answers (e.g. `Cite domain_and_compliance_rules.md section 5`, `Cite ichra_strategy.md section 7`) — not only in boundary 6's instruction ("6. CITE EVERY SUBSTANTIVE ANSWER"). The model is imitating its examples correctly; the examples are written in the imperative. The citation **mechanism works** — the right document and the right section were named, which is the substance of build-plan item 12's requirement — so this is presentation polish, not a correctness defect, and is deliberately not urgent. Surfaced during the T61 build, 2026-08-01, when the render gate was checked before adding the ICHRA entitlement checkbox; T61 was allowed to proceed because the entitlement *write* is 403-gated, so the new control neither created nor widened this gap. | T64 | Raw SQL updates to JPA-mapped config tables may be invisible until a restart | MED | 📋 Planned | Suspected during the 2026-08-01 ICHRA entitlement investigation and never tested. EclipseLink's shared cache is not updated by an `UPDATE` issued outside JPA, so a flag flipped by hand may continue reading stale for the life of the deployment. Scope is not ICHRA — it applies to any hand-edited config row anywhere in AMS, and to every operational runbook that says "run this UPDATE." **The 2026-08-01 T61 confirm-before test did not resolve this** — it verified the new JPA-backed UI path works without a restart, which is consistent with the hypothesis but does not test the raw-SQL path at all (see `docs/session_closeout_2026-08-01_session5.md`'s addendum). Test: flip any JPA-mapped flag by SQL on a running instance and observe. Logged 2026-08-01. **⭐ CONFIRMED 2026-08-02 (S9-B) — the test was finally run, and the hypothesis holds.** On an isolated local Tomcat, `UPDATE agency SET ichra_enabled=1 WHERE agency_id=123355` was issued by raw SQL against a **running** instance, and a subsequent fetch of a public proposal page whose gate reads that exact flag through `em.find`/JPQL **behaved as though the flag were still 0** — byte-identical output, zero gated sections. **The identical request after a Tomcat restart, with no further SQL, rendered the gated content** (40,902 → 42,435 bytes). Same WAR, same DB row, same URL; **the only variable was the restart.** ⚠️ **Scope of the claim, stated precisely:** this establishes that a raw-SQL flip to a JPA-mapped flag was invisible to a running deployment **in this instance, for this entity**. It does not establish the cache as the mechanism to the exclusion of every other explanation, and it was not repeated across entities. But it converts T64 from "suspected, never tested" into **observed at least once**, which is what it needed. **Operational consequence, and it is the point:** every runbook step of the form *"run this UPDATE"* against a JPA-mapped config table must be assumed **not to take effect until the application restarts** — including the ICHRA entitlement flips done by hand before T61's checkbox existed. Priority raise to HIGH is defensible now; left MED pending Kevin's call. **⭐ SECOND OBSERVATION 2026-08-02 (S9-D), and it widens the scope: it is not only scalar columns.** S9-B's observation was `agency.ichra_enabled`, a **scalar attribute**. S9-D hit the same staleness on a **`@ManyToMany` relationship collection**: `DELETE FROM agents … ; INSERT INTO agents …` (changing which agencies a person belongs to, i.e. `Person.listOfAgenciesWithThisAgent`) was issued by raw SQL against a running instance, and the next request rendered **byte-identical output** — same branding agency, same closed gate, 40,902 B — as though the membership had never changed. **After a Tomcat restart with no further SQL, the same URL resolved to the new agency and rendered 41,778 B.** ⚠️ **Consequence for runbooks: "restart after a hand-edited config row" is not limited to flag columns — it covers join-table membership edits too**, which is the shape most likely to be done by hand when wiring up an agency or an agent. Two independent entity shapes now observed; the mechanism is still inferred rather than proven, but the operational rule does not depend on the mechanism. — ⭐ **THIRD OBSERVATION 2026-08-05 (S18-D/S18-E), a third entity shape.** During S18-D's production verification of `enhancement.system_managed` (V089), a raw `UPDATE enhancement SET system_managed = 1 WHERE enhancement_id = 1` against the **running** instance did not change `/proposal/{guid}`'s rendered section list — section 5 stayed present. **A Tomcat restart, with no further SQL, made the section disappear**; resetting the column to `0` and restarting again brought it back. Same shape as S9-B/S9-D: scalar `@Column` on a JPA-mapped entity, invisible to a running deployment until restart. **Recorded as a third observation, not a resolution** — this row remains open. Detail: `docs/session_closeout_2026-08-05_session18.md`. |
| T65 | ICHRA affordability constants are seeded nowhere — step 5 of the demo may not render at all | **HIGH** | ✅ Done | **Fully closed 2026-08-01 (S7-B) — the production rows are in.** Established by arithmetic from Kevin's production walk (`v0.85.07`, Hopkins 48223, PY2026, FPL basis), not by a DB read: every affordability row showed flip = LCSP − **$132.47**, and `$15,960 ÷ 12 × 0.0996 = $132.47` to the cent. That figure is reproducible **only** if `FPL_ANNUAL_2026 = 15960` and `ICHRA_AFFORDABILITY_PCT_2026 = 0.0996` both resolve — and the servlet fails closed with a named-constant message rather than defaulting (`IllustrationServlet:508-524`), so a rendered table is itself proof both rows exist. Also confirms the percentage is stored as the decimal fraction `0.0996`, not `9.96` (which would drive every flip negative, clamp to zero, and report every offer affordable at any contribution). ⚠️ **Manual rows on production — no seed path reaches an initialized database**, so this is not self-healing: a **new plan year needs two new rows by hand**, and the failure mode is silent until the moment of a demo. LA-14 governs the FPL-year choice. Original note follows. — **Code side closed 2026-08-01 (`e90515a`); production side still open.** `DatabaseInitializer.addIchraAffordabilityConstants` now seeds `ICHRA_AFFORDABILITY_PCT_2026 = 0.0996` and `FPL_ANNUAL_2026 = 15960`, idempotently. ⚠️ **That seed does not reach production** — `addPspConstants` runs only from `initializeDataBase`, the one-time key-gated fresh-install path, and nothing re-runs it on an initialized database. **The two production rows are still a manual deployment item.** The percentage is a decimal fraction (`0.0996`, not `9.96`) — `AffordabilityCalculator` multiplies it straight into monthly income, and `9.96` would drive every flip contribution negative, clamp to zero, and report every offer affordable at any contribution. FPL year disambiguated in LA-14. The unconfigured message now names the missing constant. Original note follows. — `IllustrationServlet.computeAffordability` reads two `constant` rows: `ICHRA_AFFORDABILITY_PCT_<planYear>` (always) and `FPL_ANNUAL_<planYear>` (FPL basis). A repo-wide grep finds **exactly one file mentioning either name — the reader itself** (`IllustrationServlet.java:373,381`). No migration, no seed script, no `DatabaseInitializer` path creates them. The servlet fails closed by design (`:374-386`) — never a default, never a prior year's value — so an unconfigured installation renders "Affordability is not configured for plan year N" and no table. That is build-plan §1 **step 5**, the flip point the entire SWBD pitch turns on. **Config, not code** — two `constant` rows per plan year, Kevin's to add, no migration required. Priority HIGH because it is silent until the moment of the demo and everything upstream of it works. Settled by step 10 of the click-script in `docs/analysis/ichra_flow_and_handoffs.md` §3. Surfaced 2026-08-01 session 6 while mapping the flow; **not verified against production** — only that nothing in the repo seeds them. Logged 2026-08-01. |

| T74 | Illustration takes a county, not a ZIP — the agent has the ZIP | **HIGH** | ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-04 (S14-E)** — `95a81b155db429df5b65df26f07d8e72cc9ef182`. **Runtime-verified 2026-08-04 (S14-F)** — production walk post-`v0.88.05`: ZIP `75482` entered on `/GroupConversion` resolved to Hopkins County, TX, dropdown updated to display it. | **Part 2 shipped 2026-08-01** — `IllustrationServlet` (`5b70586`) resolves `?zip=` through `ZipCountyResolver`, and `illustration25.jsp` (`a41a481`) gained the ZIP box, the crossing-ZIP chooser and the no-match message. **`?zip=` is consulted only when `countyFips` is absent**, so every existing link is unaffected; the JSP diff is **75 insertions, 0 deletions**. Three states kept distinct: not-in-crosswalk (new message), resolves-but-unwarmed (`inputError`, T76's), and no-input-at-all (bare form, unchanged). ⚠️ **Still open: `/GroupConversion` remains on the county dropdown**, per §5's "only after the illustration path is proven" — a follow-on, not a regression. ⚠️ **Correction (S14-E, 2026-08-04): this caveat was already stale when written.** `docs/analysis/migration_tracker.md:206-219` superseded it the same day (2026-08-01, end of session) with a behavioral confirmation: V084/V085 shipped in release `v0.85.00` and are applied on Production — ZIP `75482`→Hopkins, `75009`→Collin/Denton chooser, `90210`→correct Texas-only miss, none of which is reachable against an empty `zip_county` table. `docs/claude_memory.md:13` independently corroborates the same facts. The row was never updated after that correction landed, until now. Original note follows. — **Part 1 shipped 2026-08-01** — V084 `zip_county` (`4f242af`), V085 Texas data (`2689d67`), `ZipCounty` + `ZipCountyDAO` + `ZipCountyResolver` (`d7cbc4f`), generator script for reproducible regeneration. **Part 2 — `IllustrationServlet` + `illustration25.jsp` — is the next build**; contract is in `ichra_flow_and_handoffs.md` §5. Data: US Census 2020 ZCTA-county relationship file, public domain, fetched 2026-08-01; **2,894 rows, 1,992 ZCTAs, 254 counties (exact match with `county_reference`), 686 ZCTAs (34%) spanning more than one county**, 89 KB. ⚠️ **34% is the number that shapes the UI** — disambiguation is the common path, and nothing may auto-select; `getUnique()` returns null on an ambiguous resolution by design. ⚠️ **ZCTA ≠ ZIP:** PO-box-only and single-building ZIPs are absent, so a valid USPS ZIP can miss — the UI must say "we don't have that ZIP", never "invalid ZIP". HUD's crosswalk is better and was tried first (202/zero bytes on files, 401 on the API without a registered token). ⚠️ **Texas only** — every out-of-state ZIP misses until another `V0NN` data migration runs. Original note follows. — ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-01 (prompt D). The original sizing below was wrong.** It claimed *"AMS already does [carry the crosswalk]: `county_reference.representative_zip` (V076) is exactly that data, unwired to the UI"* and called the fix *"small: a ZIP box that resolves through `CountyReferenceDAO`"*. **Both are false.** V076 is PK `county_fips` with **one** `representative_zip` per county — county → ZIP, not ZIP → county — so reversing it matches only the 254 chosen ZIPs out of ~2,600 Texas ZCTAs. Verified 2026-08-01: **no ZIP → county crosswalk exists in the repo, the schema, or the API** (`CountyReferenceDAO` exposes only `findByFips`/`listByState`/`findByFipsIn`; `healthsherpa.md` 31 Jul records the API route as closed). **This is a data build with a UI on top** — a new many-to-many `zip → county_fips` table from the same Census ZCTA source V076 used, plus a migration. A ZIP may span counties: **on a crossing ZIP the agent picks; it must never silently resolve.** Full spec: `ichra_flow_and_handoffs.md` §5. Ranked **#1** by sale-motion impact — tier 1 intake is the motion's front door. Reversal cost: the table is inert reference data and the dropdown stays as fallback, so reverting is deleting the ZIP box. Original note follows. — `plus_tier_build_plan.md` A1's scope is "**ZIP → county + FIPS** → cached market rates", and **D6** makes ZIP a mandatory quote input. The built page offers a **county dropdown only** — no ZIP field exists anywhere in `illustration25.jsp` or `groupConversion25.jsp`, and the sole `zip` reference in `IllustrationServlet` is `logRow.setZipCode(county.getRepresentativeZip())` (`:651`), derived after the fact. An agent holds an employer's ZIP, not its county FIPS, and Texas has 254 counties. The API route is closed by decision (`healthsherpa.md` 2026-07-31: "the ZIP→FIPS route via this API is closed. **AMS must carry its own county reference data**") — but AMS already does: `county_reference.representative_zip` (V076) is exactly that data, unwired to the UI. Small: a ZIP box that resolves through `CountyReferenceDAO` and preselects the dropdown. ⚠️ `representative_zip` is one ZIP per county, so the reverse lookup is not total — T47 records the containment caveat. Logged 2026-08-01 prompt C. |

Ties directly to **T77** (multi-location design). Do **O38**'s Summit UI check first (Part 8 found division fields at rate and participant grain, indicating classes may be natively supportable with no AMS class model). Original note follows. — `ichra_platform_capability_map.md` Layer 1 lists four sales-and-modeling tools; three exist. **Class optimization** — "which class structure minimizes employer cost while keeping employees whole", named by HealthSherpa as an intended use case for these endpoints — has no surface. `plus_tier_build_plan.md` A3.3 carries it as the third design deliverable, and Part 3 §A1 explicitly defers it to A3. ⚠️ Related and favorable: **O38** (Part 8, 2026-07-30) found division fields at rate grain in J7 and participant grain in the PB notice report, indicating "**ICHRA classes may be natively supportable, with no AMS class model needed**" — confirmation is a Summit UI check. | T76 | Cache warm on miss — an unwarmed ZIP should warm, not fail | MED | 💡 Backlog | ⭐ **Seam built 2026-08-01 (`8285b83`): attach at `IllustrationServlet.describeUnavailableCounty`, the branch where `CountyReferenceDAO.findByFips` returns non-null.** That is the single place, across every entry path — ZIP resolver, chooser link, hand-typed URL, dropdown — that knows "a real county with no cached rates". T76 becomes "trigger a warm here", not a rewrite. **No stub, button or TODO was left there deliberately** — a disabled control implying a capability that does not exist is worse than its absence. ⚠️ **Two scope notes recorded 2026-08-01, neither a build item:** (1) warming from staging stamps `source_env = STAGING`, so warm-on-miss makes a new county *work* but not *demoable* — the red test-environment banner still fires and the proposal hand-off stays disabled; the allow-listing gate still governs. (2) It helps **only inside Texas** — an out-of-state ZIP does not resolve at all, which is crosswalk expansion (a separate `V0NN` data migration), not this. **[K 8/1]** Today a county with no cached rates reports "no rate data for this county yet" and the agent stops. It should **warm on demand** and continue. ⭐ **Matters well beyond SWBD** — every TPA warms from their own book, so the set of counties that matters is different per installation and cannot be pre-configured centrally. Today's mechanism is `RATE_CACHE_COUNTIES` plus a scheduled `RateCacheWarmService` run, which means someone has to know the county in advance; on-miss warming inverts that. ⚠️ **Blocked in practice:** no AMS installation has ever authenticated to the HealthSherpa API (`healthsherpa.md` 31 Jul — `HEALTHSHERPA_API_KEY` absent from both `constant` and `ssa.properties`, D-78/D-79 unapplied everywhere), so the trigger has nothing to call. **Ship the honest miss message first; add the warm trigger when a key exists.** Also needs a guard against an unbounded warm on a mistyped ZIP. Reversal cost: delete the trigger, the scheduled path is untouched. Logged 2026-08-01 prompt D. |

Any instruction to "dedupe by rating area before pricing" is unbuildable and must not be restated; two counties sharing a rating area price identically but hold separate cache rows, and that redundancy is correct-but-coarse rather than a bug. `ZipCountyResolver` (shipped `d7cbc4f`) already returns county FIPS, so the input side is ready. Notes below predate this check. **[K 8/1]** Every ICHRA surface today takes exactly **one** county. Real groups have separate sites, and locations map to employee classes. A design must hold a **set** of ZIPs and price each. ⚠️ **Dedupe by resolved `county_fips`, not by rating area** — verified 2026-08-01 that `rating_area_rate_cache` has **no `rating_area` column**; its unique key is `(plan_year, county_fips, age, uses_tobacco)`. So county-FIPS dedupe is what is available: correct, but coarser than rating-area dedupe, since two counties sharing a rating area price identically yet hold separate rows. | T84 | ⚠️ SILENT WRONG RATES — a stale county selection overrode a freshly typed ZIP | **CRITICAL** | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (`de0efe0`) | **R1, and the most consequential defect this project has shipped.** Found by a production walk 2026-08-01, not by any of the three code-verified runs that preceded it. Agent had Hopkins selected from a previous run, typed ZIP `75009` (Collin/Denton), pressed Enter — and got **Hopkins results, with no warning**. Wrong county → wrong rates → **indistinguishable from right ones**, in front of a client. It is the exact failure the crossing-ZIP chooser exists to prevent, arriving through a door nobody had modelled. ⚠️ **Root cause was a written instruction, not a coding slip:** prompt F specified *"the servlet consults `?zip=` only when `countyFips` is null or blank, so the existing parameter always wins"* — written to protect the `?countyFips=` URL contract, which it does, but "always wins" also means a stale dropdown beats a fresh ZIP. **Fix:** a present ZIP is always resolved, and a county the ZIP contradicts is never computed from — not with a warning, not at all. `Resolution.containsCounty()` added so agreement is askable without callers reimplementing it. **Lesson for the record: three consecutive code-verified runs missed this, and one runtime walk caught it in a minute.** Click-script step 4f is the regression test. Logged 2026-08-01. |
| T85 | ZIP resolved only on Enter, and Enter submits the whole form | MED | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (`de0efe0`, `6543db7`) | **R2.** Tab did nothing, blur did nothing; the only trigger was Enter, which submits — so typing a ZIP with the headcount still empty was answered with *"Enter a valid number of eligible employees (1-10000)"*, a validation error about a different field. Resolving a ZIP had become entangled with computing an illustration. **Fix:** new gated GET/JSON endpoint `IchraZipLookup`, and the field resolves on `change`/`blur` — one county fills the dropdown, several render the chooser in place, none shows the no-match panel. No reload, no validation, no submit. Server-side precedence is enforced independently, since JavaScript may be off and `?zip=` can arrive in a URL. Matters more than its severity suggests: **stage 3 is a presentation surface** — the agent runs this across a desk from an employer. Logged 2026-08-01. |
| T86 | "No rate data for this county yet" printed on a pure validation failure | MED | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (`6543db7`) | **R3.** Hopkins with a blank headcount rendered **both** the employee-count error **and** *"No rate data for this county yet"* — for a county that returns a full table one screenshot later. **Cause:** the mode handler sets `inputError` and returns **before** `hasRates` is set, so the JSP's `not hasRates` branch fired. ⚠️ **This collapsed the two states prompt F required kept apart** — the unwarmed-county message (T76's territory) firing for a validation error — in the direction nobody was watching. **Fix:** both result-panel guards now also require `empty inputError`; a validation error renders no result panel at all. Logged 2026-08-01. |
| T87 | A stale result panel survived the next interaction | LOW | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (`6543db7`) | **R4.** The `90210` no-match panel was still on screen while the agent typed `ABCDE` into the ZIP field. **Fix:** any edit to the ZIP immediately hides the panels and clears the county selection — before the lookup returns, not after — plus a stale-response guard so an answer for a ZIP the agent has moved past is discarded. | T90 | ⭐ The contribution slider read as static — **nobody noticed the demo's centrepiece** | **HIGH** | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (`ced1679`) | **W10, and the most consequential finding of the first full runtime walk — because it was not a defect.** The slider works perfectly; the agent who watched it get built looked straight past it: *"the slider was actually hard for me to even see it as a tool, I looked right past it to the data. I didn't get the impression that the page was anything other than a static result."* This control **is** build-plan §1 step 5 — *"at $419 Maria keeps her subsidy, at $420 she doesn't"* — and it is the one thing the page has that a competitor's quote engine does not. **Cause, visible in the markup:** it used `.status-card`, byte-identical to the read-only result panels above and below it. It looked like output because it was dressed as output. **Fix:** own tint + accent edge; filled track; 22px handle with grab cursor and focus/hover ring; and **flip-point ticks on the track**, which make the money moment visible *before* anything is dragged. Rejected: moving it — it already sits directly above the table it changes. ⚠️ **No-steering:** every tick is identical in colour and size, no region is shaded good or bad, nothing is marked recommended or optimal, and the track fill is a position indicator in the existing SSA blue, not a green/red scale. No auto-animation or "try me" tooltip — the page must not appear to do arithmetic by itself in front of a client. Ticks read `data-flip` from rows the server already computed and are `aria-hidden`, since the affordability table exposes the same figures properly. ⚠️ **Known imprecision:** ticks are positioned as a percentage of the track, so they sit a few pixels off the handle near the extremes (thumb width). Visual cue only — the exact figures are in the table. Logged 2026-08-01. |

| T99 | ⚠️ A blank age band was force-rendered beside a real one — silently wrong headcount | **HIGH** | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (`f651633`) | **K3-b, and the most consequential defect this surface has produced**, because nothing on screen explained it: one band entered, two rendered, and the headcount was the sum of both. **Cause was one clause.** The row-render test read `not empty submittedAges[i-1] or (i == 1 and mode == 'AGE_BAND')`, force-rendering a blank row 1 whenever mode was AGE_BAND. | T100 | Illustrate revealed inputs instead of computing; contribution was mandatory in band mode | **HIGH** | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (`77af9d4`) | **K3-a and K3-c are one defect.** Contribution was required in AGE_BAND, so a band with no contribution produced a validation error instead of a result — and because `mode` had already flipped to AGE_BAND, the contribution and basis fields appeared on that error render. **The button looked like it had revealed inputs rather than computed.** It had computed nothing and said so in a message easy to miss above two newly-appeared fields; a second click appeared to "hide" them. **Fix:** contribution is optional. Blank = not supplied → per-band premium table; present-but-malformed is still an error, because that is a typo not an absence. Without it: no net columns (absent, not blank — an empty currency cell reads as zero), no group net, no outlay, no slider card (absent rather than parked at $0, which would assert the employer contributes nothing). **Affordability degrades honestly**: flip points do not depend on the contribution, so they still render; only the verdict does, so that cell says *"Enter a contribution to see the verdict"*. `isAffordable` unchanged and simply not called; `flipContribution` and the clamp arithmetic untouched. Regression tests: **T2, T5, T9**. Logged 2026-08-01. |
| T101 | A stale result survived an edit — W1's family, for every input | MED | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (`d016fde`) | **K3-d.** W1 cleared the results panel when the ZIP changed; the same defect applied to every other input. Open **Edit**, change a contribution or an age, and the table below still showed the previous answer while the form described a different question. **The figures are correct for inputs the agent has moved on from** — precisely what made W1 dangerous rather than untidy. Any `input`/`change` inside the form now hides the results region. ⚠️ Scoped to the form deliberately: the slider lives in the results card, **outside** the form, so dragging it does not clear the results it is meant to update. Clicking Edit alone does not clear them either. Regression test: **T10**. Logged 2026-08-01. |
