It bears on two concrete things: **what an agent can promise in the room**, and whether he can answer *"did everyone get enrolled?"* afterward. `ichra_administration_scope.md` Phase 3 already names the adjacent risk — *"enrollment does not end on SSA's system... chasing non-effectuated members is a staffed operational task, and the most likely failure mode in the whole flow"* — but says nothing about who staffs it. Gated by **O12** (rep), **O13** (BAA), **O14**, none of which has moved since 2026-07-29. **Ask Forrest** (what does he expect, and what did zizzl do?) **and ask HealthSherpa.** ⚠️ **Do not design around either answer** — an assumed answer here silently sets the scope of the whole enrollment phase. Logged 2026-08-01 prompt D. |
| T104 | "for 1 eligible employees" — singular/plural not handled | LOW | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (S7-B) | Three footnotes in `illustration25.jsp` interpolated a count straight into a hardcoded plural: `:818` and `:833` (`${submittedTotalLives}`) and `:1285` (`${submittedHeadcount}`). A one-life illustration — the exact shape of a single-employee ICHRA quote, and the smallest group an agent is likely to walk in with — read *"For 1 eligible employees"*. Fixed with an inline `${... == 1 ? 'employee' : 'employees'}` at each of the three sites; **no computation, no attribute, no request parameter and no figure touched**. `:826` ("Contribution × total eligible employees") was deliberately left alone — it carries no number and is already correct. Logged 2026-08-01. |
| T107 | Is the ON-EXCHANGE LCSP column actually on-exchange? — **yes** | **HIGH** | ✅ Answered 2026-08-01 (S7-B); residue fixed 2026-08-01 (S7-E) | ⚠️ **Residue closed (S7-E):** the "real observation left standing" below — no like-for-like silver pair on screen — got one sentence added to the affordability section's existing disclaimer paragraph, naming the on-exchange LCSP as what the affordability test requires and noting the range table's silver figure is a different plan on a different rail, not comparable. Copy only — no column, no `lcsp_premium` rendered, no figure changed. Original S7-B note follows. — **Raised from a production walk (`v0.85.07`, Hopkins 48223, PY2026, FPL) that backed a single age-21 base of $551.93 out of two rows, against the same page's off-exchange second-lowest silver of $394.24 at age 21 — a 40% gap suspected to mean the column was mislabelled. It is not. The label is correct and no figure or label was changed.** The two numbers are **different statistics on different market rails** and were never comparable: the affordability column is `onex_lcsp_premium` = **lowest** silver from an `off_ex=false` (**on-exchange**) call (`RateCacheWarmService:325-326`); the range-table row is `benchmark_silver_premium` = **second-lowest** silver from the `off_ex=true` (**off-exchange**) call (`:369-370`), and its own label already says *(off-exchange)*. The gap is the on/off-exchange silver spread — **independently measured at 44% in this very county** by the probe recorded in `ba023bd` (Hopkins 48223 / 2026 / staging: off-exchange LCSP 489.38 at age 40 vs true on-exchange 705.37). Kevin's 40% is *smaller* than that 44% exactly as it should be, since his denominator was the second-lowest off-exchange rather than the lowest. ⚠️ **Real observation left standing:** the page shows **no like-for-like pair** — `lcsp_premium` (off-exchange LCSP) is rendered nowhere in `illustration25.jsp`, so the only silver figure available to compare against the affordability column is a different statistic on the other rail. That is what made the column look wrong to a careful reader, and it will do so again. Worth a RANGE-table row or a footnote; **not** a relabel. See T44 (closed by the same trace). Logged 2026-08-01. |
| T103 | A hub card's first click greeted the agent with "Enter at least one age" | **HIGH** | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (S7-D, second cut) | ⚠️ **The S7-C fix below was itself a regression, walked on production at `v0.85.08` before this one shipped.** Suppressing `inputError` was necessary but not sufficient: the method still forwarded with an empty `rows` list and no other signal, which is exactly what a real computation returning empty also looks like — `hasRates` is never set on either path, and the JSP's own **R3** fix already treats an unset `hasRates` as "nothing cached". Result: `/Illustration?mode=AGE_BAND&countyFips=48223&planYear=2026` went from a red banner over a usable form to *no banner and no form* — a collapsed `Inputs` summary bar plus a "No cached rate data for age(s)… in this county" empty-state panel, for a county that was never asked about anything. **Worse than the defect it replaced.** **Fix:** the servlet now sets an explicit `illustrationLanding` request attribute, true only on that one path (`modeExplicit` and no `ageN` present at all), and both of the JSP's form/result decision points — `hasResult` (`illustration25.jsp:246`) and the `illustrationResults` guard (`:684`) — were amended with `and not illustrationLanding`. A landing now renders the open form with its starter row, no banner, no empty-state panel, no collapsed summary; a genuinely empty cache result (ages supplied, nothing cached) is unaffected and still shows the R3 panel. **Six-for-six now: every defect on this surface declared resolved from code reading alone has been disproved by the first walk that actually ran one.** Original S7-C note follows. — **Verified on production (`v0.85.07`): `/Illustration?mode=AGE_BAND&countyFips=48223&planYear=2026` (mode explicit, no `ageN` at all) rendered the error; `/Illustration?mode=AGE_BAND` alone did not.** Cause: `IllustrationServlet.handleAgeBandMode`'s `rows.isEmpty()` branch (`:374` before this fix) set `"Enter at least one age."` unconditionally, with no distinction between a landing that supplied no age parameters and a submit that supplied a blank one. `modeExplicit` — published by the servlet since prompt K for exactly this kind of "the URL asked, we didn't infer" distinction — already existed and went unused here, the same shape of gap T99/K3-b closed a few prompts earlier. **Fix:** the error is suppressed only when `modeExplicit` is true **and** every `submittedAges[i]` is `null` (the key absent from the query string, not merely blank) — so a hub-card landing renders its starter row silently, while `age1=` present-but-blank, a derived mode, and any URL that actually carries values all error or compute exactly as before. No submitted-flag, no POST — the distinction is a fact about which parameters the URL carries, not about form state. `illustration25.jsp` itself untouched; the starter row it renders for `modeExplicit` already existed (`f651633`, K3-b) and needed no change. Regression test: click-script step against `?mode=AGE_BAND&countyFips=…&planYear=…` with no `ageN`. Logged 2026-08-01. |
| T108 | prompt L — the hub still showed three steps for a surface that has none | MED | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (S7-C) | **Companion to T95/T99, which dissolved `illustration25.jsp`'s Range/Age Band toggle into one progressive form — the hub (`ichraHome25.jsp`) never caught up.** It still carried three cards (bare `/Illustration`, `?mode=AGE_BAND`, `?mode=AGE_BAND&affordabilityBasis=FPL`) for what is now a single destination, two of them the identical servlet differing only in query string, plus "Start at step 1 and work down" copy and "Step 1/2/3" badges implying a sequence that no longer exists. **Fix: one card, bare `/Illustration`, no parameters**, copy describing one tool that goes as deep as the data on hand allows rather than a walkthrough. GroupConversion, Design Advisor and Rate Cache Admin cards kept their target, copy and gating exactly as found — their category labels ("Separate analysis", "Any time", "Admin") were never sequence numbers. **No visibility gate touched**: `sessionScope.isPspAdmin` (admin card) and `applicationScope.global.chatbotEnabled` (Design Advisor) are byte-identical; the hub's own gate, `IchraHome.isAuthorized` → `IchraAccessResolver.isAvailable`, was not in the scope fence and was not opened. This build adds no destination and widens nothing. **Display-only, code-verified, not walked.** Logged 2026-08-01. |
| T106 | The outlay card asserted a spend the page's own numbers contradicted | MED | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (S7-E) | **Walked: 55x2 at $735.59, 28x3 at $358.56, contribution $400.** Age-28's Net/Employee showed $0.00 (premium below the allowance) while "Employer Total Monthly Outlay" asserted $2,000.00 — the page claimed money was being spent and not spent on the same employee at once. An ICHRA reimburses actual premium up to the allowance; unused allowance is forfeited. **Decision: option A** — keep the figure (`employerOutlay`, the correct maximum regardless of which plans employees choose), fix the words. Relabelled **"Maximum Employer Monthly Commitment"**; caption now states the reimburse-up-to-actual mechanism in plain English and points to Net/Employee above. Option B (compute bronze-floor actual spend, ~$1,875.68 in this example) rejected — assumes cheapest-plan selection, understates real cost. Option C (a range) out of scope. **Figure unchanged, label and caption only.** Logged 2026-08-01. |
| T105 | The slider drove figures nobody could see | MED | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (S7-E) | The contribution slider sits above the results table (**L2** requires the staging banner and slider visible without scrolling, so it cannot move below) and already recomputes client-side on every drag — `render()` in `illustration25.jsp` rewrites net-per-employee, band net, group net total, the outlay card and every affordability verdict, comparing against the server's own flip point (never recomputed). An agent dragging it saw a control move and nothing else, because everything it changed was below the fold. **Fix:** added Group Monthly Net Cost and Maximum Employer Monthly Commitment (T106's label) inside the slider card, written by the same `render()` pass from the same `groupNet` / `c*lives` values already written to the table below — one computation, two DOM writes, so the two locations cannot disagree for the same contribution. No new computation, no premium/net/flip figure changed. Logged 2026-08-01. |
| T109 | A flip point past the slider's track maximum was silently dropped | MED | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (S7-E) | ⚠️ **Numbered T109, not the T108 this item shipped under in its originating prompt — T108 was already assigned to the prompt-L hub restructure (row above) in the prior session. Flagging the collision rather than resolving it silently; both fixes are real and independent.** With bands at 55 and 28, the slider track showed a tick for age 28 and none for age 55 (flip $1,098.33). Cause: `slider.max` (`illustration25.jsp`, entirely client-side, no servlet involvement) is set from the highest **bronze-floor premium** across bands, scaled 1.1x and rounded up — it has nothing to do with affordability flip points, which are LCSP-based and can run well past the premium range. `renderTicks()`'s `flip > max` check silently excluded any out-of-range flip from the tick map before any element existed — not off the end of a drawn track, filtered out of the loop. **Fix chosen (smaller of two):** an edge marker, not a widened track. Extending `max` to cover every flip point would coarsen the slider's primary resolution (showing where net cost reaches zero, near the floor premiums) for every drag to accommodate one edge case. Out-of-range flips are now bucketed separately in `renderTicks()` and rendered pinned to the track's right edge (new `.contrib-tick.edge` CSS), labelled "age N beyond track ($X)" rather than plotted as a value on the track. No flip contribution or affordability figure changed — the marker echoes the same `data-flip` the table already renders. Logged 2026-08-01. |
| T110 | Unknown username on the login form returns HTTP 500 instead of "Invalid username or password" | **HIGH** | ✅ Fixed 2026-08-01 (S7-G) | ⭐ **Found by local render verification (S7-F), fixed and locally verified in S7-G** — the loop the run existed to establish, closing on its first use. `AuthDAO.validUserName` (`AuthDAO.java:46`) evaluated `getUserByUserName(em, username).getUserName()` with no null guard; `getUserByUserName` returns `null` (not a thrown exception) for a username that does not exist, so the call NPEd, and `AuthenticateUser.doThis`'s catch clause (`NoSuchAlgorithmException \| ServletException \| IOException`) does not match an unchecked NPE, so it reached the container as a 500. **Fix:** hold the DAO result in a local variable and null-guard before dereferencing — the smallest possible change, one method, `AuthDAO.java` only. An unknown username now returns `false` from `validateLogin` exactly like a valid username with a wrong password, converging on the existing `displayLoginFailure()` path. **Verified locally, not just code-read** (S7-G, isolated Tomcat on port 8089): unknown-username and wrong-password-on-a-valid-username now produce **byte-identical** 200 response bodies (34,516 bytes, 0-line diff) after an identical 302 redirect — closing the username-enumeration risk a distinguishing response would have created. Unauthenticated `/Illustration` still 302s to `/login`, confirming the guard is intact. No `SEVERE` log entries across the run. The same line's `!= ""` reference-comparison (a separate, latent defect) was deliberately left untouched, as was `AuthDAO.validPassword`'s identical unguarded shape — not independently reachable with an unknown username under the current call graph, since it is only invoked after `validUserName` has already confirmed the user exists. Fixed `3a25690`. Logged 2026-08-01, fixed 2026-08-01. |
| T111 | Local render verification is proven and needs one manual prerequisite | MED | 📋 Planned — ⚠️ **scope corrected 2026-08-05 (S16-D); see the correction at the head of the notes** | ⚠️ **CORRECTION, 2026-08-05 (S16-D) — read this before crediting T111 for any blocked verification.** **Two consecutive runs (S16-A, S16-B) found no process and no listener on port 8089** — `jps`, `netstat` and `curl` all agreeing — and S16-D reproduced the same result a third time (`Test-NetConnection` false, no `netstat` entry, no `java` process). **The local loop has been failing at *no server running*, before reaching any authenticated wall at all.** That is a **distinct condition from T111**, which is specifically about lacking a local PSP Admin credential *once a server is up*. **T111 has been credited across five-plus sessions for a failure it may not have caused** — the two were conflated because both present as "the local render walk did not happen." **T111's actual scope is unestablished until a run reaches a live local instance and is stopped by authentication rather than by absence.** Until then, a close-out that cannot verify locally should say *no-server condition* unless it actually observed a login wall. The original S7-F finding below stands on its own terms and is not withdrawn — only the attribution of every subsequent blocked run to it is. — ⭐ **Outcome of S7-F.** Claude Code can build, deploy and fetch rendered AMS pages locally — verified end to end 2026-08-01 on Tomcat 10.1 in a private `CATALINA_BASE` on port 8089: WAR builds under `-P local`, app deploys in ~40 s against local `beta_ssa`, `GET /ams/Illustration` correctly `302`s to `/ams/login`, and `/ams/login` returns 33,776 bytes of real markup. **The only unverified link is the authenticated fetch**, which needs a local test account — deliberately not created in S7-F, since no credential may be invented or reused. **What Kevin must do once:** create a local **PSP Admin (role 5)**, `is_active=1` account and store its credentials in `C:\ssa\ssa.properties`; PSP Admin suffices because `IchraAccessResolver.isAvailable` short-circuits on the `isPspAdmin` session attribute, so no `agency.ichra_enabled` flip is needed. **Value:** four of the seven recent illustration defects (doubled headcount, error banner on hub-card landing, empty-result panel instead of a form, three cards on one URL) are plain markup and would have been caught before a release was cut. **Boundary, non-negotiable:** CSS-cascade and layout defects — the `d-flex !important` unhide, edge-marker clipping, slider-above-figures ordering — are **invisible to an HTML fetch** and still require a human browser walk. The local `rating_area_rate_cache` is empty and no HealthSherpa key is configured locally, so only the empty/fail-closed states are reachable until a fixture or a warm is decided; S7-F recommends a hand-built Hopkins fixture and deliberately did not implement it. Recipe, commands and paste-in prompt block: `docs/analysis/local_render_verification.md`. Logged 2026-08-01. |
| T112 | SLCSP computed from a non-de-duplicated premium list | MED | 💡 Backlog | **[S8-B/S8-C, 2026-08-02]** `RateCacheWarmService.sortedSilverPremiums` (`:429-441`) sorts a `List<BigDecimal>` with no de-duplication and takes `get(1)` as the benchmark. Two silver plans at an identical premium collapse SLCSP onto LCSP, understating the benchmark. **Unreachable today** — `onex_benchmark_silver_premium` has no reader anywhere, and no QSEHRA code path exists anywhere in `src/` (grepped, zero matches beyond marketing copy and a chatbot-skill description). **Becomes HIGH the day anything reads that column** — QSEHRA affordability keys on the SLCSP (ICHRA keys on the LCSP, see T44/V078), so this defect sits directly in a future QSEHRA path, in the same dangerous direction T44 was built to close. Fix before the first consumer exists, not after. Requires a cache re-warm to take effect. ⚠️ Whether duplicate premiums actually occur in a live response is only answerable empirically — not established either way. Found by the S8-B reconciliation run while verifying `docs/analysis/healthsherpa_review_2026-08-02.md` §1 against shipped code. Logged 2026-08-02. |
| T113 | The unsubsidized-premium guarantee is undocumented | MED | 💡 Backlog | **[S8-B/S8-C, 2026-08-02]** Comments only — no behavior changes. `RateCacheWarmService.basePremiumOf` (`:412-420`) falls back to `premium` when `gross_premium` is null. `premium` is post-subsidy. The fallback is safe **only because** `HealthSherpaService.buildRequestJson` (`:192-215`) sends no income or household data on the warm-cache call, so no subsidy is ever applied and the fallback field is never actually discounted in practice. **Nothing in the code records that dependency.** Adding income to the quote request — which subsidy segmentation (build plan A3 #1) would require — would silently understate LCSP, understate the flip contribution, and make an unaffordable offer display as affordable: the same LA-12 failure direction T44 exists to prevent. Fix: reciprocal `SYNC-GUARD`-style comments in both methods naming the coupling. No re-warm needed — comment-only. Logged 2026-08-02. |
| T114 | `ichra_only` parsed but never consumed — decision needed, not code | LOW–MED | ❓ Question | **[S8-B/S8-C, 2026-08-02]** Parsed at `HealthSherpaService:314`/`:324`, getter at `:355`, **zero consumers anywhere**. ICHRA-only plans therefore enter `market_low_premium`, `market_high_premium` and `lowest_bronze_premium` unfiltered, alongside plans available to any individual-market shopper. Probably the correct default for an ICHRA-context illustration — but it is an unexamined default, not a decision anyone made. **Step 1 is the decision** (does the illustration want ICHRA-only plans folded in or separated out), not a code change. Separately open: nothing in the code or docs verifies that ICHRA-only plans cannot appear in an `off_ex: false` (on-exchange) response, which the affordability calculation depends on. Logged 2026-08-02. |
| T115 | Inconsistent metal-level string matching | LOW | 💡 Backlog | **[S8-B/S8-C, 2026-08-02]** `METAL_SILVER.equals(...)` at `:354` and `:432` is case-sensitive; the catastrophic filter at `:338` uses `equalsIgnoreCase`; the bronze/expanded-bronze check at `:356` is case-sensitive. **Fails closed, not open** — a casing change upstream would empty the silver set and make affordability report "re-warm required" rather than silently display a wrong number, so this is insurance against a future vendor change, not a correction of an observed one. Requires a re-warm to change any stored value, though under current, observed API casing none changes today. ⚠️ Must not ride in a bundle with any change that alters a displayed figure — ship alone so a figure change is never attributed to a string-matching fix. Logged 2026-08-02. |
| T116 | Proposal-scoped ICHRA entitlement resolution — session-free, for the public proposal view | **HIGH** | ✅ Done 2026-08-02 (S9-B, `611d7b1` + `1a41852`) | ⭐ **Shipped and runtime-verified, not just code-verified.** `IchraAccessResolver.isAvailableForProposal(EntityManager, Proposal)` (`611d7b1`) resolves entitlement from a `Proposal` alone via `OriginatingAgencyResolver.resolve(proposal)` → `Agency.isIchraEnabled()`, reads no session of any kind, and **carries no `isPspAdmin` bypass** — the javadoc states why none may be added: a PSP admin who happens to be logged in must not cause employer-facing market data to render in a document sent to a prospect. `ViewProposal` (`1a41852`) gates in **two halves**, resolved once per request while the EM is open: (a) the `ProposalIchraSnapshotDAO` query is skipped entirely unless entitled, so `ichraSnapshot`/`ichraBands` are never set; (b) a separate pass after the existing `SCOPED` filter drops any section whose type is in `ICHRA_GATED_SECTION_TYPES`. **Multi-membership resolved as decided: strictly the originating agency** — the same resolver, and therefore necessarily the same agency, that `ViewProposal` already uses for the `TITLE`/`CLOSING` override and the `AGENCY_NAME` token, so **the gate and the branding cannot disagree.** ⚠️ **Half (b) turned out to be defence in depth rather than the chrome-suppression it was specified as:** a runtime walk showed `viewProposal.jsp` wraps the *entire* ICHRA `<div>` in `<c:if test="${not empty ichraSnapshot}">`, so half (a) already suppresses all visible output today. Half (b) was kept anyway and the code comment corrected to say so — it does not depend on that JSP detail holding (move the `<c:if>` inside the div, or render a section index from `proposalSections`, and (a) alone would leak). **Verification on an isolated local Tomcat against a real proposal (PSP 4, 21 active sections):** no-ICHRA before-vs-after **byte-identical** (md5 `fceea621`, 40,902 B); ICHRA section + snapshot present with agency **not** entitled → **byte-identical to baseline**, zero `ichra-section` divs; same fixture with agency entitled → renders, 42,435 B, one `ichra-section` div. Zero `SEVERE`/`Exception` in catalina logs; fixture fully reverted. JSP untouched, no migration. Original note follows. — **[S9-A, 2026-08-02] NOT a blocker — the S9-A probe answered YES, and that is why this is a build item rather than a schema question.** LA-17's entitlement gating rule (plus-tier proposal content appears only when the proposal is tied to an agent of an `ichra_enabled` agency) has to be answerable **at render time on `/proposal/*`, which is public and has no `HttpSession`** — `ViewProposal` is exempted from `LoginFilter` (`LoginFilter.java:87`, `path.startsWith("/proposal/")`) and reads no session anywhere in its render path (grepped `ViewProposal.java`, zero `getSession`/`HttpSession` matches). **The existing resolver cannot be reused as-is:** `IchraAccessResolver.isAvailable(EntityManager, HttpServletRequest)` (`IchraAccessResolver.java:43`) takes a request, returns `false` on a null session (`:48-51`), and short-circuits on the `isPspAdmin` session attribute (`:52-54`). **What is needed is a session-free overload keyed on a `Proposal`**, not new schema and not a new FK. The chain already exists and `ViewProposal` already walks it twice on this exact page: `OriginatingAgencyResolver.resolve(Proposal)` (`ViewProposal.java:257`, `:423`) → `Agency.isIchraEnabled()` (`Agency.java:229`, V077 column). ⚠️ **Do not carry the PSP-admin bypass across** — there is no admin on a public page, and a bypass that cannot fire is one waiting to be reintroduced by someone who does not know why it was absent. ⚠️ **Real correctness caveat, and it is the reason this is HIGH rather than MED:** `OriginatingAgencyResolver.agencyOf` returns `list.get(0)` of `Person.listOfAgenciesWithThisAgent` (`OriginatingAgencyResolver.java:126-131`) — **an arbitrary first-membership pick with no ordering guarantee.** That is exactly the tie-break `IchraAccessResolver`'s own javadoc says "is not itself an authorization boundary" (`:19-26`), and session 5 already had to widen entitlement from `primaryAgencyId` to the full `AgencyScope.detailAgencyIds()` set for this reason (`a5c0d8d`). An agent belonging to two agencies where only one is entitled can therefore resolve either way depending on list order. **Under LA-17 this is no longer a visibility preference — the resolver is the enforcement point for a legal boundary**, so the multi-membership case must be decided deliberately (any-entitled-agency vs. the originating one) rather than inherited from `get(0)`. Cross-reference **LA-17**'s enforcement subsection. All findings above are **code-verified only — nothing was run.** Logged 2026-08-02. |
| T117 | The 2026 QSEHRA indexed contribution cap is unpinned — no figure exists anywhere in this repository | **HIGH** | 📋 Planned | **[S9-A, 2026-08-02]** The session-9 design decisions — **D39** (ICHRA+ and QSEHRA+ are two lines of service) and the product-determination note in **T119** — both assume a **QSEHRA annual contribution cap bounds the product choice**. **No 2026 QSEHRA cap figure exists anywhere in this repository.** It is an **indexed IRS revenue-procedure figure** (re-published annually, self-only and family limits) and must be **sourced from primary material** — deliberately not looked up in the run that logged this, because an invented or half-remembered statutory dollar limit is worse than an absent one. **Seed it the way `ICHRA_AFFORDABILITY_PCT_2026` and `FPL_ANNUAL_2026` were: via `DatabaseInitializer`** (`addIchraAffordabilityConstants`, `e90515a`), **not hardcoded in a servlet and not via `INSERT INTO constant` in a migration** — build rule 7 forbids the latter explicitly. ⚠️ **Inherit T65's known trap with it:** the `DatabaseInitializer` seed runs only from `initializeDataBase`, the one-time fresh-install path, so **it does not reach an already-initialized installation** — production rows remain a manual deployment item, and the failure mode is silent until the moment someone quotes a QSEHRA. ⚠️ **Inherit LA-14's naming caution too:** a year inside a constant's *name* means each new plan year needs a new constant **and** a reader that finds it, and `IllustrationServlet` builds that key by string concatenation, so an unconfigured year fails closed silently. Logged 2026-08-02. |
| T118 | The QSEHRA special enrollment period is load-bearing for a sales pitch and unverified | **HIGH** | ❓ Question | **[S9-A, 2026-08-02]** The PremiumPath/QSEHRA motion for **no-group-plan employers** rests on the claim that **newly-provided QSEHRA opens an individual-market special enrollment period.** If it does, an employer can adopt QSEHRA off-cycle and employees can actually buy coverage; if it does not, the motion only works at open enrollment and the pitch is wrong in the room. **Current reading — and it is a reading, not a verified rule: the SEP attaches to *new provision*** — adoption of a new arrangement, or a new hire becoming eligible — **not as a standing anytime door** for anyone already covered by an existing QSEHRA. ⚠️ **Nobody has checked this against primary material.** `QSEHRA SEP window` is already carried as a sub-item of the **O21 standing counsel docket** (`plus_tier_build_plan.md:500`; also `ichra_strategy.md:230`), where it sits filed as *downstream annual-compliance* work. **This item raises it from downstream compliance to a sales-copy dependency** — it is not downstream if it is the sentence an agent says first. ⚠️ **Filed as a new backlog item rather than as an amendment to O21, because O21 does not appear in this file at all** — the backlog carries no O-series items, so there was no existing reference here to amend. The O21 sub-item stays where it is; this is the pointer to it from the side that needs the answer sooner. **Likely resolvable by research rather than counsel**, the same way LA-08 and LA-10's exemptions are — read it before it is priced. Logged 2026-08-02. |
| T119 | Product determination is an output of the design, not an input — the agent should not be asked to choose at first contact | MED | 📋 Planned | **[S9-A, 2026-08-02]** ⭐ **A design note, not a defect.** Whether an employer lands on **ICHRA or QSEHRA is decided by the design work**, not chosen up front: it falls out of **contribution size against the QSEHRA cap** (**T117**, unpinned), **headcount**, **whether classes are needed** (QSEHRA cannot class), **whether any group plan is retained** (a retained group plan or an FSA disqualifies QSEHRA — see **D39**), and **how subsidy-heavy the workforce is** (the Part 1 asymmetry finding: the off-exchange rail serves subsidy-eligible QSEHRA participants not at all). **Consequences for what gets built:** (1) **the agent should not be asked to pick a product at first contact** — a product selector on the way in forces a guess that the tool exists to compute, and an agent who guesses wrong has to restart; (2) **the tier-1 proposal should name no product** — it describes the employer's situation and the money, and the product name appears once the design is done. Filed against **build sequence item 6** (`swbd_ichra_build_plan.md` §3 — ⚠️ **note that item 6 is marked ✅ Done 2026-07-31 `b0e524b` in that file, while the session-8 close-out §7 treats item 6 as still-open forward work; that discrepancy is unresolved and this note attaches to whichever increment is next on that surface**) and against **T81/N12**, the interactive employer proposal — which is the surface where a premature product label would actually be seen by an employer. Consistent with T81's shipped-as-sandbox decision: naming no product costs nothing to reverse. Logged 2026-08-02. |
| T120 | The branding agency on a proposal is arbitrary when an agent belongs to more than one agency | MED | 💡 Backlog — **severity now observed, not reasoned** | ⭐ **[S9-D, 2026-08-02] The multi-membership branch has now been executed at runtime, both ways, and brand and gate AGREE. T120 stays MED and the reason is no longer an assumption.** Fixture: test agent (person `136744`) made a member of two agencies with exactly one entitled, on the isolated local Tomcat, reading **both** facts out of the **same** rendered response — brand from `<title>` and the `© AgencyName` footer (`viewProposal.jsp:9`, `:252`, both fed by `OriginatingAgencyResolver.resolve(proposal)`), gate from the presence of an `ichra-section` div. **`agents` has PK `(person_id, agency_id)`**, so InnoDB returns membership in `agency_id` order and `list.get(0)` is the lowest id — which made the ordering controllable by choosing ids that bracket the existing agency. **Scenario A** (entitled agency `990001` sorts *after* non-entitled `123355` OneDigital): brand = `OneDigital`, gate **closed**, 40,902 B. **Scenario B** (entitled agency `99001` sorts *before* `123355`): brand = `S9D LOW Entitled Agency`, gate **open**, 41,778 B, one `ichra-section` div. **The resolved agency genuinely changed between the two runs**, so the branch executed both ways rather than passing trivially. ⭐ **Scenario A is the stronger result:** the agent *was* a member of an entitled agency and the gate still refused, because the **branding** agency was not entitled — that is the rejected "any entitled agency in the agent's membership" alternative being correctly rejected at runtime, which is exactly the case LA-17's constraints would not have covered. ⚠️ **What remains code-verified only:** `ViewProposal` calls `OriginatingAgencyResolver.resolve(proposal)` **three** times per request (gate, `TITLE`/`CLOSING` override + `agencyName`, and the `AGENCY_NAME` token). They cannot disagree within one request because the persistence context loads the membership collection once and caches it — **reasoned from the EM lifecycle, not forced by a test.** Escalate to HIGH only if a caller ever resolves entitlement by a different path than branding. Original note follows. — **[S9-B, 2026-08-02] Pre-existing, not introduced by S9-B, and deliberately not fixed there — it ships alone (build rule 1).** `OriginatingAgencyResolver.agencyOf` (`OriginatingAgencyResolver.java:126-131`) returns `list.get(0)` of `Person.listOfAgenciesWithThisAgent`, a `@ManyToMany` with **no `@OrderBy` and no ordering guarantee**. For an agent belonging to two or more agencies, **which agency brands a proposal is arbitrary** and may differ between deployments, between EclipseLink cache states, or after unrelated schema work. That agency drives the white-label header, the `© AgencyName` footer, the `AGENCY_NAME` merge token, and the `TITLE`/`CLOSING` agency override — so a proposal can go to a prospect branded with the wrong agency of a multi-agency agent. ⚠️ **Severity note, and it is why this is filed rather than shrugged at:** under **LA-17** this is now *adjacent to* a compliance control without being one. **T116's gate deliberately reuses the same resolver**, so brand and gate go wrong **together or not at all** — the gate can never authorise content for an agency other than the one whose name is on the document, which is exactly the failure LA-17's constraints would not cover. **That containment is the reason this stayed MED.** It would become HIGH if any future caller resolved entitlement by a different path than branding. Fix direction (undecided): an explicit ordering, a designated primary-agency FK on `Person`, or making the caller state which agency it means. ⚠️ Do **not** "fix" it inside an ICHRA change. Surfaced by the S9-A probe, confirmed while building S9-B. Logged 2026-08-02. |
| T121 | `viewProposal.jsp`'s section dispatch silently drops an unrecognised `sectionType` | LOW–MED | 💡 Backlog | **[S9-B, 2026-08-02]** The inner `<c:choose>` at `viewProposal.jsp:168-203` has branches for `TITLE`, `FEATURES`, `PRICING`, `ICHRA_ILLUSTRATION`, `CLOSING` and `CUSTOM` and **no `<c:otherwise>`** — line 203 closes the choose immediately after the `CUSTOM` branch. An unrecognised `section_type` therefore produces **no output, no placeholder, no error and no log line**: the section is configured, active, passes scope filtering, and renders as nothing, on a customer-facing document. The failure is invisible from the admin UI, which will happily show the section as present and active. ⚠️ **A new section type needs a branch in two places, not one** — the JSP dispatch above *and* `ViewProposal`'s servlet-side `htmlContent` map, which only populates for `TITLE`/`CLOSING`/`CUSTOM` (`ViewProposal.java`, the `sectionHtml` loop); a type added to the JSP alone renders an empty body via `${sectionHtml[section.getId()]}`. Fix is a `<c:otherwise>` that logs or renders a visible admin-only marker — **not** a silent skip. ⚠️ Deliberately out of scope for S9-B, whose fence forbade touching the JSP; S9-B's gate works by removing the section from the list rather than by relying on this dispatch. First recorded in S9-A's probe (§2 Q3); filed here because no equivalent backlog item existed. Logged 2026-08-02. |
| T122 | ⚠️ The T116 gate is keyed on a section-type literal and is **blind to plus-tier content already configured in `CUSTOM` sections** | **HIGH** | 📋 Planned — **decision needed, not code** | ⭐ **[S9-D probe, 2026-08-02] Not hypothetical — this is live local config today.** The gate keys on `ICHRA_GATED_SECTION_TYPES = Set.of(ProposalIchraSnapshot.SECTION_TYPE)`, i.e. `section_type = 'ICHRA_ILLUSTRATION'`. Kevin's LA-17 rule is *"ICHRA/QSEHRA-specific plus-tier proposal content"*, which is a **larger set**. In the local `beta_ssa` (a copy of SSA's own PSP 4 config): **14 of 21 `proposal_section` rows are `CUSTOM`**, all active, all `SCOPED`, and **all 14 already carry `proposalsectionlos` links.** Four of them — `PremiumPath` (25), `QSEHRA Lite` (27), `HSA Plus` (26), `BenefitBridge` (24) — hang off **LOS 135534 "SWBD PremiumPath Program" (`PATH`), which is not suppressed**, and section `SWBD` (20) hangs off LOS 134636 `SWBD ICHRA` + 134639 `SWBD QSEHRA`. **None of these is gated by anything today.** A `CUSTOM` section carrying ICHRA copy renders to an unentitled agency's prospect right now; `QSEHRA+` (a separate LOS per **D39**) is not covered at all. **Probe conclusion: the gate CANNOT be re-keyed to "this section's LOS association includes a plus-tier LOS" using only what exists today — it needs exactly one new thing, a way to identify which LOS rows are plus-tier.** Everything else is already in place: `ProposalSection.losList` is force-initialised at `ViewProposal.java:226-231`, read by the scope filter at `:234+`, and the gate runs at `:287` with the EM still open — **populated and safe, no new query and no lazy-load risk**. What is missing is the marker. `LOS` carries **no tier, category or plus-tier column** — fields are `los_id`/`description`/`short_text`/`psp_id`/`sort_order`/`suppressed`/`service_item_id`, and the only thing distinguishing an ICHRA LOS is **its row id** (`description` is free text and not a key). **Two options, materially different in reversibility:** **(a) a `constant` row** listing plus-tier LOS ids — config-reversible (delete the row, gate falls back), and there is a real precedent in `PSP_HOME_AGENCY_ID`, which stores an agency row id and is seeded **dynamically** by `DatabaseInitializer` from the row it just created (`:286-287`, `:414-415`), never hardcoded. ⚠️ **But `constant` has PK `name` and NO `psp_id` — it is global per installation, while `los.psp_id` is per-PSP.** On a multi-PSP installation one global constant cannot name the right LOS for every PSP, which is the flaw that makes (a) weaker than it looks. **(b) a column on `LOS`** (`is_plus_tier TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`) — naturally PSP-scoped since each LOS row belongs to a PSP, but it is schema, it needs a migration, and D39's two-LOS model, pricing and the proposal builder would all come to depend on it. **This is rule 3's think-first column and it is Kevin's decision — deliberately not taken in S9-D.** ⚠️ **Two traps to carry into whichever is chosen.** First, **a naive "any linked plus-tier LOS ⇒ gate the section" rule causes a false positive on ordinary content**: sections `HRA 1` (12) and `HRA 2` (13) link to **both** the ordinary active LOS 11 `HRA/MERP Plans` **and** the suppressed ICHRA_ LOS 13 — so that rule would withhold ordinary HRA pages from unentitled agencies, a behaviour regression on non-ICHRA content, which is precisely what T116's fence was built to avoid. Second, ⚠️ **the re-key cannot live in `IchraAccessResolver`**: its class javadoc forbids this absolutely — *"No `constant` row, LOS, `ServiceItem`, `PlanType`, `ServiceModule`, or `RateTable` reference may ever appear in this class"* — so the LOS-aware half belongs in `ViewProposal` (which already reads `losList`) or a new collaborator, not in the entitlement resolver. Build-plan item 2's own rule 4 points the same way: look rows up **by PSP plus a stable natural key**, never a hardcoded id. Logged 2026-08-02. **⭐ UPDATE 2026-08-02 (S9-E): the marker now exists — option (b) was taken.** `los.is_plus_tier` shipped as **V086** (`TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, no backfill), with the `LOS.plusTier` field and a "Plus Tier" checkbox on the Service Manager's edit-LOS modal. ⚠️ **Nothing reads it — that is deliberate and was runtime-verified**: a proposal carrying a flagged LOS renders **byte-identical** to the pre-change build (md5 `fceea621`, 40,902 B). **The re-key described above is still entirely unbuilt.** Kevin's stated purpose for the flag is narrower than this item assumed — it is a **workflow trigger** (the proposal builder must collect ZIP + headcount before creating a proposal carrying a plus-tier LOS), **not** the section-visibility re-key. ⚠️ **So the `CUSTOM`-section blind spot described above is NOT closed by V086 and remains open**, and whoever picks it up should decide whether one flag serves both purposes or whether visibility needs its own. The `HRA 1`/`HRA 2` false-positive trap below applies to the visibility use only. |
| T123 | 🔒 SECURITY: Service Manager has no role check on **either** the render or the **write** path | **HIGH** | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-02 (S9-G)** — all four servlets guarded, **and RUNTIME-VERIFIED in production on `v0.86.00`** | ⭐ **[Kevin, 2026-08-02] RUNTIME-VERIFIED IN PRODUCTION on `v0.86.00`, admin path — these guards are no longer code-verified only.** Kevin deployed `v0.86.00` (= `b78948e`, which carries all four T123 guards plus S9-G's two) and confirmed a PSP admin can still reach the guarded surfaces. **This settles the assumption six guards rested on: `isPspAdmin` IS what a real PSP-admin session carries.** Corroborated in code the same day (S9-H §1.2.3): `AuthDAO.assignUserRoles` (`AuthDAO.java:93-126`) sets the session attribute at `:120` from **role id 5** (`:105`), as a primitive `boolean` that autoboxes so `Boolean.TRUE.equals(...)` matches. ⚠️ **Scope of the claim, stated precisely: the ADMIN path is verified — a legitimate admin is not locked out. The NON-ADMIN 403 path is still unverified** and cannot be verified in production without a non-admin session deliberately probing an admin URL. **Those are different claims and only the first has evidence.** Prior note follows. — | ⭐ **[S9-G] Closed: the two siblings S9-F could not reach are guarded, so the whole Service Manager surface is covered.** `ServiceManagerSort.doPost` (5 sort actions — `los`, `enhancement`, `appSection`, `feature`, `appField`) and `ServiceModuleAction.doPost` (`reorder`, `toggleSuppress`) both received the identical `isPspAdmin` → `SC_FORBIDDEN` guard. ⚠️ **Placement differs from S9-F's two in one deliberate way:** both siblings return JSON, so the guard is placed **before `setContentType`/`getWriter`** — `sendError` after the writer has been acquired can throw `IllegalStateException`. ⚠️ **`ServiceModuleAction` has NO caller anywhere in the tree** — no JSP, no JS, no server-side dispatch (verified by full-tree search, S9-G) — yet it stayed mapped at `/ServiceModuleAction` and mutating. **An unguarded, mapped, mutating endpoint with no UI is arguably worse than one with a UI, because nothing would look wrong if it were exercised.** Worth a separate decision on whether to delete the servlet outright; guarded rather than removed here because deletion is out of a hardening run's scope. ⚠️ **Usability note, not a defect in the guard:** the Sort call at `serviceManager25.jsp:1601` is a bare `fetch(...)` with **no `.then` and no `.catch`**, so a 403 (or a session-expiry redirect) is **silently swallowed** — a drag-reorder appears to succeed because SortableJS has already moved the DOM node, and reverts on refresh. Pre-existing and unrelated to this change; it only surfaces for a caller who should not have been reordering anyway, or for an admin whose session expired mid-drag. Original S9-F note follows. — | **[S9-F] Guard shipped on the two named servlets.** `ServiceManagerAction.doPost` and `ServiceManagerHome.doGet` now both open with `Boolean.TRUE.equals(request.getSession().getAttribute("isPspAdmin"))` → `SC_FORBIDDEN`, placed **before any action dispatch and before EMF acquisition** — the same shape as `AgencyAction.doPost`'s V067 guard, copied deliberately rather than improved. Verified against the precedent first: `AgencyAction.java:35-39` is exactly what T123 described, so the premise held. The guard covers **all 25 actions** on `ServiceManagerAction` (S9-E named only 3; the full switch also includes `loadStarterPackage`, `resetSectionToDefault`, `deleteFeature`, and the Enhancement / ApplicationSection / AppField families). `serviceManager25.jsp` was **deliberately not edited** — it lives under `/WEB-INF/` and is reached only by `ServiceManagerHome`'s single forward, so a second guard in the view would be a second place to get it wrong. ⚠️ **THE FIX IS NOT COMPLETE, and the reason is a servlet neither S9-E nor this run's scope fence named: `ServiceManagerSort` is a THIRD servlet on the same surface.** `serviceManager25.jsp:1601` calls it by `fetch('ServiceManagerSort', …)` — an AJAX path, which is why a form-action grep never surfaced it — and it has **5 write operations and no role check**. `ServiceModuleAction` (`reorder`, `toggleSuppress`, 2 writes) is adjacent and equally unguarded. **Both were outside S9-F's may-edit list and were correctly not touched**, but it means the Service Manager surface still has an unguarded mutating path today. **T123 stays open until they are guarded** — tracked with the rest of the sweep in **T124**. Original note follows. — | **[S9-E, 2026-08-02] Found while probing the LOS admin surface for V086. Filed, deliberately not fixed — unrelated fixes ship alone (build rule 1), and this is not an ICHRA change.** ⚠️ **This is T63's gap and worse.** T63 (`agencyManager25.jsp`) is a **disclosure** gap only, because its write path `AgencyAction.doPost` returns `SC_FORBIDDEN` to every non-PSP-admin. **Service Manager has no such guard anywhere:** `ServiceManagerHome.doGet` performs no role check, `serviceManager25.jsp` carries no `isPspAdmin` wrapper, and — the material difference — **`ServiceManagerAction.doPost` (`ServiceManagerAction.java:26-38`) dispatches straight into its action switch with no role check at all.** A repo grep of that file for `isPspAdmin` / `SC_FORBIDDEN` / `getRole` returns **zero matches**. The surface is **nav-gated only**: the Admin dropdown that links it is wrapped in `<c:if test="${sessionScope.isPspAdmin}">` (`navbar25.jsp:218`), which hides the link and guards nothing. `LoginFilter` requires only that the caller be **authenticated**, not that they be an admin — so on the current call graph **any logged-in user of any role (agent 2, client 3, applicant 4, PSP user 1) who POSTs to `/ServiceManagerAction` can create, edit and suppress `LOS`, `Enhancement`, `ServiceItem`, `ServiceModule` and `ApplicationSection` rows for their own PSP.** That is a **privilege-escalation** exposure against catalog data, not merely a disclosure one, and it is **pre-existing** — it long predates V086, which only added one more field to a form that was already unguarded. ⚠️ **V086 neither created nor widened this**: the new checkbox rides the same `editLos` action that was already reachable, and `los.is_plus_tier` has no consumer, so the worst a caller could do through it today is set a flag nothing reads. Fix is the same shape as `AgencyAction`'s existing guard — a role check at the top of `ServiceManagerAction.doPost` returning `SC_FORBIDDEN`, plus one at the top of `ServiceManagerHome.doGet`. **Ships alone; do not bundle with an ICHRA or plus-tier change.** ⚠️ Worth a sweep at the same time: T63 and this were both found by asking the question of one surface, which suggests the pattern is not confined to two. Logged 2026-08-02. **The sweep was done in S9-F — it is systemic. See T124.** |
| T124 | 🔒 SECURITY (SYSTEMIC): six admin write servlets have no role check — **`RateTableAction` is worse than T123 was** | **HIGH** | 🔨 **2026-08-02 (S9-H): 12 of 13 surfaces guarded. One deliberately skipped, two excluded by instruction.** | ⭐ **[Kevin, 2026-08-02] The S9-F/S9-G guards are RUNTIME-VERIFIED in production on `v0.86.00`, admin path** — `RateTableAction`, `PspAdminHome`, `ServiceManagerAction`, `ServiceManagerHome`, `ServiceManagerSort`, `ServiceModuleAction`. **They stop being carried as code-verified.** ⚠️ Verified claim is *"an admin is not locked out"*; the *non-admin 403* path remains unverified. **[S9-H] GUARDED THIS RUN (6 more):** `PriceItemAction` (2 actions — pricing, guard placed before `setContentType` since it returns JSON), `LibraryAction` (**6** actions — `createResource`/`editResource`/`deleteResource`/`createCategory`/`editCategory`/`deleteCategory`, multipart uploads and hard deletes), `LibraryHome`, `QuestionnaireManager25`, `PspDashboardHome`, and `PspAgencyHome` — **which closes T63.** ⚠️ **DELIBERATELY SKIPPED — `QuestionnaireInstanceAction`, and it must NOT be guarded on `isPspAdmin`.** Its six callers are all in `detailQuestionnaires25.jsp`, a column of `activityDetail25.jsp` (`:323`), and `GoActivityDetail25.agentBlocked` (`:62`) admits `isAgent`, `isPspUser`, `isPspSales` and `isAgencyAdmin`. **Agents and PSP Users legitimately work activities and post to it — an `isPspAdmin` guard would be an outage, not a fix.** It needs a *different* authorisation model (activity-scoped, like `AgencyScopeResolver`), which is a design decision, not a patch. **This is the first surface in the sweep where the N-patches approach does not apply**, and it is worth noting as evidence for the convention question T124 records. ⚠️ **STILL EXCLUDED BY STANDING INSTRUCTION, NOT OVERLOOKED: `BillingAction`, `GoBillingHome`** — Kevin's billing domain; not read for changes in S9-G or S9-H. **A deliberate deferral.** **Remaining after this run: only those two, plus `QuestionnaireInstanceAction` pending a model decision.** Original sweep and the N-patches decision follow. — | ⭐ **[S9-G] DECISION RECORDED (Kevin, 2026-08-02): T124 is fixed as N patches now, using the guard already running in production, rather than waiting on a filter or base-class convention.** The reasoning is worth keeping: **neither a filter nor a base class makes the next servlet safe by default — only default-deny does**, and that is not a change to make at speed on `LoginFilter`. Each patch is **~5 lines and is deletable** if a real convention lands later, so this is not work that has to be undone, only removed. **FIXED THIS RUN (2 of the 6 write servlets, plus 1 render path):** ⭐ **`RateTableAction`** — the worst item on the board, now guarded across all **12** actions (count re-verified this run, matches S9-F). `updatePrice`, `assignAgencyToRate`, `addRateTableRow`, `deleteRow` and `cloneRate` can no longer be POSTed by a non-admin, so **pricing and rate-table assignment are closed.** **`PspAdminHome`** — its render half, the page that serves `rateManager25.jsp` (the only poster to `RateTableAction`); same relationship `ServiceManagerHome` has to `ServiceManagerAction`. Plus `ServiceManagerSort` and `ServiceModuleAction` under **T123**, which is now closed. **STILL OPEN — write paths:** `LibraryAction` (7 writes), `QuestionnaireInstanceAction` (5 writes, `doGet` **and** `doPost`), `PriceItemAction` (2 writes — **pricing, so this is the next-highest of the remainder**). **STILL OPEN — render/disclosure paths:** `LibraryHome`, `QuestionnaireManager25`, `PspDashboardHome`, `PspAgencyHome` (**= T63**). ⚠️ **EXCLUDED BY INSTRUCTION, NOT OVERLOOKED: `BillingAction` and `GoBillingHome`.** Kevin's billing-pipeline domain; S9-G was explicitly told not to read them for changes, not to guard them, and not to propose changes. **They remain filed here and are a deliberate deferral, not a gap in the sweep.** ⚠️ The structural cause is unchanged and still argues for a convention eventually: `LoginFilter` enforces **authentication** for every path and **authorisation** for none, and admin surfaces are gated by hiding a nav link, so a servlet written without a guard **looks protected in a browser and is not.** Original sweep follows. — | ⭐ **[S9-F read-only sweep, 2026-08-02] The suspicion in T123 was right and it understated the problem. This is not a list of individual defects; it is a missing convention.** Every `*Action` / `*Home` / `*Manager` / `*Sort` servlet under `controller/` was enumerated and checked for a role gate. **Result: of the servlets with a write path, six have none at all**, listed here in severity order by what they mutate. ⚠️ **#1 is worse than T123 ever was, and it should be fixed first:** **(1) `RateTableAction`** — `@WebServlet("/RateTableAction")`, **no guard, 18 write ops, 12 actions**: `createRate`, `editRate`, `suppressRate`, `addRateTableRow`, `deleteRow`, `assignAgencyToRate`, `removeAgencyFromRate`, `createPriceItem`, `copyRate`, **`updatePrice`**, `reorderModules`, `cloneRate`. Posted from `rateManager25.jsp`, served by `PspAdminHome` (**also unguarded**). **This mutates pricing.** T123 exposed catalog *structure*; this exposes **what a client is billed** and **which agency is assigned which rate table** — an authenticated non-admin could alter prices or reassign rate tables to themselves. Same unguarded shape as the pre-fix `ServiceManagerAction`: `doPost` opens straight into EMF acquisition and the action switch. **(2) `LibraryAction`** — 7 writes, no guard (marketing/resource library, incl. uploads). **(3) `QuestionnaireInstanceAction`** — 5 writes, `doGet` **and** `doPost`, no guard. **(4) `ServiceManagerSort`** — 5 writes, no guard; **same surface as T123**, reached by `fetch()` from `serviceManager25.jsp:1601`, which is why a form-action grep missed it. **(5) `PriceItemAction`** — 2 writes, no guard (pricing again). **(6) `ServiceModuleAction`** — `reorder`, `toggleSuppress`, 2 writes, no guard. **Unguarded render/disclosure paths as well:** `PspAdminHome` (Rate Manager), `PspAgencyHome` (**this is T63**), `PspDashboardHome`, `LibraryHome`, `QuestionnaireManager25`, `BillingAction`, `GoBillingHome`. **For contrast, these already guard correctly and are the template:** `AgencyAction`, `ServiceManagerAction`/`ServiceManagerHome` (as of S9-F), `UserManager`, `RateCacheAdmin`, `KnowledgeManager`, `SkillManager`, `ImportTransitionManager`, `OutlookLinkManager`, `VendorManager25`, `ApplicationsHome`, `AgentHome` — all on `isPspAdmin`; `BpoHome`/`SkillManager` also on `isBpoAdmin`; `IchraHome` on `IchraAccessResolver` (a different but real mechanism — an early grep for `isPspAdmin` alone wrongly reported it unguarded, worth knowing before anyone re-runs this sweep). ⚠️ **The decision this needs is shape, not effort, and it is Kevin's.** Nine-plus individual `if (!isPspAdmin) 403` patches is one option; a servlet filter keyed on a path list, or an admin base class, is another and would stop the next servlet inheriting the gap by default. **Do not design that here.** ⚠️ Note the pattern's cause is structural, not careless: `LoginFilter` enforces **authentication** for everything and **authorisation** for nothing, and admin surfaces are gated by hiding a nav link (`navbar25.jsp:218`) — so a servlet written without a guard *looks* protected in the browser and is not. Any fix should make the safe thing the default. Logged 2026-08-02. |

Two candidate fixes, not decided here: (a) a final pass in `replaceTokens` that strips any remaining `{{[A-Z_]+}}` pattern after substitution, which changes behavior for every existing section and needs a blast-radius check; (b) an authoring-time validator in `ProposalSettings`'s save action that warns on an unrecognized token before it ships. If pasted HTML ever uses spaced tokens they will still render literally; that is a separate, unfiled case. **WARN fires only when a residual is found**, so the normal render path stays silent; it names the token(s) only, because no proposal or section identifier is in scope inside the method and the signature was fixed by the run brief. **Discovery worth not repeating:** the method is `private` with **exactly one** call site (`:386`, rendering `ProposalSection.htmlContent` for TITLE/CLOSING/CUSTOM); nothing else in `src/` reads `getHtmlContent()` for render, so there is no second proposal-render path and no separate PDF reader to cover. | T140 | Five servlets populate `CurrentActivity` partially — `setActivity()` without `intializeActivity()` — leaving `toDoList` unset | MED | 📋 Planned | **Found 2026-08-04 (S14-B) as the actual root cause behind T139, whose own filed mechanism did not hold.** `CurrentActivity.toDoList` (`AmsDataLocal.java:1039`) is only ever populated by `intializeActivity` (`:1185-1234`, `setToDoList` at `:1202`), which has exactly two callers — `AmsDataLocal:651` (`VIEW_ACTIVITY`) and `:656` (`VIEW_CHECKLIST`). But **five servlets set `setReFilterOnExit(true)` and forward to `ViewHome25`** without going through it, populating `CurrentActivity` by hand instead: `AddRenewal25:81-82`, `CreateBlankRenewal25:77-78`, `CreateOpportunity:267-268`, `GenerateProp25:113`, `CloseActivity25:119`. Since `reFilterOnExit` is the flag gating `ViewHome25.processData`'s persistence block (`:145`), each of these opens that gate itself and arrives at `:151` with whatever `toDoList` the session happens to hold. **⚠️ Reach vs. throw, stated explicitly because S14-B's own first summary got it wrong.** All five **reach** `:151` unconditionally — the flag they set is the gate. Whether they **throw** there depends on the entry point, because `toDoList` is null only until the first `intializeActivity` of a session: **`AddRenewal25`, `CreateBlankRenewal25` (`addActivityModal25.jsp:40`) and `CreateOpportunity` (`addActivityModal25.jsp:58`, `agentHome25.jsp:656`) are reachable straight from the PSP home / agent home landing pages with no prior activity view — so these three have been throwing**, and describing them as "successful workflows" was wrong. **`CloseActivity25` cannot throw**: its only entry point is `checklistFooter25.jsp`, under `a/activityDetail/`, so the activity was necessarily opened (and the list populated) first. **`GenerateProp25` is undetermined** — three entry JSPs (`generateSetupForm25.jsp` under `a/setup/`, `manualSetup.jsp` and `reviewApplications.jsp` under `sales/`) and S14-B did not trace how each is reached; *what would settle it:* determining, per entry JSP, whether the user must pass through a `VIEW_ACTIVITY`/`VIEW_CHECKLIST` dispatch first. **Second failure mode, not fixed by T139's guard:** when the list *is* non-null it holds the **previous** activity's to-dos, so these paths evaluate a stale list against a freshly-created activity — same single-session-slot family as the known multi-tab `currentActivity` bug. In practice the `t.isComplete() != t.wasComplete()` test at `ViewHome25:152` means an untouched stale list writes nothing, which is why this has stayed latent. **T139 guarded the symptom silently (`240cc4f`); this row is the cause.** Fix shape, undecided: either these five call `intializeActivity` (consistent state, extra queries on a path that just built the entity), or `CurrentActivity` initialises `toDoList` to an empty list and the partial-population pattern is made explicit. **Requires touching `AmsDataLocal.java`, which was deliberately read-only in S14-B.** Logged 2026-08-04 (S14-B). |

Either the guards at `:129`/`:133` are dead and should go, or the dereferences at `:124`/`:145` are unsafe and need the same guard — it cannot be both. **Not investigated in S14-B**: establishing which requires determining whether `CurrentActivity` can ever be null at all (it is a non-static inner class instantiated with `AmsDataLocal`, so plausibly never), which is an `AmsDataLocal.java` question and that file was read-only under S14-B's fence. **No known failure attached to this** — filed as an internal contradiction worth resolving deliberately rather than an observed defect, hence LOW. Ships separately from T139 and T140. Logged 2026-08-04 (S14-B). |

| T144 | HealthSherpa `_agent_id`: documentation contradicts itself on whether it is required | MED | 📋 Planned — blocks the AOR design | **Found 2026-08-04 (O2 documentation pass).** The **API Changelog, 2026-04-09** records *"`_agent_id` no longer required"* (a pre-launch change, listed alongside cancel/terminate becoming asynchronous). The **current Application Deeplink API page** lists `_agent_id` among its required parameters: *"Slug of agent of record."* **Both cannot be true of the same endpoint today.** The most likely reading is that the 2026-04-09 change applied to EnrollConnect — where `agent_of_record` is an optional object — while the deeplink retained the requirement; **but that is inference, not verification.** ⚠️ **Why this is not cosmetic:** `_agent_id` is the AOR mechanism. The entire SWBD downline attribution model rests on AOR travelling per application keyed on NPN (resolved favourably 2026-07-29), and `_agent_id` is how AMS would express that per call. If it is optional, attribution may fall back to the account's default agent — silently re-creating the single-AOR failure mode the 2026-07-28 evaluation flagged as potentially fatal to the design. Resolve by reading the linked **OpenAPI YAML** for both endpoints (see T145), not by asking a representative — this is answerable from public material. Logged 2026-08-04. |

| T146 | Evaluate `GET /api/v1/plans` (state-level catalog) as the rate-cache warm path | MED | 📋 Planned | **Found 2026-08-04 (O2 documentation pass).** `GET /api/v1/plans?state=&plan_year=&off_ex=` (also `dental_only`, `filter`, `page`, `per_page`) was **added 2026-07-09**, and the changelog states its purpose in as many words: *"List All Plans by State/Year — New `GET /api/v1/plans` endpoint **enables plan catalog caching** with pagination support."* ⭐ **HealthSherpa built this endpoint for the use case `RateCacheWarmService` currently solves by looping per-county `POST /quotes` calls.** Today the warm job makes **2 calls per county-year** (off-exchange base at age 21, on-exchange base at age 21) plus one canary per plan year; warming all 254 Texas counties for two plan years is ~1,016 calls per cycle. A state-level catalog pull could collapse most of that. ⚠️ **Not a drop-in replacement — evaluate, do not assume.** Open questions: does the state-level payload carry per-rating-area premiums, or only plan metadata (the docs describe it as *"a lighter payload than single lookup"*)? If it carries no premiums, it replaces plan/carrier **counts** but not `market_low`/`lcsp`/`benchmark_silver`, and the per-county quote stays. Interacts with **T148** and with the refresh-cadence question (rates are annual filings; the current job re-warms every 24h). `healthsherpa.md` 2026-07-30 already flagged this endpoint as *"worth evaluating"* and nobody had. Logged 2026-08-04. |
