A standalone `POST /api/v1/aptc_estimates` also exists — required fields `zip_code`, `fip_code`, `household_income`, `applicants[]`; returns `estimated_aptc` and `csr_level` (enum `00`-`06`). ⭐ **This reframes capability #5 in `docs/ichra_strategy.md` §3** ("subsidy segmentation"), which is recorded there as *"`POST /api/v1/aptc_estimates` documented, never called; the income request field on this product is **unverified**"* and sized as **Medium**. The income field is now **verified as present on the quotes endpoint itself**, so the segmentation output is reachable by adding one optional parameter to a call `HealthSherpaService` already makes — not by integrating a second endpoint. ⚠️ **Does not change the compliance position:** household income is collected pre-sale, and **D21** keeps the design census (age, ZIP, family tier, income band, no SSN) separate from the post-sale administrative census precisely so this stays outside the BAA question. ⚠️ **Also does not change the credential position** — this needs a live call, so it is behind the same staging/production gate as everything else. Logged 2026-08-04. |
| T148 | `AgeCurve` has no plan-year 2027 curve — the warm job will silently skip the year | **HIGH** | 📋 Planned — **dated: bites at plan-year rollover** | **Found 2026-08-04 during the rate-cache design discussion**, not from a defect report. `AgeCurve.CURVES_BY_PLAN_YEAR` is a hardcoded static map containing **plan year 2026 only** (`AgeCurve.java:90-92`). `hasCurveFor(2027)` therefore returns `false`, and `RateCacheWarmService.scheduledTick` skips the year. ⚠️ **Correction to this row's first draft (2026-08-04, same session):** it originally read *"there is no error"* — **that is false and was not checked before being written.** `RateCacheWarmService.java:252-256` logs at **ERROR** level, per skipped year, naming the year: *"No AgeCurve configured for plan year {} — skipping this year only, other configured years continue."* **The failure is loud in `ams.log`.** What is true, and is the actual risk: **it is silent to the user** — no signal reaches the illustration, the run does not fail overall, other configured years warm normally, and the year simply returns no counties. So this is a defect that an operator reading logs would catch immediately and an agent using the page would not. `docs/deployment_backlog.md` **D-84 was already correct on this point** and states it plainly; this row was wrong, not D-84. ⚠️ **This makes D-84 wrong as written.** D-84 records the plan-year constant moving to `2026,2027` **from 1 November** and is scoped as a constant seeding task. Adding `2027` to `RATE_CACHE_PLAN_YEARS` **produces nothing at all** until a code change ships the 2027 curve — so D-84 is a code item with a deployment, not a config item. ⚠️ **Compounding risk, and the reason this is HIGH:** the 2026 curve's own Javadoc flags that **only 5 of its 44 factors are empirically confirmed** (ages 21, 25, 40, 45, 64); the other 39 are transcribed from the CMS federal default and *"have not been checked against a published CMS source."* Every premium the illustration displays at any age other than 21 is **derived** from that table by `AgeCurve.scale()`. A wrong interior factor produces a confidently wrong figure with no symptom — the same failure shape as T47. **Verify the 2026 table against the published CMS curve at the same time as adding 2027**; it is desk work with no external dependency. Also note the curve is **state-scoped** (Texas uses the federal default; several states publish their own) — a state dimension is required before any non-federal-default state is warmed. Logged 2026-08-04. |
| T149 | Rate-cache warm paginates at `per_page: 100`; the documented maximum is 500 | LOW | 📋 Planned | **Found 2026-08-04 (O2 documentation pass).** `HealthSherpaService` paginates at `per_page: 100` (set by `eba17ad`, the fix for the 20-plan truncation finding). The QuoteConnect documentation gives **`per_page` max 500**, default 20 — and the **2026-08-15 changelog entry** records *"Quote `per_page` increased to 500."* ⚠️ **No correctness impact whatever** — the pagination loop already runs until a short page, so completeness is established either way; this is purely round-trips. In the reference county (65 plans) it makes **no difference at all** — one page either way. It only matters in a market above 100 plans, and only as one fewer HTTP call. **Deliberately LOW and arguably not worth doing on its own** — fold it into T146 if that lands, since both touch the same fetch path. Recorded so the 100 is known to be a deliberate floor rather than the documented ceiling. Logged 2026-08-04. |
| T150 | Time-boxed, agency-scoped demo override that treats staging rates as presentable | **HIGH** | ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-05** — Phase A `a0e9d11d2633b8bd422cf12e629d4244f3bf628c` (S16-C), build `86e41778bae3eb8e28eb8c522e57ad13f285cca3` (S16-D). ⭐ **Runtime-verified 2026-08-05** — full production walk against `v0.88.07` with `ICHRA_DEMO_ALLOW_STAGING_PROPOSAL=true` set and Tomcat restarted. Upgraded from code-verified; evidence in the S16-D note appended below. ⚠️ **Shipped shape differs from the sketch below on three points, deliberately** — see the S16-D note appended to these notes. | **Kevin's ask, verbatim in intent:** a PSP-admin flip that *"just treats staging like production for display purposes,"* so the Forrest demo can run the full quote→proposal path, *"understanding it won't be shown to anyone other than Forrest from a 'this is what a quote would look like'"* — and *"maybe that flip expires for safety."* ⚠️ **This reopens a decision session 10 recorded as settled** (*"the provenance gate is not to be relaxed to make the market-data demo work — not open for relitigation"*). **Deliberately reopened, not drifted into:** what session 10 rejected was a silent loosening of the check; this is admin-gated, time-boxed, agency-scoped, and aimed at a partner rather than a client. **Recorded here so the reversal is legible.** — **Why it is needed at all:** `illustration25.jsp:991-1003` renders the *"Use This in a Proposal"* control as a **disabled button** with *"Available once production rates are configured"* unless `sourceEnv == 'PRODUCTION'`. **The §1 demo walkthrough therefore breaks at step 6**, mid-pipeline, on a greyed-out control — see the correction added to `docs/swbd_ichra_build_plan.md` §1. ⭐ **The button's gate is redundant for employer-facing exposure.** `ViewProposal` independently refuses staging data at **three** further points — the illustration snapshot section (`:142`), the conditional MARKET page (`resolveMarketPage`, `:467`) and the seven market tokens (`:796`). Enabling the button on staging data therefore puts **no staging dollar figure in front of an employer**; the public proposal renders exactly the tier-1 administration content it renders today. **The gate costs the demo's central claim and buys nothing the downstream gates do not already enforce.** — **Design sketch (not a spec):** (1) **Contain on two axes** — **agency-scoped** (reuse the `agency.ichra_enabled` / `markup_enabled` checkbox pattern T61 already built, so blast radius is SWBD alone rather than every agency on production) **and time-boxed with a hard cap in code** (an expiry you can set to 2099 is not a safety mechanism; cap at ~72h from the moment it is set regardless of what is typed). Absent/blank/past = off, fail-closed, matching the `HEALTHSHERPA_BASE_URL` posture T43 established. (2) **One policy point, not six.** There are currently six independent provenance gates (illustration banner, hand-off button, snapshot section, MARKET page, market tokens, `IchraZipLookup` priced advisory). `RateCacheDAO.check()` is already the shared question S11-G made it; the override belongs there or in a thin policy class. ⚠️ **Two call shapes, not one** — `ViewProposal:142` compares the snapshot row's `sourceEnv` string directly rather than going through `check()`, so both must route through the same policy or the snapshot section stays dark while everything else lights up. (3) **Stamp the artifact.** Expiry does not cover a proposal *sent* during the window — the public link and any saved PDF outlive it. Mark `proposal_ichra_snapshot` (or the intake) as demo-override-created so *"which proposals carry demo numbers?"* is a query, not archaeology. — **Open decision for Kevin:** whether the *public* proposal keeps a discreet marker. Weakens *"this is what a quote would look like"* slightly; the build plan's own argument is that a system visibly refusing to launder staging data is evidence of the discipline SSA sells. Recommended: keep something small and unambiguous. Logged 2026-08-04. — ⭐ **[S16-C Phase A + S16-D build, 2026-08-05] Shipped, and the shape changed on three points the sketch above got wrong.** Phase A (`docs/analysis/phase_a_t150_demo_override.md`) established that **`/proposal/*` is unauthenticated** (`LoginFilter:87`) and that `ViewProposal`'s ICHRA gate was *deliberately* built session-free under LA-17, carrying a code comment forbidding exactly what a PSP-admin-gated override proposes: *"a PSP admin who happens to be logged in must not cause market data to render in a document sent to a prospect."* **(1) Not agency-scoped and not time-boxed** — a single global `ssa.properties` flag, `ICHRA_DEMO_ALLOW_STAGING_PROPOSAL`, ANDed with a PSP-admin session; absence is OFF. **(2) Not one policy point** — the override deliberately does **not** route through `RateCacheDAO.check()`, because that method is shared with `IchraZipLookup`'s advisory and `IllustrationServlet`'s county dropdown and loosening it would silently change both. **(3) No artifact stamp was needed**, because the snapshot already carries an honest `source_env` and `ViewProposal:142` refuses a `STAGING`-stamped snapshot permanently — so a demo snapshot is self-identifying by construction and the *"which proposals carry demo numbers?"* query is already answerable. ⭐ **Kevin's D1 decision: take the limitation, keep G5 closed.** The demo shows the hand-off working — button enabled, proposal created, snapshot row written — but **the proposal renders without the ICHRA section**, and that is accepted rather than worked around. The open marker decision above is therefore **moot as shipped**: no staging figure reaches the public page at all, so there is nothing to mark. **Scope: `AppConfig` (append-only getter), `IllustrationServlet` (one attribute resolved once in `doGet`), `illustration25.jsp` (the two hand-off `<c:when>` tests), `ProposalBuilder` (the two provenance guards). No `ViewProposal` change, no migration, no DB constant, no method signature changed anywhere.** ⭐ **Side value beyond the demo: because these guards have always refused, `attachRangeSnapshot` and `attachAgeBandSnapshot` had never successfully written a row on any installation** — this is the first mechanism that can exercise that path before production rate data arrives and a real client is downstream. **To use:** add `ICHRA_DEMO_ALLOW_STAGING_PROPOSAL=true` to `ssa.properties` and **restart Tomcat** — `AppConfig.get()` reads a `Properties` object loaded once at startup, never per call. Detail: `docs/session_s16d_closeout.md`. — ⭐ **[S16-F, 2026-08-05] Full production walk completed against `v0.88.07`, operator-observed by Kevin at a browser and read from production MySQL — not a Claude Code run.** `/Illustration` RANGE mode, Hopkins County TX (ZIP 75482), 3 employees → **"Use This in a Proposal" rendered enabled**, exercising **G2** (the RANGE-mode hand-off gate session 15's original inventory missed entirely). **All three staging banners still rendered**, footer still read *"Source: STAGING environment"* — **D4 held: the override enabled the button and suppressed nothing.** Hand-off routed to `/ProposalBuilder?mode=RANGE&countyFips=48223&planYear=2026&headcount=3`, parameters surviving the redirect; page source confirmed all four as hidden inputs inside the `action=createProposal` form. Proposal **#138651** created against a test prospect (A1 Door Company) already resident in production, rate package SWBD Pricing, LOS SWBD PremiumPath Program — no error, pricing rendered normally, and `journalctl -u tomcat10` showed no snapshot or `ProposalBuilder` exception in the window. **A `proposal_ichra_snapshot` row was written** — `snapshot_id 138652 · proposal_id 138651 · mode RANGE · county_fips 48223 · county_name "Hopkins County" · plan_year 2026 · headcount 3 · group_monthly_low 989.58 · group_monthly_high 2968.74 · source_env STAGING · snapshot_at 2026-08-05 14:18:34`. ⭐ **Two properties confirmed in practice, not only in design:** (1) `group_monthly_low`/`high` match the illustration to the cent, so the snapshot captured the **computed result**, not merely the input parameters. (2) `source_env` is honestly `STAGING`, so `ViewProposal:142` (G5) refuses this row permanently and it can never render on the public proposal link — **the self-limiting property held under a real write, not only on paper.** ⭐ **`attachRangeSnapshot` is no longer unexercised code** — the primary reason this override was built, ahead of its demo value. Detail: `docs/session_s16f_closeout.md`. |
| T151 | Probe: do on- and off-exchange price the same plan identically, and how much do the catalogs overlap? | MED | 📋 Planned — **two staging calls, credential already in hand** | **Found 2026-08-04 during the rate-data discussion.** Two questions, one probe, both currently unanswered and both cheap. **(a) Same-plan pricing.** ACA's single risk pool means a given HIOS id should price identically on and off exchange (see `docs/business/healthsherpa.md` 2026-08-04(b)). **This has never been tested**, and V078's Hopkins numbers show a **44% gap between lowest-off-ex silver and lowest-on-ex silver** ($489.38 vs $705.37 at age 40; $382.93 vs $551.93 at age 21 — identical 1.441 ratio). The leading explanation is **silver loading** creating off-exchange-only silver mirror plans — i.e. **different plans**, not different prices for one plan. ⚠️ **A min-vs-min comparison cannot distinguish those two cases**, and the difference matters: if matched plans price differently, the reasoning under T44/V078 is wrong and needs revisiting **before any affordability figure ships**. **(b) Catalog overlap, never measured.** The only figures on record are **65 off-exchange vs 45 on-exchange** in Hopkins, and the 45 predates the pagination finding. Counts do not give overlap. — **Method:** quote Hopkins (75482 / 48223) at age 21 with `off_ex: true` and again with `off_ex: false`, intersect on `hios_id`, compare `gross_premium` for matched plans. ⭐ **`RateCacheWarmService.canaryCheck` (`:452-491`) already implements this exact shape** — builds a `Map<hiosId, premium>` from a second response and compares plan-by-plan; it just points at age instead of `off_ex`. **Needs only the staging key already held.** Both answers land in one pass. Logged 2026-08-04. |
| T152 | Rate-cache refresh cadence is daily; the data is filed annually | MED | 📋 Planned | **Found 2026-08-04 during the rate-cache design discussion.** `RateCacheWarmService.INTERVAL_HOURS = 24`, full delete-and-replace per county via `RateCacheDAO.replaceCountyRates`. **ACA individual-market rates are annual filings — set per plan year, fixed for the plan year.** So the job re-fetches ~365 times a year data that changes once a year. The cadence was a scheduled-job default, not a reading of the data's volatility. — ⭐ **This is what made "which counties can we warm" look like a decision.** Cost model, verified against `warmCounty`: **2 API calls per county-year** (off-ex base at 21, on-ex base at 21) plus one canary per plan year; 44 rows out, every row above 21 being `AgeCurve` arithmetic. All 254 Texas counties × 2 plan years ≈ **1,016 calls per cycle**. At 24h that is ~370k calls/year; **at plan-year cadence it is ~1,016 calls once** — less than three days of the current four-county schedule. **Storage was never the constraint** (22,352 rows for the whole state × 2 years; ~276,600 for every US county). — **Proposed shape, three parts:** (1) **Full re-warm at plan-year rollover**, not daily. (2) **Periodic drift check** — one age-21 call per county on a weekly-ish cadence comparing `plan_count` / `carrier_count` / `market_low_premium` against stored values and rewriting **only on change**. This is the real risk surface: the cache holds *aggregates over a catalog*, so one plan added, corrected or withdrawn moves the stored figures **even though no rate moved**. Same shape as the existing age-curve canary, applied to the catalog. It also turns the cache into its own drift alarm. (3) **Warm the whole state**, making **T76** (warm-on-miss) the safety net rather than the primary mechanism. ⚠️ **Interacts with T146** — if `GET /api/v1/plans` can supply catalog-level data, part (2) may be one state call rather than 254 county calls. **Evaluate T146 before building part (2).** ⚠️ **Does not interact with the provenance gate** — cadence changes nothing about `source_env`. Logged 2026-08-04. |
| T153 | `/GroupConversion` can render a cache-completeness empty-state alongside an unrelated input-validation error | LOW | 📋 Planned — ⚠️ **filed into this table for the first time 2026-08-05 (S16-D); severity MED → LOW on the same date** | **Found 2026-08-05 (S16-B), recorded in `docs/session_s16b_closeout.md` but — discovered by S16-D — never written into this table.** The row is created here at its corrected severity rather than at its original one; nothing about the defect changed between filing and correction, only its reachability was established. **The defect:** `GroupConversionServlet.doPost`'s census-row loop returns immediately with `inputError` set on an invalid age or count (`:228-238`), **before** `hasRates` is ever computed (`:313-314`). Since `selectedCounty` is already set by that point (`:218`), the JSP's `<c:if test="${not empty selectedCounty}"><c:choose><c:when test="${not hasRates}">` evaluates `hasRates` as unset/falsy and renders *"No cached rate data for age(s) … in this county"* — a cache-completeness message — directly alongside the actual, unrelated *"Row N: count must be a positive whole number"* alert. **Two disagreeing explanations for the same blocked submission.** ⭐ **MED → LOW 2026-08-05: the trigger is browser-blocked.** `min="21"`/`max="64"` on the age input and `min="1"` on count mean a normal browser refuses the submission client-side before the servlet's row-validation branch can fire at all. **Reachable only by a crafted POST**, which makes this a defense-in-depth display inconsistency rather than a defect an agent can hit in normal use. ⚠️ **The server-side check is correct and stays** — client-side `min`/`max` is not validation, and removing the server branch because the browser usually prevents it would be the wrong lesson entirely. **Fix shape, if taken:** narrow the `<c:when test="${not hasRates}">` condition to also require `empty inputError`, mirroring the defensive conjunct S16-B already added to `hasResult`. Cheap, and best done by a session that already has the results block in scope. Code-verified only — read from `GroupConversionServlet.java`, never triggered live. Logged 2026-08-05 (S16-D). |
| T154 | `illustration25.jsp`'s W14 comment describes the no-JavaScript fallback backwards | LOW | 📋 Planned — ⚠️ **filed into this table for the first time 2026-08-05 (S16-F)** | **Found 2026-08-05 (S16-B), recorded in `docs/session_s16b_closeout.md` §9 but never written into this table** — the same filing gap T153 had, on the same close-out. **The defect:** `illustration25.jsp`'s W14 comment (`:241-244`) claims *"With JavaScript off the summary never renders and the form is simply always open, which is today's behaviour."* **Reading the actual markup says the opposite.** `#inputSummary`'s visibility and `#inputCard`'s visibility are both computed via the *same* server-side `hasResult` ternary (`:252`, `:260`), so with `hasResult` true and JavaScript off, the summary div **is** visible — with an empty `#inputSummaryText`, since only the client-side `describe()` populates it — while the form stays hidden and the Edit button is inert. **The opposite of "form is simply always open."** A genuine stale-comment-vs-code discrepancy, not a runtime defect: nothing renders incorrectly for a user, a comment simply misdescribes what the code does. Low priority — an internal admin tool already depending on Bootstrap JS elsewhere is not a realistic no-JS environment. **Fix shape:** correct the W14 comment at `illustration25.jsp:241-244` to describe the actual `hasResult`-ternary behavior. Note for whoever takes it: T143's `/GroupConversion` build (S16-B) inherits the *toggle*-without-JS limitation this comment describes (Edit does nothing without JS) but **not** the *content* one — its summary line renders correctly via EL regardless of JavaScript. Code-verified only, read from `illustration25.jsp`, never triggered live. Logged 2026-08-05 (S16-B). |
| T155 | `/GroupConversion` has no stale-result clearing after an unsubmitted Edit | LOW | 📋 Planned — ⚠️ **filed into this table for the first time 2026-08-05 (S16-F)** | **Found 2026-08-05 (S16-B), recorded in `docs/session_s16b_closeout.md` §9 but never written into this table** — the same filing gap T153 and T154 had, on the same close-out. **The defect:** after S16-B's T143 build, no K3-d-equivalent stale-result clearing exists for `/GroupConversion`. | T158 | The illustration hand-off and the proposal-builder intake record the same facts in two unreconciled namespaces | MED — dated to T136 | ✅ **Decided 2026-08-05 (S18-E)** — one section model, `MARKET` retired; not yet built | **Found 2026-08-05 (S16-F) during the T150 production walk, by source inspection of the resulting `proposalForm`.** The illustration hand-off carries `countyFips`, `planYear`, `headcount`, `contribution` as hidden inputs. The intake independently collects `intakeCountyFips`, `intakeCountyName`, `intakeState` and its own employee-count and contribution fields. **Both post together in the same `proposalForm`.** Confirmed by source inspection: 11 occurrences of the string `countyFips` in the page, one being the hand-off's hidden input and ten being `intake`-prefixed. **No name collision exists** — the two namespaces are textually distinct, so the `countyFips`-always-wins defect class does not apply here. **The actual problem is different: a single proposal can carry two independent records of the same real-world facts (county, headcount, contribution) that are free to disagree**, and per Phase A's gate inventory (`docs/analysis/phase_a_t150_demo_override.md` §2), the snapshot render (**G5**) and the merge tokens (**G7**) read from different ones — the snapshot from the hand-off's parameters, the merge tokens from the intake row. ⚠️ **Inert today**: G5 refuses the staging-stamped snapshot outright (T150's own self-limiting property), so a divergence between the two namespaces cannot currently surface anywhere a reader would see it. **Live the moment production rate data lands and G5 stops refusing** — which is what dates this to **T136**, not to an open-ended timeline. **Design context, not part of the defect, recorded because it bears directly on the fix:** Kevin has stated that proposal-builder-first is the flow he expects to be primary, with illustration-first as a secondary entry, and wants both to coexist. The cheap direction — illustration-first prefilling the *intake* fields, with the intake as the single source of record rather than the hand-off carrying a parallel set — is noted here as a **direction, not specced and not decided.** **Sub-item, recorded inside this row rather than as its own:** the hand-off carries no ZIP, and the intake's county `<select>` is `disabled` until a ZIP resolves it, so **county cannot prefill without either adding ZIP to the hand-off or letting the intake's select accept a FIPS directly.** `Eligible Employees` and contribution **can** prefill from the hand-off's `headcount`/`contribution` today with nothing new built — that is the cheap half of this, independent of the county question. Operator-observed during the walk plus source inspection, not independently code-verified this run. Logged 2026-08-05 (S16-F). — ⭐ **[S17-B, 2026-08-05, docs-only] Reframed: this is not one section fed two ways — it is two distinct section types, confirmed by S17-A** (`docs/analysis/proposal_render_and_tier_audit.md`, commit `69d8e37`). `ICHRA_ILLUSTRATION` (fragment `proposalIchra.jsp`, gate **G5** `ViewProposal:142`, reads exclusively from the stored `ProposalIchraSnapshot`) and `MARKET` (fragment `proposalMarket.jsp`, gate **G6** `ViewProposal:466`, reads live from the intake + rate cache via `resolveMarketPage`) are separate `proposal_section` rows with separate gates and separate data sources — never a blend, confirmed again independently this run by reading `ViewProposal.java` directly. **Which one an employer sees depends entirely on which flow the agent used**: illustration-first → hand-off params → `attachRangeSnapshot`/`attachAgeBandSnapshot` write `proposal_ichra_snapshot` → `ICHRA_ILLUSTRATION` renders; proposal-builder-first → `attachIchraIntakeIfPresent` writes `proposal_ichra_intake` → `MARKET` renders. **The namespace divergence this row originally described is a symptom of the two-flow, two-table design, not the underlying problem** — unifying `countyFips`/`headcount`/`contribution` with `intakeCountyFips`/etc. into one namespace would not, by itself, make a proposal carry both sections or make the flow-choice visible; the two write to different tables and render through different gates regardless of parameter naming. **Open decision, Kevin's, for a dedicated future session (not this run):** converge the two section types into one, or keep them structurally distinct with an explicit, documented flow-to-section rule. Not specced or decided here. Related: **F3 / T162**, where the same two section types also disagree on staleness semantics, not just data source. — ⭐ **[S18-E, 2026-08-05] Decided by Kevin, answering S17 handoff (a): one section model, not two.** A frozen payload is `ICHRA_ILLUSTRATION`'s semantics; `MARKET`'s live recompute contradicts it directly, so the two cannot converge into one type — `MARKET` is **retired**, not merged. **`MARKET` is a singleton PSP-scoped `proposal_section` row and no installation currently has one created** (S11-H shipped the render path; nothing in this codebase's history shows a `MARKET` row ever being added through `ProposalSettings`), so retirement removes a section type nothing currently uses, not a live customer-facing feature. **This does not resolve the namespace divergence this row describes on its own** — the hand-off/intake dual-write remains until the ICHRA JSON payload (T165) replaces both writers with one. **Not yet built**: `MARKET`'s section-type constant, `resolveMarketPage`, and `proposalMarket.jsp` are all still present in the codebase as of this session; this row records the decision, not a completed removal. Logged 2026-08-05 (S18-E), decision recorded in `docs/session_closeout_2026-08-05_session18.md`. |
| T159 | `/GroupConversion`'s results headline mislabels the employer share as the group premium | LOW | 📋 Planned | **Observed 2026-08-05 during walk 1 (`v0.88.06`, T142/T143 verification), flagged three times in conversation and never written down until this run — the same failure S16-F's backlog reconciliation corrected at scale for T154/T155/T156.** The results headline reads *"Current group cost $600.00/mo…"* where **`$600` is the employer's monthly share, not the group premium** — the table row directly beneath the headline labels the identical figure correctly as *"Current employer monthly cost."* **The underlying comparison is correct** — employer cost is compared against employer cost throughout — **only the headline's noun is wrong**, and it is the sentence an agent is most likely to read aloud to an employer verbatim. JSP-only, no servlet change expected. Fix shape: reword the headline to match the correct table-row label directly beneath it (`"Current employer monthly cost"` or equivalent), rather than changing which figure is displayed — the figure is already right. | T160 | AGE_BAND proposal snapshots never store `headcount`, though the servlet computes it | LOW | 📋 Planned — ⚠️ **filed into this table for the first time 2026-08-05 (S17-B), from S17-A's deferred finding** | **Found 2026-08-05 (S17-A, §4), filed this run per the standing rule that a finding lives in this table or it does not exist.** `ProposalBuilder.attachAgeBandSnapshot` computes `totalLives` across all six age-band rows (`:678` init, `:713` accumulate) and uses it once, to compute `employerOutlay` (`:720`) — then never calls `snapshot.setHeadcount(...)` anywhere in the method, confirmed by grep across the full method body this run. The sibling `attachRangeSnapshot` path **does** set `headcount`. Result: `proposal_ichra_snapshot.headcount` is populated on RANGE rows and permanently `null` on AGE_BAND rows, with no comment explaining the asymmetry. ⚠️ **Confirmed this run, not merely inherited from the audit: the rendering consequence is narrower than "may render blank."** `proposalIchra.jsp` references `${ichraSnapshot.headcount}` exactly once, at `:71`, inside the `<c:otherwise>` branch of the mode `<c:choose>` (`:30`/`:60`) — i.e. the **RANGE** branch only. The `AGE_BAND` branch (`:30-59`) renders entirely from `${ichraBands}` (per-row `lives`) and `${ichraSnapshot.groupNetTotal}`/`contribution`, and never references `headcount` at all. **So no AGE_BAND section renders blank today** — the audit's own §4 already said this correctly ("renders `headcount` in the RANGE branch only"); this row corrects the looser paraphrase that circulated in conversation. **The real risk is latent, not active:** the value is recoverable by summing `ichraBands[].lives`, but any future consumer that assumes `headcount` is populated for every mode — a report, an export, a future `ProposalDetail` preview (see S17-B handoff item c) — would silently get `null`/zero for every AGE_BAND-sourced proposal. Fix shape, if taken: either call `snapshot.setHeadcount(totalLives)` in `attachAgeBandSnapshot` (one line, `ProposalBuilder.java` near `:720`) or document why the column is RANGE-only. Code-verified this run — read from `ProposalBuilder.java` and `proposalIchra.jsp` directly, never triggered live (S17-A: the AGE_BAND write path has never run on any installation). Logged 2026-08-05 (S17-B), original finding S17-A. |
| T161 | A missing or negative `contribution` silently drops the entire AGE_BAND proposal snapshot | MED | 📋 Planned — ⚠️ **filed into this table for the first time 2026-08-05 (S17-B), from S17-A's deferred finding** | **Found 2026-08-05 (S17-A, §4), filed this run per the standing rule that a finding lives in this table or it does not exist.** `ProposalBuilder.attachAgeBandSnapshot:661-662` — `BigDecimal contribution = parseDecimalOrNull(request.getParameter("contribution")); if (contribution == null \|\| contribution.signum() < 0) return;` — confirmed this run by reading the method directly. **The return is silent**: no snapshot row is written, no error or warning attribute is set, nothing forwarded to the JSP explains the absence. The agent ends up with a created proposal that simply has no ICHRA illustration section, with no signal pointing at the cause. **Why MED rather than LOW:** this sits on the same demo/hand-off path T150 built and exercised (S16-F's production walk used RANGE mode, which has no equivalent guard — `attachRangeSnapshot` has no comparable early return on a missing input of this shape), the input is presented on `illustration25.jsp` without being marked as required for the hand-off specifically, and the failure is silent rather than surfaced. RANGE mode has no equivalent failure mode — confirmed by reading `attachRangeSnapshot`, which does not gate on `contribution` at all. Fix shape, if taken: either surface a request attribute the hand-off's landing page (`proposalBuilder.jsp`) can render as a warning, or make `contribution` a required, validated field ahead of the hand-off button on `illustration25.jsp` in AGE_BAND mode specifically. Code-verified this run — read from `ProposalBuilder.java` directly, never triggered live (S17-A: the AGE_BAND write path has never run on any installation). Logged 2026-08-05 (S17-B), original finding S17-A. |
| T162 | `ICHRA_ILLUSTRATION` and `MARKET` proposal sections have different staleness semantics, and one silently re-prices on every view | MED | ✅ **Decided 2026-08-05 (S18-E)** — snapshot and disclose, never recompute; not yet built | **Not previously recorded anywhere — confirmed against the code this run (S17-B), not merely inherited from S17-A's framing.** The two ICHRA section types (established by S17-A, `docs/analysis/proposal_render_and_tier_audit.md` Q1) have opposite staleness behavior. **`ICHRA_ILLUSTRATION` is point-in-time**: `ViewProposal:143-146` reads a stored `ProposalIchraSnapshot` via `ProposalIchraSnapshotDAO.findByProposalId` — a plain entity read, no rate-cache call — and the snapshot's `ratesFetchedAt`/`snapshotAt` are stamped once, at write time, by `ProposalBuilder.attachRangeSnapshot`/`attachAgeBandSnapshot` and never rewritten. **`MARKET` recomputes live on every render**: `resolveMarketPage` (`ViewProposal:440-514`) is called unconditionally from `doGet` (`:154`) on every request, and internally calls `RateCacheDAO.check(...)` (`:466`) and `RateCacheDAO.getRatesForCounty(...)` (`:475`) fresh each time — confirmed this run by reading both call sites directly; there is no caching or memoization anywhere in the path. **Consequence:** an employer who opens the same public proposal link weeks apart can see different `MARKET` figures on each visit, with no version history and no indication anything changed — the page carries a "Rates as of" date (`marketRatesAsOf`, sourced from the newest `fetchedAt` among the rows read) but nothing marks that the date itself moved since a prior view. **Observed today, not merely theoretical:** the rate cache re-warmed mid-walk on 2026-08-05 during S16-F's production session and the "Rates as of" timestamp advanced between two views in the same walk — operator-observed, not this run's own verification, recorded here because it is the concrete instance of the mechanism this row documents. **Filed as a design question, not only a defect:** should `MARKET` snapshot at proposal-creation time (matching `ICHRA_ILLUSTRATION`'s semantics), carry a visible "may change" disclosure alongside its as-of date, or stay live by deliberate design (the case for staying live: a proposal viewed after a correction to a bad cache row should show the corrected figure, not perpetuate an error)? **Decision belongs to the dedicated future proposal session** (see that session's handoff scope), not to this run. Related: **T158**, where the same two section types diverge on data source/namespace as well as staleness. Code-verified this run — read from `ViewProposal.java` directly; the mid-walk timestamp observation is operator-observed from S16-F, cross-referenced here rather than re-verified. Logged 2026-08-05 (S17-B), original finding S17-B (not carried from S17-A, which did not record this). — ⭐ **[S18-E, 2026-08-05] Decided by Kevin, answering S17 handoff (b): snapshot and disclose, never recompute.** The proposal is a point-in-time document — ICHRA plus-tier data is captured during the proposal-build phase and frozen, exactly like prospect/agent/LOS/rate data already is. Staleness is **disclosed** (an as-of date, a "may have changed" marker where applicable), never solved by re-fetching live. This settles the design question this row filed as open; it does not by itself resolve the live-recompute behavior described above, because that behavior belongs to `MARKET`, and `MARKET` is being retired per **T158**, not modified in place. Once `MARKET` is actually removed, this row's specific "one silently re-prices on every view" complaint becomes moot by construction rather than by fix — recorded here so that connection is explicit rather than assumed. **Not yet built**: no disclosure UI, no proposal-creation-time snapshot mechanism beyond what `ProposalIchraSnapshot` already does for the hand-off flow, exists for the future unified payload. Logged 2026-08-05 (S18-E), decision recorded in `docs/session_closeout_2026-08-05_session18.md`. |

| T169 | `SkillManager`'s Model dropdown can't represent an unlisted stored value, blanks it, and the save path silently overwrites — wrote an invalid model ID into three production skills | **HIGH** | ✅ **Shipped, code-verified — not yet runtime-verified** (S20-J, 2026-08-06) | **Observed on production, 2026-08-06.** `ICHRA_DESIGN_ADVISOR` was stored as `claude-sonnet-5` (verified working end to end). The Edit Skill modal's Model dropdown (`skillManager25.jsp:164-167`) has exactly two hardcoded `<option>`s — `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` and `claude-sonnet-4-5-20250514` — and `claude-sonnet-5` matches neither, so `editSkill()`'s `document.getElementById('smModel').value = d.model` (`:235`) silently fails to select anything and the field renders blank. Kevin selected the only sonnet-class option to clear the blank, and the save overwrote a working configuration with `claude-sonnet-4-5-20250514` — **which is not a real model ID.** Sonnet 4.5's actual snapshot is `claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929`; `20250514` is Sonnet 4's date — the dropdown's second option combines a 4.5 minor version with Sonnet 4's release date, an ID that has never denoted a real model. Three of four skills were left on that string — `NDT Testing Specialist`, `EMAIL_DRAFT_ASSISTANT`, `ICHRA_DESIGN_ADVISOR`. The fourth, `ACH Report Analyzer`, was untouched and stayed on `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` — valid and current — solely because that value came from `DatabaseInitializer.java:1002`'s seed, never from an edit through this dropdown. Repaired by Kevin via direct SQL (`UPDATE chatbot_skill SET model = 'claude-sonnet-5' WHERE psp_id = 4 AND model = 'claude-sonnet-4-5-20250514'`, 3 rows) plus a Tomcat restart. **The data is fixed; the dropdown is not — this will recur.** — **The core requirement, verified this run rather than assumed: the dropdown must always include the currently-stored value, whatever it is**, so an unrecognized model displays and survives a save instead of blanking and inviting an overwrite. Confirmed by reading `SkillManager.populateFromRequest` (`:129-141`), called unconditionally on every `"update"` (`:92`): `skill.setModel(request.getParameter("model") != null && !request.getParameter("model").isBlank() ? request.getParameter("model") : "claude-haiku-4-5-20251001")` — there is **no branch that leaves the stored value alone.** Per standard HTML `<select>` submission behavior, an element with no option selected (`selectedIndex == -1`, exactly the state the blank-rendering leaves it in) contributes no entry to the submitted form data at all, so `request.getParameter("model")` returns `null` and the ternary falls to the **silent Haiku 4.5 default** — meaning **any edit to any skill whose stored model is outside these two options** — including one that never touches the Model field, e.g. only editing the system prompt — **silently downgrades that skill's model with no warning, no log line, no confirmation.** This is worse than what Kevin actually hit: the incident required a deliberate (if forced) selection; the underlying mechanism does not. No validation of the submitted model string exists anywhere in the save path — any string reaches `chatbot_skill.model` unchecked, real model ID or not. **Secondary, lower-cost improvement:** source the option list from configuration rather than a code literal (`skillManager25.jsp:165-166`), so a model-lineup change does not require a release — the same durable fix S20-D's and S20-F's close-outs both named and explicitly deferred. **`chatbot_skill` is JPA-mapped** (`ChatbotSkill.java`), so Kevin's raw-SQL repair needed the accompanying Tomcat restart to take effect on the running instance — the third of three logged **T64** observations (a JPA-mapped config table not reflecting a raw `UPDATE` until restart; T64 rows: 2026-08-02 first, 2026-08-02 second/widened-scope, 2026-08-05 third). **Cross-reference, same rot, different sessions:** S20-D fixed the two other hardcoded-and-stale model strings this sweep found still live — `ProposalAiBuilder.MODEL` and `EmailDraftService.DEFAULT_MODEL`, both previously `claude-sonnet-4-20250514`, a retired prior-generation snapshot, now `claude-sonnet-5`. S20-F fixed a related but distinct defect in the same family — `ClaudeApiService`'s response parsing silently mishandling a well-formed Anthropic reply. Neither touched `SkillManager` or its dropdown; this is the same class of silent AI-config rot, now confirmed to have actually corrupted production data, not merely gone stale. **Every other hardcoded model reference found in this sweep, for scope accuracy — all currently valid, none of them today's defect:** `SkillManager.java:137` (this dropdown's own fallback default), `ClaudeApiService.java:31` (`DEFAULT_MODEL`, used at `:58/:103/:357/:434`), `DatabaseInitializer.java:1002` (seed value), `ChatbotSkill.java:40` (JPA entity field default), `skillManager25.jsp:215` (Add-Skill modal's default preselection). One more, lower priority and inert (a comment, not executable): `ClaudeApiService.java:112`'s javadoc example still reads `claude-sonnet-4-5-20250514` — the exact wrong string, in documentation only, no runtime effect. **Severity — HIGH, reasoned explicitly:** arguing lower — this surface is admin-only and the immediate incident was repairable by SQL; arguing higher, and the deciding factors — it already corrupted three production configuration rows with a string that is not a valid model ID at all, with no error, no warning, and no log entry anywhere in the path, undetected until an operator happened to notice; and the mechanism is not a one-time mistake but a standing landmine that refires on any future edit to any skill whose model falls outside the two hardcoded options, whether or not that edit touches the Model field at all. **Not proposed here: a specific UI fix.** The requirement is stated; the design (a computed/injected current-value option, a text field with format validation, a server-fetched live model list, or something else) is a build decision for whoever picks this up. Logged 2026-08-06 (S20-I). |
| T170 | AI page builder response truncates mid-`` ```html `` fence with no visible truncation notice — reproducible, cause not established | MED | 📋 Planned | **Observed by Kevin, deferred, during session 20's own close-out.** A "Build with AI" generation in Proposal Settings stops at the opening `` ```html `` code fence — no closing fence, no generated content after it — and **no** `⚠️ This response was cut off...` notice (added this session, S20-F commit 3) appeared anywhere in the response. Any unclosed tag inside the truncated HTML (an open `<style>` is the obvious candidate for a page-builder response) would be parsed as live DOM by the browser, and could visually swallow or misplace anything concatenated after it — including a notice that genuinely reached the client. **Both are code-plausible from this session's own reading of `extractText` and `formatProposalAiResponse`; neither is confirmed** — settling it needs one live "Build with AI" call reproducing the symptom with server-side logging of the raw Anthropic response body (which `extractText`'s existing `log.warn`/normal path does not capture on a *successful* extraction, only on a structural failure — S20-E's finding, still true here: the success path logs nothing raw). Not built or fixed this run — diagnosis only, and even that is deferred pending reproduction. Logged 2026-08-06 (S20-Z). |

| T173 | Proposal Settings' merge-token helper popup listed 8 tokens; the code resolves 30+ | LOW | ✅ **Shipped, code-verified — not yet runtime-verified** (S21-H, 2026-08-06) | **Built this session (S21-H).** The popup (`proposalSettings.jsp`, `#mergeTokensModal`) listed exactly the same 8 tokens (`PROSPECT_NAME`/`AGENT_NAME`/`AGENT_EMAIL`/`AGENCY_NAME`/`PSP_NAME`/`DATE_CREATED`/`APPLY_BUTTON`/`PROPOSAL_ID`) it always had — no ICHRA token of any kind, including the two shipped this session (T171, T172). This is the same class of defect that produced the `{{PROPOSAL_DATE}}` incident (S21-F): an author working from an incomplete list of what the code actually resolves. Token list enumerated fresh from `ViewProposal.buildTokenMap` and every `putIchra*` method (not from any doc) — 20 ICHRA tokens confirmed: 16 scalar/sentence (`ICHRA_COUNTY`, `_COUNTY_FIPS`, `_HEADCOUNT`, `_PLAN_YEAR`, the four `_CONTRIBUTION*` T80 tokens, `_PLAN_COUNT`, `_CARRIER_COUNT`, the three `_FLOOR_AGE_*`, `_RATES_AS_OF`, `_RATES_SCOPE`, `_PAYLOAD_AS_OF`) plus 4 table-emitting (`_AGE_BAND_TABLE`, `_PLAN_LANDSCAPE_TABLE`, `_CONTRIBUTION_SCENARIO_TABLE`, `_GROUP_COMPARISON_TABLE`). Added as a new group in the popup, gated by `IchraAccessResolver.isAvailableForNav(request)` (a JSP scriptlet call, mirroring `navbar25.jsp:206-210`'s own established pattern for a shared page with no controller-set `em`/availability attribute) — an agency without ICHRA entitlement sees exactly the same 8-token popup as before, byte-identical. Table tokens are visually separated from scalar ones with an explicit one-line note that they resolve to a complete `<table>` element. The 8 existing tokens' entries, order, and unconditional visibility are unchanged. **Two discrepancies found and flagged, not fixed (out of this run's scope):** (1) `buildTokenMap` also resolves `PRIMARY_COLOR`/`ACCENT_COLOR` (`:689-690`) — neither is in the popup, though both are documented in the separate AI page builder system prompt (`ProposalAiBuilder.java:89-90`); (2) the AI page builder's own system prompt (`ProposalAiBuilder.java`, `MERGE TOKENS`/`ICHRA TOKENS` blocks) is confirmed stale — it lists all 18 ICHRA tokens that existed before this session but has never been updated for `ICHRA_CONTRIBUTION_SCENARIO_TABLE` (T171) or `ICHRA_GROUP_COMPARISON_TABLE` (T172); not edited this run, a separate surface needing its own review. **`{{DATE_CREATED}}` investigated further (report only, not fixed):** `ProposalBuilder.createProposal` (`:435-458`) never calls `em.refresh()` after `em.persist(proposal)`; `Proposal.dateCreated` (`Proposal.java:27`) is `nullable=false, insertable=false, updatable=false` with a DB-level `DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` — JPA never writes or re-reads it. No `@Cacheable`/`@Cache` override on `Proposal` and no `shared-cache-mode` setting in either persistence XML, so EclipseLink's default (cache-everything) L2 policy applies. Working hypothesis: a proposal's in-memory instance is cached with a null `dateCreated` at the moment of its own creation (within the current server process) and serves that same stale null to every later read for the rest of that process's uptime — not a function of the proposal's age, but of whether it was *created* since the last restart. A proposal already resident in the L2 cache from a normal read before this bug's window would render correctly; the cheap decisive test is a restart, Kevin's to run, not this run's. Logged 2026-08-06 (S21-H). |
| T174 | One resolver now governs which rate source env (`STAGING`/`PRODUCTION`) is authoritative — every hardcoded check, including `ViewProposal.java:144`'s snapshot gate, now asks it | MED | ✅ **Shipped, code-verified — not yet runtime-verified** (S21-L/S21-M, 2026-08-06) | **Built across S21-L (two commits) and S21-M (one commit), same T-number — S21-M closes the gap S21-L's own filing named.**** S21-K found Hopkins County 2026 fully warmed at `STAGING` and two hardcoded-`PRODUCTION` checks disagreeing about whether that counts. **Commit 1 (`903c73c`):** new `RateSourceEnvResolver.authoritativeSourceEnv(em)` (`data/resolver/`), backed by a `constant` row (`ICHRA_RATE_SOURCE_ENV`, seeded `STAGING` in `DatabaseInitializer`, uncached per-call read via `AppConstantDAO` so flipping it takes effect with no restart). Every hardcoded `SOURCE_ENV_PRODUCTION` comparison this run was authorized to touch now asks it instead: `RateCacheDAO.check()`'s per-row loop, `ViewProposal.putIchraMarketTokens`'s per-row loop, and both of `ProposalBuilder`'s snapshot-attach provenance checks (RANGE and AGE_BAND). Fails closed to `STAGING`, not `PRODUCTION`, on a missing/unreadable constant — reasoned explicitly in the resolver's own javadoc and in `legal_assumptions.md` LA-18: defaulting to `PRODUCTION` would silently blank every currently-working market token installation-wide, a larger regression than continuing today's already-accepted state; the failure itself still logs loudly (`log.error`). Two sites automatically inherit the fix without being touched directly: `ViewProposal.resolveMarketPage` and `IllustrationServlet`'s crossing-ZIP chooser both call `RateCacheDAO.check()`, not a raw comparison, so fixing `check()`'s internals fixes them too. **Commit 2 (`0416f21`):** filed `legal_assumptions.md` LA-18, the accepted-risk entry this decision required — staging presented as authoritative with no marker, the risk stated without softening, reversal cost split (low for the constant, not reversible for proposals already sent under it), and the confirm-before trigger (granting `agency.ichra_enabled` to any agency while the constant still reads `STAGING`). **What this run did not reach, and why:** `ViewProposal.java:144` (`if (... RatingAreaRateCache.SOURCE_ENV_PRODUCTION.equals(ichraSnapshot.getSourceEnv()))`) gates a separate, older mechanism — the `ichraSnapshot`/`ichraBands` request attributes feeding the conditional "Market" page — and sits outside the fence's specific `ViewProposal.putIchraMarketTokens`-only scope for this file; it still hardcodes `PRODUCTION` and will disagree with the new resolver until a follow-up run authorizes touching it. `GroupConversionServlet`'s two `SOURCE_ENV_PRODUCTION` references (`:486`, `:505`) were deliberately left alone, not merely out of scope: they build an internal, agent-facing staging-warning banner (T137/T138) — a genuinely different question ("should an agent be warned this county is staging") that this installation's new staging-as-authoritative decision does not change and should not change. `RateCacheWarmService`'s own `STAGING`/`PRODUCTION` stamping (what gets written to a cache row) is untouched by design — this run changes only what is treated as authoritative on read. **T150 demo override status:** left in place per instruction; with the constant reading `STAGING`, its OR-clause is functionally redundant against today's data (nearly every cached row already matches the authoritative env on its own), but it is not dead code and remains meaningful for a row that doesn't match (e.g. a genuinely `PRODUCTION`-sourced row while the constant is still `STAGING`). It does not become harmful if the constant later flips to `PRODUCTION`: `ProposalBuilder`'s own comment (`:770-772`, unchanged) confirms a demo-admitted row is still stamped with its actual `sourceEnv`, so `ViewProposal`'s render-time gate — once also resolver-driven — independently refuses to render a `STAGING`-stamped snapshot once `PRODUCTION` is authoritative. The override affects build-time admission only, never read-time trust. **`ICHRA_PLAN_LANDSCAPE_TABLE` remains empty regardless of this change** — confirmed again this run: `ProposalBuilder.buildIchraPayload` writes `planLandscape` as `JsonNull` unconditionally (T166, the live HealthSherpa plan-fetch, was never built); no source-env check gates it at all. **Runtime-verified: no.** Both commits compiled (`BUILD SUCCESS`); no live render, no constant flip, no cache state inspected — this run touched no database.
| T175 | Texas silver load — does an off-exchange mirror advantage exist, or does the state load both channels? | LOW | 💡 Backlog — investigation, not a build; not on the demo path | Cost-sharing reduction costs are recovered by loading on-exchange silver premiums; where a state loads on-exchange silver only, carriers may file separate off-exchange-only silver plans without the load, priced lower. Sources conflict on Texas: one describes Texas as mandating a 35% silver load "across the board," another lists Texas, Vermont and New Mexico as having mandated an *on-exchange* silver load specifically — compatible only if "across the board" means uniform across carriers, not across channels. **If Texas loads both channels, there is no off-exchange price advantage in the primary market and any pitch resting on one is wrong.** **Method:** compare `RatingAreaRateCache` (table `rating_area_rate_cache`) off-exchange `lcsp_premium`/`benchmark_silver_premium` against on-exchange `onex_lcsp_premium`/`onex_benchmark_silver_premium` (V078, `RatingAreaRateCache.java:39-55`) for the same Texas rating areas. A material 20-40% spread indicates an on-exchange-only load; parity indicates both channels are loaded. **Runnable today from cached data for any Texas rating area already warmed since V078/T44 shipped** — `RateCacheWarmService` populates both pairs from two separate HealthSherpa calls per county-year (`off_ex:true` for the off-exchange pair, a second `off_ex:false` call for the on-exchange pair — `RateCacheWarmService.java:75-92`, `:325-388`), and the V078 migration header itself records a live probe with both figures already captured for Hopkins TX (FIPS 48223, plan year 2026). A Texas rating area warmed only *before* V078 shipped will carry `NULL` onex columns and needs a rewarm first — no way to tell which without a DB read, which this run did not do. |
| T176 | Document why on-exchange and off-exchange silver figures diverge — the CSR silver load, not a data defect | LOW | 💡 Backlog — comment-only, no behavior change | Decision D38 deliberately split the compliance-facing on-exchange LCSP from the illustration-facing off-exchange silver figure — see `docs/ichra_strategy.md` D38. The two will diverge systematically (V078 migration header records a live Hopkins TX probe: age 40 off-exchange $489.38 vs. on-exchange $705.37), and that divergence looks exactly like a data bug rather than the CSR silver load it actually is. **What:** add an explanatory comment recording this at the LCSP resolver — `AffordabilityCalculator` (`AffordabilityCalculator.java:17-46`, which already documents *which* column to use but not *why* the two diverge) — and at each site that calls it and renders a silver figure on the affordability surface: `ProposalBuilder.java:1227` and `IllustrationServlet.java:585` (plus its `AffordabilityResultRow.getOnexLcspPremium()` accessor, `IllustrationServlet.java:1081`). **Why:** this project has already spent a full session reconciling a contradictory account of the LCSP columns; a comment at the resolver is cheap insurance against repeating it. **Note:** ICHRA affordability is correctly measured against the lowest cost silver plan offered *on* the Exchange for the rating area, which is a CSR-loaded figure — that is correct behavior, not something to fix. **Priority:** small, comment-only, no behavior change. |

Two partial pieces do exist: `Employee.custom_id` (`:55`) already round-trips Summit's participant custom ID inbound — mapped at `SummitProviderSeeder.java:98` (`addMapping(em, ftEmployeesJ2, "participantcustomid", "custom_id", ...)`) and accepted by `UniversalImportService.java:281` — so for an **already-imported** employee the correct value is in hand; and `Person.id` (from `assignee`, `@GeneratedValue`) is AMS-owned and globally unique but sits on a contact/user record, not an employee roster, linked to `Employee` only via `Person.employee_id` when an import exists. **The gap is net-new capture, not a mapping problem.** Nothing here is decided — whether to mint on an existing entity, add a column, or introduce an AMS-side employee key is a design question for the emitter spec. |
| T178 | No per-participant annual election amount exists anywhere in the model | HIGH | 💡 Backlog — blocks the Summit HRA Enrollment export | **Found S24-B (2026-09-07), code-verified.** The HRA Enrollment file's fifth column is `Participant Annual Election Amount`, per participant per benefit. The only contribution figure in the entire ICHRA build is `proposal_ichra_intake.monthly_contribution_per_employee DECIMAL(10,2) NULL` (V088) — **employer-level, uniform across the group, monthly, and attached to the proposal rather than to any participant**. V093's two additions do not help and must not be conscripted: its own header states `monthly_stipend_per_employee` is unconditional taxable wages that must never be summed with the ICHRA contribution, and `alternative_coverage_monthly_cost` carries no carrier or product identity. `Benefit` (`model/summit/archive/Benefit.java`) has no participant dimension and no amount field at all; enrollment amounts live only in the inbound staging entities (`summit/temp/Enrollment.java`, `summit/imports/sEnrollment.java`). **Deriving a per-participant annual figure by multiplying the group monthly contribution by 12 is not a decision this backlog item authorizes** — it silently asserts uniformity and a full plan year, both of which the ICHRA design may not intend. Capture, derivation rule, or per-participant override is a design question for the emitter spec. ⭐ **Partly superseded by [T197] (S30-A, 2026-09-08): the "no source exists" premise is wrong.** This survey covered `proposal_ichra_intake`, V093's additions and `Benefit`, but **not the application answer set**, where `hra_annual_ee` ("Annual Amount per Employee", HRA package, `hra_benefit_allocation`) is a **required** annual per-employee field and is what the HRA Enrollment emitter now reads. ⚠️ **The uniformity concern above survives and is now [T197]** — the amount is flat per employee, the tiered siblings are unreachable because `employer_participant` has no coverage tier — and **the refusal to authorize a ×12 derivation was honoured**: S30-A took the annual answer precisely so no derivation was needed. |
| T179 | `Importer.processExcelFile` hardcodes `em.find(PSP.class, 4L)` — Rule 4 violation in a path that runs monthly | MED | 📋 Planned | **Found S27-D (2026-09-07), code-verified, not fixed.** `Importer.java:604` and `:696` both resolve the owning PSP as a literal `4L`, so **every `ServiceItem` auto-created by the plan-type import is stamped PSP 4 regardless of installation**. Correct on this installation by coincidence, wrong on the second. Reached live via `handleCustomImport` → `processExcelFile` (`RunSelectedImportsServlet:60`), so this runs on the monthly billing plan-type import, not in dead code. Same file also hardcodes `ActivityCategory` 1 and 2 (`:579-580`, `:677`). Fix shape: resolve the PSP from the import provider (`provider.getPspId()`, which `UniversalImportService` already does) rather than a literal. |
| T180 | `SendEmployerBillingDetail` hardcodes a `ServiceItem` id and a `Task` id — monthly billing, every installation | MED | 📋 Planned | **Found S27-D (2026-09-07), code-verified, not fixed.** `SendEmployerBillingDetail.java:166` calls `EntityLookup.getServiceItemById(em, 18)` and `:132` calls `EntityLookup.getTaskById(em, 56401L)`. Both are PSP-scoped reference rows whose ids differ per installation (Rule 4), and both sit in the monthly billing path that runs on every installation every month. A second installation would silently attach the wrong service item and the wrong task, or none. Fix shape: a resolver keyed on a stable discriminator, following `FlaggedEnhancementResolver`/`AgencyScopeResolver` precedent, not a literal. |
| T181 | `AddSetupModule25` reads one `RequiredTaskList` where every other path reads a list | LOW | 📋 Planned | **Found S27-D (2026-09-07), code-verified, not fixed.** `AddSetupModule25.addMissingTasksFromServiceItem` (`:115-119`) queries `RequiredTaskList WHERE rtl.serviceItem.id = :id` and binds a **single** result, while `ApplicationTaskDAO.getTaskListsForModule`, `RenewalService.getTasksRequiredForRenewal2` and `RenewalService.getTasksRequiredForBenefit` all use `getResultList()` and iterate. The `@OneToOne` on `RequiredTaskList.serviceItem` is **not enforced by the schema** — the baseline DDL declares `purpose_id` as a plain non-unique `KEY`, not a unique constraint. So a `ServiceItem` carrying two sequences would have **one silently applied and the other silently dropped** on this surface only. No known data in that shape today; this is a latent divergence, not an observed failure. |
| T182 | `Importer.processCsvFile` assigns a **category-2** `ServiceItem` to a `PlanType`, where every other importer assigns category 1 | MED | 📋 Planned — needs a DB probe before it can be sized | **Found S27-E (2026-09-07), code-verified, not fixed.** `Importer.java:677-708` creates one `ServiceItem` in `ActivityCategory` **2 (Setup)** and assigns it straight to `pt.setServiceItem(tp)`. Every other creation path — `processExcelFile`, `SummitImportService`, `ImportCommitService`, `UniversalImportService` — assigns a **category 1 (Renewal)** item. **The branch is dead**: `processCsvFile` is reachable only from `importMissingPlanTypes:516`, whose sole caller is commented out (`UpdateTables25.java:33`). ⚠️ **But any `PlanType` row created through it before it was disabled still points at a Setup-category `ServiceItem` today**, and a renewal sequence built under the Renewal bucket in Sequence Manager **would never attach to it — silently, with no error and no empty-list warning**. Whether any such row exists is a data question. **Query for Kevin to run when convenient, not part of any run so far:** `SELECT pt.PlanType_ID, tp.group_id FROM plantype pt JOIN templatepurpose tp ON tp.purpose_id = pt.purpose_id WHERE tp.group_id = 2` |
| T183 | `ServiceItem.providerRef` is written by every importer and read by nothing | LOW | 📋 Planned | **Found S27-E (2026-09-07), code-verified, not fixed.** `provider_ref` is set by `Importer.processExcelFile` (both items), `processCsvFile`, and `createRenewalServiceItem` in all three modern importers — always to `String.valueOf(planTypeId)`. A repo-wide grep returns **only the field declaration and its getter/setter** (`ServiceItem.java:42-43, 125-129`); no query, resolver, filter or render path consults it. It is the only surviving trace linking an auto-created `ServiceItem` back to the plan type that produced it, so it is **the natural key for fixing T182 and T184** — recorded as latent capability, not merely dead weight. Decide whether to use it or drop it; leaving it write-only invites a future reader to assume it is load-bearing. |
| T184 | The Setup-category `ServiceItem` the XLSX plan-type importer creates is unreferenced by code but user-selectable in the Add Module picker | LOW | 📋 Planned | **Found S27-E (2026-09-07), code-verified, not fixed.** `Importer.processExcelFile:606-613` creates a second `ServiceItem` in `ActivityCategory` 2 per new plan type (`tp`), persists it, and **never references it again** — it is not assigned to the `PlanType` (which takes `tp2`, the renewal one), not returned, not collected. It is nonetheless **enumerated unfiltered** into the Setup "add module" picker by `AmsDataLocal.fillModsNotInSetup` (`:1258-1261`, `SELECT t FROM ServiceItem t WHERE t.activityCategory.id = 2`). So one such row accumulates per imported plan type and is **selectable by a PSP user on any Setup, while contributing no tasks** (nothing attaches a `RequiredTaskList` to it).
