| T185 | `Import Plan ID` embeds the plan year and is an upsert key — renewal would create a second plan rather than renew | HIGH | 📋 Planned — belongs to the file 2 row-set build | **Found in the session 27 production walk (2026-09-08), runtime-observed, not fixed.** File 2 emitted `Import Plan ID` = `158-136748-ICHRA-2026`. At renewal the same plan emits `158-136748-ICHRA-2027`, which **Summit reads as a different plan** — so a renewal import would **create a second plan rather than renew the existing one**. Whether the year belongs in the key depends on **whether Summit models one plan carrying successive plan years, or a plan per year — which is unestablished.** ⚠️ **This compounds the suspicion already recorded against `Import Plan ID` in `docs/business/summit_data_exchange.md`'s ID-ownership table:** a per-employer value was accepted in testing on thin evidence (two employers both took `ICHRA2027` and enrollment still resolved), and that table already says to treat per-employer uniqueness as suspect and namespace by employer unless a test proves otherwise. **Do not change the key shape without settling the plan-year modelling question first** — an upsert key that changes orphans every record keyed on the old value, the same hazard recorded for `SUMMIT_TPA_ID_PREFIX` in deployment item D-89. Related: T186. ⚠️ ⭐ **RETIRED S31-J (2026-09-08) — the premise was wrong.** This item reasoned that keeping the year was the recoverable error (a spare plan to delete) and dropping it the destructive one (a renewal overwriting the prior year's plan). **A renewal does not overwrite: a Summit plan persists across plan years and a re-import attaches another plan year to the same plan.** So the year in the key was the destructive option all along — it would have created a second plan every renewal, indefinitely, with elections split across duplicates. `Import Plan ID` is now `{employerKey}-{keySegment}` at **both** composition sites, and the plan year travels in `Plan Year Begin`/`End`. ⚠️ **The do-not-touch is lifted, but the underlying question is not settled** — the new premise is Kevin's account and has never been tested by an import. That is now [T210], and it is HIGH for the same reason this item was. |
| T186 | The CDH plan name is static and carries a year the plan-year fields already hold | LOW | 📋 Planned — sequence after T185 | **Found in the session 27 production walk (2026-09-08), runtime-observed, not fixed.** File 2 emitted `Plan Name` = `ICHRA 2026` and `Plan Description` = `ICHRA Plan 2026 for Red Creek Solutions`. The row already carries `Plan Year Begin` `20260901` and `Plan Year End` `20270831`, so the year is duplicated into free text that **will read `2026` forever** once the plan rolls to 2027. Candidate: `ICHRA Plan for Red Creek Solutions`, dropping the year from the name. ⚠️ **Sequence this after [T185], not before.** The name should follow whatever T185 settles about the key: if Summit turns out to model a plan per year, a year in the name is correct and matches the key; if one plan carries successive years, both the key and the name should drop it. **Deciding the name first would pre-commit the key.** |
| T187 | Mojibake in the three Summit export link labels on the Setup detail screen | LOW | 📋 Planned — cosmetic, PSP-admin-only | **Found in the session 27 production walk (2026-09-08), runtime-observed, not fixed.** The three links added by S27-C render as `Summit file 1 â<box><box> Employer` — a **UTF-8 em dash (`—`, `E2 80 94`) being decoded as Latin-1**. ⚠️ **Only the new markup is affected:** the adjacent Census Upload button and the checklist on the same page render clean, so this is **those specific bytes, not a page-wide charset problem** — which rules out the JSP `pageEncoding`/response-charset fix that a page-wide symptom would point to. **Simplest fix is a plain hyphen** (`Summit file 1 - Employer`), which cannot be misdecoded under any charset and needs no encoding investigation; changing the file's encoding declaration is the larger, riskier alternative and is not obviously warranted for three labels. Cosmetic only — the links themselves work, as the walk proved. **Independent of T185/T186**; can be fixed in any order. |
| T188 | The "Services To Implement" panel hides elected services below a scroll fold | LOW | 📋 Planned — cosmetic severity, real consequence | **Found in the session 28 browser walks (2026-09-08), runtime-observed, not fixed.** `detailSetup25.jsp:28` renders the chip list inside `<div id="setupContent" class="overflow-auto" style="max-height: 120px;">`. On Red Creek Solutions it showed **four** chips while the application actually holds **six** `ApplicationModule` rows — `DCAP` and `PRA` sat below the fold. ⚠️ **This misled an investigation inside this session:** S28-C reasoned from four elected services, and the sixth only surfaced when the new Summit export error page listed all of them. It would mislead an operator the same way, and with worse consequences — configuring `SUMMIT_PLAN_TEMPLATES` (D-90) requires seeing the **complete** elected set, and a silently truncated list is how a plan goes missing from the export. ⚠️ **An affordance does exist and is not sufficient:** `#btnExpandSetup` (`:12-15`) ships `d-none` and is unhidden by the inline script at `:104-110` when `scrollHeight > clientHeight`, but it is a bare fullscreen icon in the section header with **no count and no label**, and reads as decoration rather than as "2 more". Fix shape: a count or an explicit "+N more" affordance; the overflow detection is already there and correct. **Independent of T189/T190; ships alone.** |
| T189 | `ServiceItem.code` is an unreachable column — no UI can set it, and it is null on every non-demo installation | MED | 📋 Planned — decision, not urgency | **Found S28-C (2026-09-08), code-verified.** `ServiceManagerAction` creates every Setup-category `ServiceItem` — the LOS path at `:67-81`, the Enhancement path at `:154-168` — and **neither calls `setCode`**; the string `code` does not appear anywhere in that file, in any case, in any context. `serviceManager25.jsp` has **no `code` input** (its complete field set is `action`, `description`, `shortText`, `plusTier`, `losId`, `enhId`, `sectionId`, `fieldKey`, `fieldType`, `label`, `helpText`, `isRequired`, `selectOptions`, `scope`, `name`, `packageId`, `headline`, `moduleId`, `featureId`, `libraryResourceId`, `systemManaged`, `systemSectionKey`). The `DatabaseInitializer` baseline that would have seeded coded items sits **inside a commented-out block, lines 476–510**. The only writers are `DemoDataSeeder` (via `createServiceItemWithCode`) and the inbound import path, which writes it **only on Renewal-category items** (`ImportCommitService:752`, `SummitImportService:1099`, `UniversalImportService:945`, `DatabaseInitializer:1620`). ⚠️ **So on any non-demo installation the column is null everywhere.** Either expose it in the Service Manager or drop it — **a column that looks like a key and cannot be set is how S28-B came to be built on it**, costing a build and an investigation before S28-D re-keyed on `ServiceItem.id`. Sibling case: T183 (`providerRef`, written by every importer, read by nothing). |
| T190 | Renaming a LOS leaves its linked `ServiceItem.description` stale | LOW | 📋 Planned | **Found S28-C (2026-09-08), code-verified, not fixed.** `ServiceManagerAction.createLos:70` sets `si.setDescription(shortText.trim())` — the `ServiceItem`'s description is a **copy of the LOS's short text taken at creation time**. `editLos` (`:114-128`) then updates `los.description`, `los.shortText` and `los.plusTier` but **never propagates to the linked `ServiceItem`**. The Enhancement pair behaves identically (`:154-168` create, `:201` edit). Two surfaces read that stale description: the Setup "Services To Implement" chip list (`detailSetup25.jsp:32`) and — new this session — the Summit export's mismatch error page, which is **the operator's only way to discover a `ServiceItem` id without SQL** (D-90). So a renamed service shows its old name in exactly the place someone is trying to match it to a plan template. Fix shape: propagate on edit, or stop copying and render the LOS/Enhancement short text directly. |
| T194 | Summit dedupes a re-sent file on content, so a retry after a Summit-side fix is a silent no-op | MED | 📋 Planned — blocks nothing today; needs an answer before automated transport | **Established by import 2026-09-08 (S29-J), runtime-observed.** Summit rejects a byte-identical re-send as a duplicate **on content rather than filename** — an AMS-generated Demographics file was refused as a duplicate of a hand-built probe with identical content under a different name, and changing a single byte was enough to make it process. The export has no way to perturb its bytes: `resolveFilename` gives each download a fresh `yyyyMMddHHmmss` stamp, but **the row content does not change**. Harmless while transport is manual upload and the state is genuinely unchanged — re-sending state that already applied is a no-op anyway. ⚠️ **But when an import fails for a Summit-side reason** — an unmapped element, a wrong template, a missing employer — **the operator fixes the template and re-sends the same file, and nothing happens.** The file is unchanged; the outcome would not have been. That is precisely when an operator expects a retry to work. Any automated transport needs an answer before it ships. ⚠️ **Do not solve this by adding a timestamp or nonce column to the file** without checking it against the template first — the Demographics template's mandatory-element ordering means a new column cannot simply be appended, and anything after `Branch Code` reintroduces the trailing-empty-optional defect (see D-92). Medium priority; blocks nothing today. |
| T193 | Summit enforces field length limits that AMS does not — a row that uploads cleanly can fail at Summit | MED | 📋 Planned — decide where the guard lives before building it | **Established by import 2026-09-08 (S29-I), runtime-observed.** `Mailing Address Line 1` is capped at **50 characters**: a 55-character address was rejected per-row with `'…' exceeds the maximum column size of 50. Length of data exceeded for Mailing Address Line 1.` while the other five rows in the same file were created. The AMS census parser (`CensusParseService`) imposes **no length limit on any field**, so a census that uploads cleanly can still fail at Summit, one row at a time, after the operator has moved on. ⚠️ **No other field has been tested** — name, city, email and the employer-side address fields are all unknown, and the element picker does not document lengths. Assume any of them may have a limit and expect to discover it the same way. **The fix probably belongs at census upload**, where the person is present and can correct the data, rather than at export where truncation would silently misdirect mail — but that is a decision, not a foregone conclusion, and it should be made before anything is built. Medium priority: a real employer will eventually have a 52-character address. |
| T192 | Five places assert the Summit TPA custom ID composition; the employer shape moved and they were not all updated | LOW | 📋 Planned — documentation only | **Filed S29-G2 (2026-09-08).** Five places assert the Summit TPA custom ID composition: `EmployerParticipant.java:21` javadoc, the `V094__employer_participant.sql:18` comment, LA-33 in `legal_assumptions.md:1592`, `migration_tracker.md:154`, and `phase_a_participant_identity.md:174`. The participant shape `{prefix}-P-{id}` is **unchanged and those assertions remain correct** — hyphens were proven acceptable through HRA Enrollment on 2026-09-08. But the **employer** shape moved from `{prefix}-{id}` to `{prefix}E{id}` in S29-G2. Audit all five and correct any that assert the employer form. ⚠️ **An applied migration's comment (`V094`) is among them and cannot be edited in place** — that one needs a decision about how a superseded comment on a shipped migration gets corrected, which is the only non-mechanical part of this item. `summit_data_exchange.md` was **also** on this list and is now **resolved**: its File 1 passage was corrected in S29-H, so that file no longer contradicts its own ID-ownership section. Low priority, documentation only. |
| T191 | JSPs have no project-wide `pageEncoding`; the container default (ISO-8859-1) mojibakes any non-ASCII character | MED | 📋 Planned — decision, not urgency | **Found S29-A (2026-09-08), byte-verified.** JSPs have no project-wide `pageEncoding`; the container default (ISO-8859-1) mojibakes any non-ASCII character. Fixed per-file on `detailSetup25.jsp` (T187, S29-B). A `web.xml` `<jsp-config><page-encoding>UTF-8` would fix all at once but flips decoding for every JSP, including any that carry compensating bytes. Decide before the next non-ASCII label ships. ⚠️ **The latent defect remains for every other JSP** — any file without `pageEncoding` will mojibake the first non-ASCII character anyone types into it. Evidence from S29-A: `detailSetup25.jsp` stored a well-formed UTF-8 em-dash (`e2 80 94`, U+2014) and the browser rendered `â€"`; neither the JSP nor `web.xml` declared an encoding. The response side is already correct — if both the source decode and the response encode were ISO-8859-1 the two faults would cancel and the em-dash would render correctly, so exactly one stage is wrong and it is the source decode. |
| T195 | The `125 PI Elections` emitter — `Ins125` enrollments need a different Summit file type, and its field set has never been established by import | MED | 📋 Planned — **establish the field set by one hand-built import first, then emit**; do not build from the element picker | **Filed S30-A (2026-09-08).** S30-A added the HRA Enrollment emitter, which covers the **ICHRA leg only**. Per Summit's vendor AI (2026-09-07, **not test-verified**), `125 PI Elections` is the correct file type for enrolling participants into an `Ins125` plan with per-participant premium amounts, and **HRA Enrollment is wrong for that leg**. `docs/business/summit_data_exchange.md` records the corrections this forced on files 6 and 7. ⚠️ **Its required field set rests on vendor AI, nothing else** — SDX-12 in that document notes that the true set has to come out of a results file, not the element picker, exactly as the Demographics column order did (S29-I, where an eleven-column reading would have landed City in a state field). ⚠️ **Do not generalise `writeHraEnrollment` to cover it.** S30-A deliberately did not: a different file type with an unproven field set is not a mode of a proven one. ⚠️ **Contribution schedules are a prerequisite for any election file** — they must already exist in Summit and supplying a name does not create one; that sits between file 2 and any election file. Sequence: hand-build one file, import it, read the results file, *then* write the emitter. Related: T196, SDX-11, SDX-12. |
| T196 | First live execution of the HRA Enrollment emitter, then import — it is compile-verified only; neither the emitter nor its new Setup-screen link has ever run | HIGH | 📋 Planned — nothing else in this chain should be built on it until it has run once | **Filed S30-A (2026-09-08).** `SummitExportServlet?proposalId={id}&type=enrollment` was added and **compiles; it has never been executed and nothing it produces has ever been imported.** The *layout* is import-proven from a hand-built file (2026-09-08); the *emitter* is not, and five consecutive sessions have caught wrong assertions only at execution. ⭐ **The Setup-screen link was added in S30-C (2026-09-08)** — `detailSetup25.jsp`, fourth in the Summit export block, behind the same PSP-admin gate and using the same `proposalId` expression as the other three. Labelled `Summit — HRA Enrollment` with **no file number**: the setup-sequence numbering the other three labels use has no slot for this file, and `file 5` in that scheme is a **different** file (enrollment into the Premium Billing ICHRA notice plan). ⚠️ **Compile-verified only — the link has never been rendered in a browser**, so whether it appears at all, and to whom, is unverified and is part of the first run below. What the first run must settle, in order: (1) that the ICHRA plan selection resolves to exactly one template on a real installation, and that its `Import Plan ID` is **byte-identical** to the one file 2 emitted for the same proposal — they are composed separately by design (see the deliberate-duplication comment in `writeHraEnrollment`) and only an execution proves they agree; (2) whether Summit accepts an `Effective Date` that is the plan year start rather than the participant's own (T198); (3) whether the flat per-employee amount is what the tenant expects (T197); (4) whether `hra_annual_ee` is actually attached to the LOS being sold on this installation — it is a **required** field in the HRA package's `hra_benefit_allocation` section, but section-to-LOS attachment is per-installation configuration, so a refusal naming that field key is expected behaviour, not a defect. ⚠️ Requires **D-93** (`SUMMIT_IMPORT_TEMPLATES` needs an `enrollment:` entry) and a Summit-side HRA Enrollment template before the file can bind. Related: T195, T197, T198, T194. |
| T197 | The enrollment amount is flat per employee — the roster carries no coverage tier, so the HRA package's tiered amounts are structurally unreachable | MED | 📋 Planned — a design question, not a bug; the emitter behaves correctly for a flat allowance | **Filed S30-A (2026-09-08), code-verified.** `writeHraEnrollment` sources `Participant Annual Election Amount` from the `hra_annual_ee` application answer — "Annual Amount per Employee", a **required** `TEXT` field in the `hra` package's LOS-scoped `hra_benefit_allocation` section, whose own help text reads "If flat rate, just complete this entry." Its three siblings — `hra_annual_ee_plus_one`, `hra_annual_ee_plus_children`, `hra_annual_family` — are optional and **cannot be reached**: `employer_participant` (V094) has no coverage-tier column, no dependent count, and no household dimension of any kind, so nothing could select among them. **Every row in an emitted file therefore carries the same amount**, and a group whose ICHRA is tiered cannot be emitted correctly today. ⭐ **This corrects [T178]**, which concluded that no per-participant annual election amount existed anywhere in the model: T178 surveyed `proposal_ichra_intake`, V093's two additions and `Benefit`, but **not the application answer set**, where an annual per-employee figure has been a required field all along. T178's *uniformity* concern survives intact and is this item; its "no source exists" premise does not. ⚠️ **T178's refusal to authorize a ×12 derivation from `monthly_contribution_per_employee` also survives and was honoured** — S30-A took the annual answer precisely so no derivation was needed. Fix shape, if tiering is ever wanted: a tier column on `employer_participant` fed by the census upload, then a four-way selection. That is a migration and a parser change, not an emitter change. Related: T178, T196. |
| T198 | Demographics and HRA Enrollment emit different `Effective Date` sources for the same participant | MED | 📋 Planned — settle at the first live import, not before | **Filed S30-A (2026-09-08), code-verified.** File 4 emits `EmployerParticipant.effectiveDate` in its column I — the entity's own field, applied uniformly across a roster from a single form field by `CensusUploadServlet`. The HRA Enrollment file emits the `plan_year_start` application answer instead, which is the same value file 2 emits as both its `Effective Date` and its `Plan Year Begin`, so an enrollment can never open before the plan it enrols into. **In practice the two agree**, because the census form's date is normally the plan year start; **nothing structurally forces it.** A participant loaded mid-year against a plan year that started in January would carry a participant effective date of, say, `20260601` and an enrollment effective date of `20260101`. ⚠️ **Which one Summit wants is unestablished** — the hand-built proof file used a single date for both, so the round that proved the layout could not have distinguished them. Do not "fix" either side before an import shows which Summit honours: an enrollment effective before the participant exists and an enrollment effective after the plan year opens are different failures, and guessing picks one blind. Related: T196. |
| T199 | The Maven build has no JSP precompiler, so a JSP or EL syntax error surfaces at first request rather than at build time | MED | 📋 Planned — a decision about build coverage, not a defect in any one page | **Found S30-C (2026-09-08), code-verified.** `pom.xml` carries only `maven-war-plugin` — there is no `jspc`, no `jasper-compiler`, no precompilation step of any kind. JSPs are **copied into the WAR, not compiled**; Tomcat compiles each one lazily at its first request. ⚠️ **So `mvnw package` succeeding proves nothing about a JSP change beyond the file having been packaged.** S30-C added a link to `detailSetup25.jsp` and the build passed; a typo in the EL expression or an unclosed tag would have passed identically, and surfaced only when a PSP admin opened a Setup. That is the whole reason S30-C could not claim its own change was verified. ⚠️ **The exposure is proportional to how rarely a page is opened** — a PSP-admin-only surface reached from one screen is exactly where a broken JSP can sit undetected, which is the shape of both surfaces this session touched. Fix shapes, in increasing cost: add a `jspc` execution bound to `compile` so every JSP is parsed at build time (catches syntax and taglib errors, not runtime EL resolution); or accept the gap and require a local render before any JSP change is called done. ⚠️ **The second option is what the project does today by default, but nothing records it as a decision** — which is why a run can reasonably read `BUILD SUCCESS` as coverage it is not. Sibling case: T191 (no project-wide `pageEncoding`) is the same class — a build-time property of JSPs that nothing enforces. |
| T200 | The enrollment export's annual amount is read from `hra_annual_ee` on the application, which locks at Setup conversion — a blank or wrong value at lock time leaves the employer permanently unexportable with no correction path | HIGH | 📋 Planned — decide where a correctable amount lives before the first real group | **Runtime-verified 2026-09-08 (S31-E)** on proposal 140956: `/SummitExport?proposalId=140956&type=enrollment` refused, and the refusal advised correcting the value on the application — advice that **cannot be followed**. Kevin confirms applications lock when they convert to a Setup, and the enrollment export only ever runs against a Setup; he also confirms the element would not render for an unquoted LOS in any case. ⚠️ **There is no unlock mechanism anywhere in AMS — searched and confirmed absent in S31-E, not assumed.** `Application` has no `locked` column; the lock is `status`, and `ApplyForProposal:48-50` blocks the editable form for any status other than `IN_PROGRESS`. All four `setStatus("IN_PROGRESS")` sites are creation-only paths (`ApplyForProposal:124,330,373`, `SaveApplicationProgress:55-59`, each guarded by `application == null` or the same status block), and `ReviewApplication` only moves status **forward** — `APPROVED`, `DENIED`, `MORE_INFO`, `UNDER_REVIEW` (`:303,333,352,366`). **Nothing returns an application to `IN_PROGRESS`**, and even `MORE_INFO` renders "your administrator will be in touch" rather than an editable form (`ApplyForProposal:57-58`). So today a locked wrong answer is correctable only by editing the database. Options to weigh: a PSP-admin unlock path; a Setup-side override that the export prefers over the application answer; or a different source for the amount entirely. ⚠️ **The message text was corrected in S31-E so it no longer advises the impossible, but two javadoc lines in `SummitExportServlet` (`parseAnnualElectionAmount`, "A refusal costs one corrected answer on the application") still repeat the false claim** — they were outside that run's fence and should be fixed with whatever this item settles. Related: T197, T196. |
| T201 | `writeHraEnrollment` enrolls the full census roster into the **funded** ICHRA plan, but AMS stores no election state | HIGH | 📋 Planned — **do not run the enrollment export against a real group until this is resolved** | **Confirmed by Kevin, 2026-09-08 (S31-E).** The emitter takes exactly the Demographics roster — every participant on `employer_participant` — and writes an enrollment row for each. ⚠️ **That is correct only for a census-scoped plan, and the ICHRA CDH plan is not one.** Funded plans (ICHRA, both `Ins125` rails, FSA, DCAP) must load from **confirmed elections**; only the Premium Billing ICHRA **notice** plan is census-scoped, because everyone offered the ICHRA needs the notice including those who will opt out. A Presidio-style ICHRA is expected to see **heavy opt-out**, so enrolling the full census would fund benefits for people who declined. ⚠️ **AMS stores no election state at all** — there is no entity, column or import that records who accepted or declined, so the emitter could not filter even if it wanted to. **Enrollment therefore remains a manual process by decision** until the census, file 2 mapping and FTP transport work is finished. This is not a defect in the emitter's code, which does what it was specified to do; it is a scope boundary that was not visible when the emitter was built. Related: T196, T197. |
| T202 | Admin screen for the Summit plan template mapping — until it exists the V095 table cannot be populated and the `ssa.properties` property is the only working path | HIGH | 🔨 **Built S31-F (2026-09-08), pending verification — NOT done.** `SummitPlanTemplateAdmin` + `summitPlanTemplateAdmin25.jsp` + one nav entry, compile-verified only. ⚠️ **The screen has never been rendered, the nav entry has never been seen, and no row has ever been written to the V095 table** — which is still unapplied. The build carries no JSP precompiler (T199), so a JSP or EL error would surface at first click, not at build time. Verify by applying V095, deploying, and adding one mapping. | **Filed S31-D (2026-09-08).** V095 created `summit_plan_template_map` and `SummitPlanTemplateResolver.configured(em, pspId)` reads it ahead of `SUMMIT_PLAN_TEMPLATES`, falling back to the property when a PSP has no active row. ⚠️ **There is no way to insert a row.** No screen, no servlet, no seed — the migration deliberately inserts nothing (build rule 5), so on every installation the table is empty and the fallback is what actually runs. **The whole point of V095 — a mapping a PSP admin can change without server access and a Tomcat restart — is unrealised until this ships.** Screen needs: list active and inactive mappings for the PSP; create and edit `service_item_id`, `template_id`, `key_segment`, `label`, `sort_order`; toggle `is_active`. ⚠️ **The `ServiceItem` picker is the hard part, not the form** — `ServiceItem.code` is null on every real installation (T189) and `description` is a stale copy of a LOS's short text after a rename (T190), so the picker must show the id, and the id is currently discoverable only by deliberately mis-configuring the property and reading the export's error page (D-90). ⚠️ **`uq_summit_plan_template_map_psp_service` will reject a second row for a service item even when the first is inactive** — the screen must re-point the existing row rather than offering a create that fails at the database. Related: T189, T190, D-90. |
| T203 | Tables added by migration are absent from `DatabaseInitializer`, so a freshly initialized database lacks them | MED | 📋 Planned — a decision about what `DatabaseInitializer` is for, not a defect in any one migration | **Found S31-D (2026-09-08), code-verified.** V095 followed V094's precedent and did not touch `DatabaseInitializer`; the check that established that precedent is that `employer_participant` and `EmployerParticipant` appear in that file **zero times**, despite V094 having shipped and being applied on production. ⚠️ **So there are now at least two tables that exist only if the migrations were run** — a database stood up purely through `DatabaseInitializer` has neither, and the failure surfaces as a runtime SQL error on the first read rather than at initialization. Whether that matters depends on a question nobody has written down: **is `DatabaseInitializer` meant to produce a complete schema, or only to seed reference rows into a schema the migrations built?** If the former, every migration since it was last synced is missing and this is a backlog of unknown size; if the latter, it is correct as it stands and the expectation should be recorded so no future run re-raises it. **Answer the question before writing any code** — adding one table to it without settling the intent would make the file half of two things. ⚠️ Note the numbering: T200 and T201 were unused when this row was written and are **now assigned** (S31-E, 2026-09-08) — S31-A and S31-B never ran, so neither claimed a number and no gap was left. |
| T204 | The `key_segment` validation rule now exists in two places and nothing keeps them in step | MED | 📋 Planned — small, but the failure it prevents is silent | **Filed S31-F (2026-09-08), code-verified.** `SummitPlanTemplateResolver.keySegmentRejection` is `private`, and S31-F's fence did not permit editing that class, so `SummitPlanTemplateAdmin.keySegmentRejection` is a **deliberate verbatim copy** — the rule was read from the resolver rather than recalled, and both reject a blank segment and one containing a pipe or whitespace. ⚠️ **The drift is silent in the worst direction.** A segment the screen accepts but the resolver rejects is **saved successfully and then skipped at export time**, costing one plan from Summit file 2 with nothing on screen to say so — the operator sees a saved mapping and a file missing a plan, with no connection between them. (The reverse — the screen rejecting what the resolver would accept — is merely annoying and visible.) Fix shape: make the resolver's method package-visible or public and delete the copy, so there is one rule. ⚠️ **Do not instead relax the screen's check** — the constraint exists because `key_segment` travels inside `Import Plan ID`, which is an upsert key in a pipe-delimited file. Related: T202. |
| T205 | How Summit wants a Boolean in a delimited file is unproven — `true`/`false`, `1`/`0` and `Y`/`N` are all plausible and none has been imported | MED | 📋 Planned — **settle by one hand-built import**, the way every other Summit question was settled | **Filed S31-H (2026-09-08).** The `Employer CDH Plan` template's optional elements include several Booleans — `Run-out Enabled`, `Run-out Calculation (by date)`, `Grace Period Enabled`, `Grace Period Calculation (by date)` — and **the element list says only "Boolean"**, with no indication of the representation a delimited file should carry. ⚠️ **S31-H deliberately did not guess in code.** Both sides are config — `SUMMIT_CDH_BOOL_TRUE` and `SUMMIT_CDH_BOOL_FALSE`, defaulting to `true`/`false` (D-94) — so settling this is a properties edit and a Tomcat restart, not a build. ⚠️ **A wrong Boolean representation is the silent kind of wrong:** Summit accepts a wrong plan setting without complaint, exactly as it does a wrong amount (T197) and a wrong grace setting, so this will not surface as an import error — it surfaces as a plan that behaves differently from what was sold. Settle it the way `Employer TPA Custom ID`'s alphanumeric rule and the Demographics A–L order were settled: hand-build one file, import it, read the results file. **Do this in the same round that maps the optional elements on `ZZ_TEST_CDH`** — the two questions cannot be answered separately, since no Boolean can be tested until an element carrying one is mapped. Related: T206, D-94. |
| T206 | Plans dropped from Summit file 2 are logged and never shown — an operator sees a file with fewer rows than expected and no explanation | MED | 📋 Planned — the behaviour is correct; only the visibility is missing | **Filed S31-H (2026-09-08), code-verified.** Two distinct cases now omit a plan from file 2, and **both are `WARN`-log-only**: (1) an **elected service with no configured plan template** (S31-D) — the filter tests config against elections and never the reverse, so the item is skipped silently; (2) a plan whose end-of-year answer is **`Carryover`** (S31-H) — carryover has no element anywhere in the `Employer CDH Plan` template, so the plan cannot be imported at all and must be hand-built in Summit. ⚠️ **Neither reaches the person generating the file.** They download it, see three rows where they expected four, and have no way to learn why without reading `catalina.out` — which this project does not ask of anyone, the same reasoning that made file 2's mismatch error page the operator's only route to a `ServiceItem` id (D-90). ⚠️ **The carryover case is the worse of the two**, because the omitted plan still has to be built by hand and nothing tells anyone that: the failure is not a missing row, it is a benefit that never gets set up. ⚠️ **AMS collects no carryover amount** (S31-H searched the whole `s125_fsa` field set), so even a screen can only name the plan and say "get the amount from the employer". Fix shape: a post-generation summary on the Setup screen, or an interstitial before the download — deliberately **not** a refusal, since emitting the other plans is correct. Related: T202, D-90. |
| T207 | No carryover amount is collected anywhere on the application, so a skipped carryover FSA cannot tell anyone what amount to build by hand | MED | 📋 Planned — **a form gap, not a code gap**; the emitter is behaving correctly | **Filed S31-I (2026-09-08), code-verified.** A Health FSA answering `Carryover` on `hfsa_roll_or_grace` is omitted from Summit file 2 and logged, because carryover has no element anywhere in the `Employer CDH Plan` template and the plan must be built by hand in Summit (S31-H). ⚠️ **But the log line cannot name an amount, because AMS never collects one.** S31-I searched the complete `s125_fsa` field set and none of its 22 fields is a carryover amount. **`hfsa_maximum_amount` is the annual election limit, not a carryover amount**, and the `hra` package's `hra_roll_limit_annual`/`hra_roll_limit_total` belong to a different plan type. ⚠️ **So whoever hand-builds the plan has to go back to the employer for a figure the employer already chose** — the sale recorded the feature but never the number. Fix shape: add a carryover-amount field to the `health_fsa` section, conditional on the `Carryover` answer, and name it in the skip log. Related: T206, T197. |
| T208 | `OPEN_ENROLL_START` and `OPEN_ENROLL_END` are supported tokens with no source and always emit empty | LOW | 📋 Planned — decide: collect the dates, or drop the tokens | **Filed S31-I (2026-09-08), code-verified.** S31-H's optional-element block supports both tokens so a Summit template mapping those columns still binds positionally, **but AMS collects no open-enrollment dates anywhere** — S31-I searched every application package and no `open_enroll*` field exists in any of them. ⚠️ **Configuring either token today produces a permanently empty column**, which is honest but useless, and a reader of `SUMMIT_CDH_OPTIONAL_ELEMENTS` has no way to know that from the key alone. Two clean answers, and the choice is a business one: **collect the dates** (a new section on the `s125_fsa` package, since open enrollment is a plan-year concept) and emit them; or **drop both tokens**, on the grounds that a token nothing can fill is a trap. ⚠️ **Do not leave it as-is indefinitely** — the tokens read as capability and are not, the same shape as `ServiceItem.code` looking like a key while being null everywhere (T189). Low priority: nothing is broken today and the default emits nothing at all. Related: T189. |
| T209 | The Demographics and enrollment writers share file 2's zero-row defect — an empty roster produces an empty file with no guard | HIGH | ✅ **Shipped 2026-09-08 (S35-A, `85cfaaa`)** — Demographics **runtime-verified**; enrollment **code-verified only, never executed** | **Filed S31-J (2026-09-08), code-verified.** S31-J added a zero-row guard to file 2 after a 0-byte file downloaded on proposal 140956. ⚠️ **The same hole exists in two other writers and was deliberately left open**: `writeDemographics` and `writeHraEnrollment` both iterate the `employer_participant` roster and both document "an empty roster emits a zero-row file rather than refusing" as intended behaviour. ⚠️ **It is not intended behaviour, it is the same defect.** A zero-row Demographics file creates no participants, and a zero-row enrollment file enrols nobody — both upload cleanly, both do nothing, and the operator has no signal at all. The likely trigger is ordinary: generating file 4 before the census is uploaded. ⚠️ **Fixing it means editing two import-proven writers**, which is why S31-J did not: its fence covered file 2 only, and changing a proven writer to fix an unproven-but-suspected problem is the trade S30-A already declined once. Do it as its own run, with the same shape as file 2's guard — refuse, name the reason, no file. ⭐ **SHIPPED 2026-09-08 (S35-A, `85cfaaa`).** The guard is a **mirror of file 2's, not a redesign**: same `lines.isEmpty()` test in the same position (last statement before `resolveFilename`), the same `writePlainError` at 400, the same message shape, no logging of its own. It deliberately omits file 2's `dropReasons` enumeration — that serves per-candidate drop logic **neither writer has**, where an empty roster is the single reason and needs no list. Both javadoc sentences asserting *"an empty roster emits a zero-row file rather than refusing"* were corrected with it: they documented the defect as intended behaviour. ✅ **Demographics is RUNTIME-VERIFIED** — prospect **136810**, empty roster, via the Setup screen export link: the plain-text refusal rendered, named 136810 correctly, and **no file downloaded**. ⚠️ **The enrollment guard is code-verified and compile-verified only — never executed.** On the same prospect the `hra_annual_ee` refusal **fires ahead of the roster check** and returned first. Reaching it needs an application carrying a valid `hra_annual_ee`, converted to Setup, with no census — and that amount **cannot be corrected once the application locks on conversion** (T200), so the setup cost was judged not worth chasing. **What the Demographics pass verified is the shared mechanism** — `lines.isEmpty()` reached inside a writer, `writePlainError` at 400, no file written; **what remains unverified is which method the guard sits in.** ⚠️ **Refusal ordering, as a finding:** on a real empty-roster Setup whose amount is also unanswered, the operator sees the **amount** error, not the roster message. Both are refusals and neither is a zero-byte file, so the defect this item names is **closed either way** — but a reader expecting the roster message should know why it did not appear. Related: T196, T206. |
| T210 | Does Summit attach a new plan year to an existing plan on re-import, or replace the existing one? The corrected upsert key depends on the first | HIGH | 📋 Planned — **one import settles it**; nothing else can | **Filed S31-J (2026-09-08).** S31-J removed the plan year from `Import Plan ID` on the stated basis that **a Summit benefit plan persists across plan years and accumulates them** — a renewal imports into the same plan and attaches another plan year, with elections separating by plan year. ⚠️ **That account is Kevin's and has never been tested.** The whole justification for the key change rests on it, and the two possible behaviours have opposite consequences: if a re-import **attaches** a plan year, the corrected key is right and the old year-suffixed form would have created a duplicate plan every renewal; if it **replaces**, the corrected key silently destroys the prior year's plan year and its elections, and the year-suffixed form was right after all. ⚠️ **The failure direction if the premise is wrong is the destructive one**, which is exactly the trade T185 originally reasoned about — correctly in structure, on a wrong fact. **Test before any renewal season**: import a plan, then re-import the same `Import Plan ID` with a different `Plan Year Begin`/`End` and read whether the plan then holds one plan year or two. Related: T185 (retired), T205. |
| T211 | `Plan Name` uniqueness within one file 2 is now the operator's responsibility, and nothing enforces it | LOW | 📋 Planned — a guard rail, not a defect | **Filed S31-J (2026-09-08), code-verified.** S31-J made `Plan Name` and `Plan Description` the mapping row's `label` alone, which is editable per mapping on the T202 admin screen — a deliberate win: renaming a plan needs no code and no config. ⚠️ **But file 2's results template correlates on `Plan Name` and carries no row number**, so two rows in one file sharing a label make the results file ambiguous about which submitted row each line refers to. ⚠️ **Nothing prevents it**: the mapping table's unique constraint is on (PSP, service item), not on the label, so two service items can carry identical labels and the export will emit them both. Fix shape: warn (not refuse) at export time when two emitted rows share a label, or validate on the admin screen at save. Low priority — it degrades a diagnostic, not the import itself. Related: T202. |
| T212 | No screen lists the retained Summit export files, so the V096 record is invisible | HIGH | 🔨 Built S32-G (2026-09-08) — **compile-verified only, never loaded** | **Filed S32-A (2026-09-08).** S32-A created `summit_file_export` (V096) and wired `SummitExportServlet` to write one row per generated file, carrying the exact bytes sent, a row/byte count, the acting user and an indexed `content_sha256`. **Nothing reads any of it.** `SummitFileExportDAO` ships with the three reads the screen needs — `findByPspId`, `findByProposalId`, `findByContentHash` — and none has a caller. Shape: a PSP-admin listing (newest first, filterable by file type, reachable from the Setup screen beside the four generate links), each row re-downloadable from the stored `content` so the bytes that were actually sent can be handed to Summit support or diffed against a results file. ⚠️ **It must also carry the T194 same-hash warning**: before generating, call `findByContentHash(sha, fileType)` and, on a hit, say plainly that Summit dedupes a re-sent file on content and this exact file has already been generated — otherwise a re-send is a silent no-op with no error and no results row. ⚠️ **`content` holds participant names, addresses and emails** (Demographics rows), so the screen is PSP-admin + `IchraAccessResolver`, the same gate the export itself carries, and re-download must not be reachable by GUID or any public path. ⭐ **BUILT S32-G (2026-09-08), NOT VERIFIED.** `SummitFileExportAdmin` (`/SummitFileExportAdmin`, PSP-admin via the `isPspAdmin` gate copied from `SummitPlanTemplateAdmin`) plus `summitFileExportAdmin25.jsp`, linked from the navbar Admin dropdown's Data Import section beside Summit Plan Templates. Lists this PSP's exports newest first via the already-present `SummitFileExportDAO.findByPspId`; **the list never selects `content`** (PII, and the full file bytes on every row). Rows sharing a `content_sha256` carry a "Same content" badge with the T194 explanation on the page. Download is `?action=download&id=NN`, serving **stored bytes verbatim — never a regeneration** — under the original filename, with the content type, encoding and `Content-Disposition` shape read from `SummitExportServlet.writeFile` (that file was not edited). **A download writes nothing**; the screen has no `doPost` and opens no transaction. One DAO method was added, `findById`, because no single-row read existed; it is deliberately unscoped, so the servlet re-checks the row's `pspId` against the session and returns the same 404 for a foreign row as for a missing one. ⚠️ **Never loaded.** There is no JSP precompiler (T199), so the build cannot verify the JSP at all — a syntax error there surfaces at first page load. Neither the listing, the badge, nor a re-download has ever executed. |
| T213 | Transport for the four Summit files is undecided, and the questions that would settle it have not been asked | HIGH | 📋 Planned — a decision, then an ask; no code until both | **Filed S32-A (2026-09-08).** AMS generates all four proven files and streams them to a browser. How they reach DataPath is unmade. **The client posture is recommended:** the PSP's own workstation pushes the file to DataPath's site, and the response file arrives back over the same connection. That keeps **no inbound service on the production host** — no listener, no credential at rest on a server that also serves `superiorstate.biz`, and no new attack surface on a host carrying PII — and it makes the return leg free rather than a second integration. The alternative, AMS hosting a drop that DataPath collects from, inverts all three. ⚠️ **S32-A deliberately built no transport** — no FTP, SFTP, FTPS, HTTP client, scheduled job or outbound connection of any kind, and no credential handling or config key that could hold one. The V096 record is needed identically under either choice, which is why it was safe to build first. **Unanswered, and not yet asked of DataPath:** **SDX-01 — FTPS versus SFTP on 443**, which protocol their MOVEit endpoint actually speaks on that port; whether the per-employer or per-PSP **folder is created by them or by us**, and by what request; and the **key exchange** — what credential type, issued to whom, rotated how. None of these has been put to them. The decision cannot close before they are. |
| T214 | Summit response files are not ingested, and the two file types match on different keys | MED | 📋 Planned — dependent on T212's record | **Filed S32-A (2026-09-08).** Summit returns a results file per import and AMS reads none of them, so an import's outcome is only visible by someone opening the response by hand. The V096 record is the prerequisite — matching a result back to what was sent needs the bytes that were sent, and a regeneration is not a substitute, since mapping rows (V095), `SUMMIT_BRANCH_CODE`, `SUMMIT_TPA_ID_PREFIX` and the participant roster can all change underneath an export. ⚠️ **The two file types match on different keys, and one of them is weak.** **Demographics results carry a row number**, so a result correlates positionally against the stored `content`. **File 2's results template carries no row number at all** — correlation falls back to **`Plan Name`**, which since S31-J is the mapping row's `label` alone, is not enforced unique within a file (T211), and is operator-editable on the Summit Plan Templates admin screen. So a duplicated or renamed label makes a file-2 result ambiguous or unmatchable, which ties this item to T211 rather than leaving it a formatting detail. Sequence after T212. |
| T215 | S31-J's zero-row guard covers `cdhplan` only — `demographics` and `enrollment` still emit a silent empty file | MED | 📋 Planned — the same defect S31-J closed, in two more writers | **Found S32-A (2026-09-08), code-verified, not fixed.** S31-J (`1139fc6`) made `writeEmployerCdhPlan` refuse a zero-row file rather than write an empty one, because **an empty file imports as nothing at all** — Summit accepts it, does nothing, and returns no error, so the send reads as successful. That guard was written because it had already happened once. ⚠️ **It covers `cdhplan` and nothing else.** `writeDemographics` (`SummitExportServlet:845`) and `writeHraEnrollment` (`:1052`) both iterate `EmployerParticipantDAO.findByProspectId` with **no empty-roster guard**, so a prospect whose census has never been uploaded — or was uploaded empty — produces a zero-row file 3 or file 4 that downloads cleanly, imports as nothing, and looks like a completed step. The failure is worse here than for file 2: files 3 and 4 are the participant-carrying files, so a silent empty send is precisely a roster that never reached Summit. Fix shape: the S31-J refusal, applied to both writers, naming the prospect and pointing at the Census Upload button on the Setup screen — `writeDemographics`'s own Javadoc currently states the opposite ("An empty roster emits a zero-row file rather than refusing"), so that line changes with the guard. Note that S32-A's V096 recording deliberately **records** the zero-row case rather than suppressing it (`row_count` = 0 is a legitimate stored value), which is what makes an already-sent empty file discoverable after the fact once T212's listing screen exists. |
| T216 | The JDBC URL asserts `serverTimezone=UTC` against a MySQL server running `SYSTEM` (UTC−5), so **every** datetime AMS writes is stored five hours ahead of local | LOW | ✅ Resolved 2026-09-08 — round trip is symmetric, no AMS screen affected | ⭐ **RESOLVED 2026-09-08 by the page view this row was filed to prompt.** `/RateCacheAdmin` displayed **`2026-09-08 16:04`** against a stored `fetched_at` of `2026-09-08 21:04:32`. **The EclipseLink round trip is symmetric** — the driver converts local→believed-UTC on write and applies the inverse on read, so JPA hands back local time and **every AMS screen has always displayed correct local times.** No fix is needed for any screen, and **screens must not add timezone arithmetic** — doing so would introduce the five-hour skew this row was filed about. `SummitFileExportAdmin` (T212) accordingly displays `generatedAt` as-is. ⚠️ **What remains true, and is why this row is kept rather than deleted:** the stored values genuinely are five hours ahead of local, so **every out-of-band reader — raw SQL, MySQL Workbench, any report or tool on a different connection string — sees times five hours ahead.** Anyone reading `summit_file_export`, `billing_run`, `rating_area_rate_cache` or any other datetime column outside the application must subtract five hours (or connect with the same `serverTimezone=UTC` assertion). Dropped to LOW: there is no application defect to fix, only a reading hazard to know about. **Original finding, retained for provenance:** the JDBC URL asserts `serverTimezone=UTC` against a MySQL server running `SYSTEM` (UTC−5) — `NOW()` returned `16:10:15` against `UTC_TIMESTAMP()` `21:10:15` — evidenced by `summit_file_export.generated_at` holding `2026-09-08 21:00:41` for a request the access log served at `[08/Sep/2026:16:00:40 -0500]` (S32-E). ⚠️ **If a fix is ever wanted anyway, reversal cost is high and rising:** removing `serverTimezone=UTC` corrects new writes and leaves every existing row shifted, so it needs a data migration across an unknown number of columns and tables, growing with every row written. |
| T217 | The retained-export listing loads every file's full bytes to render a page that never shows them | MED | 📋 Planned — harmless at today's row count, a memory problem at a few hundred | **Found S32-G (2026-09-08), code-verified on the built screen.** `SummitFileExportAdmin` lists through `SummitFileExportDAO.findByPspId`, whose JPQL is `SELECT e FROM SummitFileExport e ...` — it returns the **entity**, so EclipseLink materializes every mapped field including `content`, which is the complete bytes of every retained export file. The page renders **no** cell from `content` (deliberately — it is PII, and the list is metadata only), so the whole payload is loaded and discarded on every page view. ⚠️ **Today this is invisible**: three rows totalling under 2 KB. It becomes a real problem at a few hundred retained exports, and Demographics files grow with the participant roster, so the per-row cost is not fixed. The same load also lands in the EclipseLink L2 cache, so the cost is not paid once. **Fix shape — a decision, not an instruction:** either a projection query selecting the metadata columns into a DTO (leaving `findById` to fetch `content` for the download, which is the only place it is needed), or a lazy `@Basic(fetch = FetchType.LAZY)` on `SummitFileExport.content` — noting that lazy basic mapping requires weaving and is easy to get silently wrong, and that `SummitFileExport.java` is where the second option lands. Sequence after the screen has been used enough to know the real row growth rate. |
| T218 | `generated_at` renders raw on the retained-export listing, with a `T` separator | LOW | 📋 Planned — cosmetic, PSP-admin-only | **Found S32-G (2026-09-08) on the first page load.** `summitFileExportAdmin25.jsp` renders `${e.generatedAt}` directly, so JSTL calls `LocalDateTime.toString()` and the GENERATED column reads `2026-09-08T21:00:41` — ISO-8601 with the `T` separator — where the nearest sibling screen, `/RateCacheAdmin`, formats the same Java type as `yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm` through a `getXxxDisplay()` String getter (`RateCacheDAO.java:208,213`). The codebase convention is that convention, precisely because `fmt:formatDate` does not accept `java.time`. ⚠️ **Any fix must add formatting only and no timezone arithmetic** — T216 established the JPA round trip is symmetric, so the value is already correct local time and converting it would reintroduce the five-hour skew T216 was filed about. Cosmetic severity: the screen is PSP-admin-only, the value is legible as rendered, and nothing reads it programmatically. |
| T219 | Per-employee first-month funding source and front-loadable deduction schedule | MED | 📋 Planned | Card funding must record, per employee, which month-one on-ramp applies (bonus or stipend, PTO sale, double first deduction, employee direct pay, or delayed effective date) and must support per-pay-period deduction amounts that vary rather than levelling across the plan year. The sub-accounts, ledgering and monthly cadence are unchanged across all five on-ramps — this is a funding-source field plus a schedule that can be front-loaded, not a new mechanism. See LA-36. |
| T220 | Remove the now-inert `/CreateBpoTestUser` entry from `LoginFilter.ALLOWED_ENDPOINTS` | LOW | ✅ Done 2026-09-09 — literal removed, `BUILD SUCCESS`, no behaviour change | ⭐ **Closed 2026-09-09.** The dangling allowlist literal `"/CreateBpoTestUser"` was deleted from `ALLOWED_ENDPOINTS` after T29 removed the servlet whose `@WebServlet` annotation declared the mapping; the set went from **18 entries to 17**, every other entry kept its name and position, and no logic in `LoginFilter` was altered. **No behaviour change** — the path already 404'd on production. ⚠️ **Code-verified only:** the file compiles and packages (`BUILD SUCCESS`, run without `clean`), but nothing in that run exercised a login, a filtered request, or a redirect, and `LoginFilter` sits on every request. **Not on production — rides the next release**; verify on deploy that an unauthenticated request to a genuinely protected path still redirects to `/login`. Original note follows. — **Cleanup, not a security defect.** `src/main/java/net/superiorstate/ams/LoginFilter.java:22` still lists the string `"/CreateBpoTestUser"` in `ALLOWED_ENDPOINTS`. T29 (S36) deleted the servlet class whose `@WebServlet` annotation declared that mapping, so the path now resolves to no servlet and 404s — the allowlist entry grants unauthenticated passage to nothing and is inert. Filed rather than fixed in the same run because **`LoginFilter` is a shared filter mapped to `/*`**: session 36's scope fence explicitly forbade altering it, and any edit to it sits on the path of every request in the application. It therefore ships alone, under its own fence, rather than riding along in a security diff. The fix is the deletion of one string literal from the set — no behaviour change is intended or expected, which is precisely why it wants its own compile and its own review rather than being folded into unrelated work. |
| T221 | SECURITY: check for and disable the BPO test accounts `/CreateBpoTestUser` may already have created | HIGH | ✅ Done 2026-09-09 (S37) — production queried, **zero rows**, nothing to revoke | ⭐ **CLOSED 2026-09-09 (session 37) by direct query.** Production's `user` table was queried for both accounts by user name and by email (`SELECT … WHERE user_name IN (…) OR email IN (…)`) and returned **zero rows** — the endpoint never ran on production, so nothing was ever created there and nothing needs disabling or deleting. This is a genuine runtime observation, not an inference, and it is consistent with the same session's finding that `/CreateBpoTestUser` returns **404** on `superiorstate.biz` and that `CreateBpoTestUser.class` is absent from the production filesystem. **Local caveat, one line:** local `beta_ssa` refreshes weekly **from production**, so any copy that ever existed locally is transient and reimports clean from a source now confirmed empty — no further action, on any environment. The credential value is deliberately not recorded here or anywhere. **Original note retained below.** ⚠️ **Removing the creator does not revoke what it created.** T29 (S36) deleted the endpoint, which stops *new* accounts being minted, but any account it already minted remains a live account carrying a known, hard-coded credential. Two to check, per environment: **`bpoadmin@test.com` (UserRole 102, BPO Admin)** and **`bpouser@test.com` (UserRole 103, BPO User)**. Both were created with `emailVerified=true`, `allowSetPassword=false` and `guidUsed=true`, so neither sits in a pending or otherwise unusable state — they are ready-to-use logins, and **BPO Admin is a privileged role**. Requires a per-environment query of the `user` table — Production, local `beta_ssa`, and any refreshed copy, noting that the weekly refresh from production would re-import them if they exist there — followed by disablement or deletion of every hit. ⚠️ **Nothing in session 36 could answer whether these rows exist anywhere:** no environment was queried, and the endpoint's own idempotency guard means a single historical hit on any host would have created them permanently and silently. Needs production DB access, hence Kevin's rather than an agent's. **Do not record the credential value in this row, in any doc, or in any commit message.** |

Re-enumerated `SOURCE_ENV_PRODUCTION`/`SOURCE_ENV_STAGING`/`getSourceEnv()` across `src/main/java` afterward: the only remaining raw comparisons are `RateCacheWarmService`'s write-path stamping (out of scope by design, unchanged) and `GroupConversionServlet`'s two references (a deliberately distinct advisory-banner question per T137/T138, not a candidate). **Every source-env authoritativeness gate in the codebase now routes through `RateSourceEnvResolver` — the set S21-L opened is closed.** Compiled (`BUILD SUCCESS`), not runtime-verified. **A fourth scope-fence correction this session** — S21-L's own fence named `ViewProposal.putIchraMarketTokens` specifically rather than the file, missing this sibling check; the pattern (fences written from methods already in mind rather than from what the change actually spans) is named explicitly in S21-M's own prompt rather than quietly re-fixed. Logged 2026-08-06 (S21-L / S21-M). |

HealthSherpa's off-exchange **ICHRA Partner API** is the ICHRA connectivity layer being integrated (supplier, not competitor) — free, off-exchange, **not** EDE, **not** HSOne. Webhooks (Submission Confirmation, Policy Status) are confirmed. Would let AMS be a quote-to-admin ICHRA platform. No AMS integration commitment until the eval clears; then a Phase-A investigation. See `docs/business/healthsherpa.md`. Logged 2026-07-28. **Update 2026-07-28 (a) — quoting DONE:** HealthSherpa One (`api.one.healthsherpa.com`, `POST /v1/quotes`) matched the zizzl CSA baseline (BCBS Blue Advantage Silver HMO 306, off-exchange) at $582.78 exactly; on-exchange and off-exchange quoting are both open on the free self-serve tier. **Update 2026-07-28 (b) — enrollability DONE, product model corrected:** there is ONE API surface — quoting, on-exchange enrollment deeplinks, and off-exchange direct enrollment all live on `api.one.healthsherpa.com` with one key; the gate is per-workflow account approval, NOT a separate gated product (this retracts the earlier "docs.ichra.* is a different product" framing). `api_enrollable` is **exchange-scoped**: it means "enrollable via `POST /v1/enrollments`", which accepts only product `ichra` and exchange `off_exchange`, so all 45 on-exchange Hopkins plans return `false` by definition — this explains and retracts the earlier "sample Christus plan was false" note. Off-exchange Hopkins TX: BCBS 24 plans and CHRISTUS 18 plans are all **enrollable** (including the Sandoval baseline, HIOS `33602TX0460776` @ $582.78), UnitedHealthcare 23 plans are **not** — 42 of 65 enrollable, split cleanly by carrier. **No Ambetter in Hopkins on either exchange**; the statewide issuer list is not the same as county-level availability, and the earlier note conflated them. **#38 unblock is now specific:** off-exchange `GET /v1/enrollments?product=ichra&exchange=off_exchange&employer_external_id=…&updated_since=…` is employer-scoped and stable-looking; the on-exchange `/v1/policy-status/*` endpoints are agent-scoped and **alpha**, so a population split across both exchanges will NOT have uniform status coverage — do not oversell internally. **Remaining step is a portal access request, not a rep call:** register, sign in to the developer portal, request off-exchange enrollment workflow approval, and carry the two genuinely open questions into their follow-up — (1) **AOR / TPA account model**: `_agent_id`, `tpa_slug`, `actor.agent_id`, and `agent_of_record` are all rejected if caller-supplied, and onboarding says one unique key plus one linked HealthSherpa agent account, so per-agent attribution for SWBD's downline may not exist on the off-exchange path even though on-exchange `agent_assisted` uses a per-agent OAuth link — this maps onto nothing in the GA/sub-agency model (V070/V071); (2) **BAA**: the enrollment payload carries SSN/ITIN, immigration status, incarceration status, and disability data, so a BAA is mandatory, not prudent — counterparty is Geozoning, Inc. DBA HealthSherpa. |
